# Spec: Filtering subsystem (`FilterSet`, `RelatedFilter`, `Meta.filterset_class`)

Target release: `0.0.8` (card [`DONE-027-0.0.8`][kanban]).
Status: shipped (`0.0.8`). The [`django_strawberry_framework/filters/`][filters] subpackage ships its five modules; `Meta.filterset_class` is in `ALLOWED_META_KEYS` and bound at finalizer phase 2.5; the fakeshop library app carries [`filters.py`][fakeshop-library] / [`filters_genre.py`][fakeshop-library] wired through `Meta.filterset_class` on its `DjangoType` classes, with `FilterInput[...]`-annotated root resolvers; the live HTTP filter tests are in [`examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_library_api.py`][fakeshop-test-library]. The cross-family composition test lives at `tests/orders/test_composition.py` ([Slice 6](#slice-checklist)).
Owner: package maintainer.
Predecessors: [`docs/SPECS/spec-015-relay_interfaces-0_0_5.md`][spec-015] (Relay-Node wiring at finalizer phase 2.5 — the synchronization point this subsystem reuses); [`docs/SPECS/spec-018-meta_primary-0_0_6.md`][spec-018] (the `Meta.primary` design and the [`TypeRegistry`][registry-typeregistry] keying convention [Decision 9](#decision-9--input-class-namespace-vs-typeregistry-and-lifecycle) respects); [`docs/SPECS/spec-020-list_field-0_0_7.md`][spec-020]; [`docs/GLOSSARY.md`][glossary] entries [`FilterSet`][glossary-filterset], [`RelatedFilter`][glossary-relatedfilter], [`Meta.filterset_class`][glossary-metafilterset_class].

Each Decision's justification and rejected alternatives live in [`spec-027-filters-0_0_8-rationale.md`][spec-027-rationale].

## Key glossary references

Skim these [`docs/GLOSSARY.md`][glossary] entries first — they anchor the vocabulary used throughout the spec:

- [`FilterSet`][glossary-filterset] — the declarative filter class. Subclasses `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet` per [Decision 5](#decision-5--django-filter-as-the-foundation) and ports Layers 1–5 of `django-graphene-filters`' lazy-resolution pipeline per [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline); Graphene's `graphene.InputObjectType` becomes `strawberry.input` and Graphene's `lambda:` forward references become `Annotated["TypeName", strawberry.lazy("django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs")]` over module globals.
- [`RelatedFilter`][glossary-relatedfilter] — cross-relation filter traversal. Accepts a target `FilterSet` class, an absolute import path string, or an unqualified name for circular references; lazy-resolved at finalizer time.
- [`Meta.filterset_class`][glossary-metafilterset_class] — the consumer-facing key that points a [`DjangoType`][glossary-djangotype] at its `FilterSet`. In `ALLOWED_META_KEYS` per [Decision 7](#decision-7--metafilterset_class-promotion-gate).
- [`DjangoType`][glossary-djangotype] — the model-backed Strawberry type the filter sidecar attaches to. The `Meta`-driven shape is what makes `Meta.filterset_class = ItemFilter` legible to a Django audience.
- [`finalize_django_types`][glossary-finalize_django_types] — the synchronization point where the filter subsystem's lazy-resolution pipeline runs (phase 2.5, the same seam [`Meta.interfaces = (relay.Node,)`][glossary-metainterfaces] uses).
- [`DjangoOptimizerExtension`][glossary-djangooptimizerextension] — filter clauses applied to a queryset do not break the optimizer's [Queryset diffing][glossary-queryset-diffing] cooperation; covered by [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) and a live HTTP test.
- [`get_queryset` visibility hook][glossary-get_queryset-visibility-hook] — pre-filter visibility scoping. Composes with filter `check_*_permission` gates ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation)).
- [`OrderSet`][glossary-orderset] — the sibling ordering family; it reuses the same lazy-resolution architecture and the shared set-family substrate.
- [`AggregateSet`][glossary-aggregateset] / [`get_child_queryset`][glossary-get_child_queryset] — planned Layer-3 sidecars that will reuse the same architecture; [`apply_cascade_permissions`][glossary-apply_cascade_permissions] — the shipped cascade helper a `get_queryset` hook can call before any filter clause runs.
- [`DjangoConnectionField`][glossary-djangoconnectionfield] — reads the wrapped type's `Meta.filterset_class`, builds its `filter:` argument through `filter_input_type(...)`, and calls `apply_sync` / `apply_async` itself.
- [`Meta.primary`][glossary-metaprimary] — the multi-`DjangoType`-per-model design whose `TypeRegistry` keying convention this subsystem respects per [Decision 9](#decision-9--input-class-namespace-vs-typeregistry-and-lifecycle).
- [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] — raised at type-creation and finalization time for a non-`FilterSet` `Meta.filterset_class`, unresolvable `RelatedFilter` targets, unregistered related targets, multi-owner conflicts, orphan helper references, and unsupported relation lookups — see [Error shapes](#error-shapes) and [Edge cases](#edge-cases-and-constraints).
- [`Meta.search_fields`][glossary-metasearch_fields] — the planned search-input surface; the `LOOKUP_PREFIXES` map (`^` / `=` / `@` / `$`) ported verbatim from `django-graphene-filters` lives in the filter subsystem because `construct_search` belongs in the filter pipeline.

Foundational invariants and cross-cutting surfaces — all defined in [`docs/GLOSSARY.md`][glossary]:

- [Relation handling][glossary-relation-handling] — the FK / OneToOne / M2M / reverse-relation traversal `RelatedFilter` plugs into.
- [Relay Node integration][glossary-relay-node-integration] — the `Meta.interfaces = (relay.Node,)` surface that Decision 4's Relay-vs-scalar conditional consults; relation targets implementing `relay.Node` route through `GlobalIDFilter`.
- [Definition-order independence][glossary-definition-order-independence] — the invariant the lazy-resolution pipeline preserves so cross-module `RelatedFilter("...")` references work regardless of import order.
- [Cross-subsystem invariants][glossary-cross-subsystem-invariants] — the deferred-Meta-key promotion rule; Decision 7's promotion gate is one instance of it.
- [Choice enum generation][glossary-choice-enum-generation] — the pipeline `convert_filter_to_input_annotation` consults for choice-backed filter inputs, so choice-enum filter inputs round-trip through Strawberry enums.
- [Scalar field conversion][glossary-scalar-field-conversion] — the `SCALAR_MAP` the converter reads to type scalar and raw-primary-key filter inputs from the model column.
- [Specialized scalar conversions][glossary-specialized-scalar-conversions] — Decimal / Date / DateTime / Time / UUID conversions the converter handles per Decision 4.
- [BigInt scalar][glossary-bigint-scalar] — one of the scalar key shapes a 64-bit column produces.
- [Input type generation][glossary-input-type-generation] — the package's general input-type-generation surface; `FilterArgumentsFactory` is the filtering-specific instance built on the shared generated-input substrate.
- [OptimizerHint][glossary-optimizerhint] — the hint primitive `Meta.optimizer_hints` declarations carry; filter clauses compose with hints at queryset-translation time.
- [only() projection][glossary-only-projection] — the optimizer's projection contract; queryset filters compose with `.only(...)` projections.

Project conventions to follow:

- [`AGENTS.md`][agents] — the test-placement rule at [`AGENTS.md`][agents] #"Test placement:" (package tests under `tests/filters/`; live HTTP tests under `examples/fakeshop/test_query/`); the live-HTTP-priority rule at [`AGENTS.md`][agents] #"any line reachable via a real GraphQL query against fakeshop"; the settings-keys rule at [`AGENTS.md`][agents] #"Add a settings key only when the feature that needs it lands".
- [`CONTRIBUTING.md`][contributing] — 100% coverage target; coverage is earned through fakeshop live-HTTP flows where practical per [Decision 12](#decision-12--live-http-coverage-strategy).
- [`docs/TREE.md`][tree] — tests mirror source one-to-one. The subsystem lives at [`django_strawberry_framework/filters/`][filters] per [Decision 2](#decision-2--subpackage-layout-and-public-export-surface); the mirror partner is `tests/filters/`.
- [`START.md`][start] — markdown link convention (reference-style for cross-file links, all defs at the bottom under the 10 canonical group headers).

## Slice checklist

Slice 4a distinguishes two annotation dependencies after the active-input gates. A genuinely missing automatic package list or range inner parser needs the final declared or scoped undeclared source before the consumer form cleans. A complete native or consumer-owned field cleans first; its active final value is bind-asked after the entire actual form clean. Root and logical-arm forms in one apply input finish native cleaning before any new bind-only source is acquired; native staging stops at its first form rejected by the consumer's public `is_valid()` policy, so an invalid root constructs no logical siblings. Direct raw forms retain suppressed native staging of safe logical siblings for their partial `.qs` application. Ordinary resolved flat hops stay in step 8. The request-wide hop map is reused by validation and application. An actual `Transform` node or starting expression, rather than a difference between field objects, marks an integer transform: a stored `GeneratedField` and its native output field are distinct objects. A supplied scoped annotation leaf asks its actual visible child Query for integer-transform eligibility and uses that same Query when its package list or range constructs the predicate; no synthetic parent queryset is assumed.

Six slices; boxes stay unticked by convention, the Status line is the truth.

- [ ] Slice 1: Foundation — module layout + `Filter` primitives + `FilterSet` metaclass
  - [ ] [`django_strawberry_framework/filters/`][filters] is a subpackage with `__init__.py`, `base.py`, `sets.py`, `factories.py`, `inputs.py` per [Decision 2](#decision-2--subpackage-layout-and-public-export-surface). Module docstrings pin each file's responsibility (`base.py` = filter primitives + `RelatedFilter`; `sets.py` = `FilterSet` + metaclass + apply pipeline; `factories.py` = `FilterArgumentsFactory`; `inputs.py` = the input-class namespace, input-data adapters and lifecycle ledger).
  - [ ] `base.py` ships the upstream primitives per [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor): `TypedFilter`, `ArrayFilter` + `ArrayFilterMethod`, `RangeFilter` + `RangeField` + `validate_range`, `ListFilter` + `ListFilterMethod`, `GlobalIDFilter` + `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter`, plus the raw-primary-key relation pair `RelationPkFilter` / `RelationPkMultipleFilter` and `RelatedFilter`. `Filter` is `django_filters.Filter` itself, re-exported from `filters/__init__.py` (not a port). Each ported primitive mirrors the matching `graphene_django/filter/filters/*.py` primitive (under `~/projects/django-graphene-filters/.venv/lib/python*/site-packages/graphene_django/filter/filters/`), Strawberry-adapted only at the input-type-construction boundary. Each value of a declared `RangeFilter` / `ListFilter` / `ArrayFilter` is cleaned by the comparison form field of the column it compares (an `ArrayField`'s innermost element column at any nesting depth; each member of an `in` over one a whole array value) under any django-filter `FilterSet` parent, a relation's by the target column it compares on (its `to_field`, else the primary key), as `<relation>__<that column>` cleans it, one whose `lookup_expr` crosses a relation by the column the crossing reaches (`filters/base.py::_filterset_walked_lookup`), a `method=` filter's on a relation by none (`filters/base.py::_column_form_field`), and one over a path the model names no field for (a queryset annotation, a `FilteredRelation` alias and the columns past it) by the field the queryset handed to the set (`filter.parent.queryset`, read uncached per request by `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_bound_lookup_operand`, under any django-filter `FilterSet` parent) compares there, a `method=` filter's by none. Two bounds of the package's `RangeFilter` compare as a range whatever lookup it declares, through its declared transforms (`filters/base.py::RangeFilter.filter`).
  - [ ] `RelatedFilter` is the `BaseRelatedFilter` port from `django_graphene_filters/filters.py::BaseRelatedFilter` (Layer 1 of [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline)). Its owner-bind / lazy-target machinery is [`django_strawberry_framework/sets_mixins.py::RelatedSetTargetMixin`][sets-mixins], a subclass of the Layer-2 [`sets_mixins.py::LazyRelatedClassMixin`][sets-mixins] every set family shares; `filters/base.py` re-exports `LazyRelatedClassMixin` so the `filters` public surface carries the name.
  - [ ] `sets.py` ships `FilterSetMetaclass` (port from `django_graphene_filters/filterset.py::FilterSetMetaclass`) and `FilterSet` (port from `django_graphene_filters/filterset.py::AdvancedFilterSet`, with `get_filters` doing Layer-4 cycle-safe expansion, a `RelatedFilter(flat=False)` declaration copying nothing) — Layers 3 + 4 of [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline). `FilterSet` accepts [`Meta.model`][glossary-metamodel], [`Meta.fields`][glossary-metafields] (dict form `{"name": ["exact", "icontains"]}`, a per-field `"__all__"` value (refused at build where a served set's input cannot type one of its lookups, as over an `ArrayField` column, so the consumer lists the lookups it wants there), or the top-level `"__all__"` shorthand with the override pinned in [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline)), `Meta.exclude` (alone, the same whole-set sweep as `"__all__"` with that exclude; each name a field of `Meta.model` or a `Meta.fields` entry and never a declared filter's name, else `ConfigurationError`), and per-field `check_<field>_permission` hooks. It carries the `_owner_definition: DjangoTypeDefinition | None` slot bound at finalizer phase 2.5 and the resolver-facing `apply_sync` / `apply_async` classmethod pair per [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation), which carry `info` end-to-end, derive each declared hop's target visibility once per request for every nested branch at every level, logical arm and flat leaf walking it (`FilterSet._declared_hop_rows` / `FilterSet._await_declared_hop_rows`), and call `filterset.form.is_valid()` explicitly (raising `GraphQLError("Invalid filter input")` with `extensions.code == "FILTER_INVALID"` on failure, and before it on a value holding text the package refuses, a lone surrogate or an embedded NUL, or one a widget refuses as the form reads it, and after the form cleans on a temporal value the queryset's database cannot bind; both refusals run in the form's own clean, so raw form data answers them as form errors).
  - [ ] A projected child keeps its constructor queryset restriction on the actual leaf and correlated hop through `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._leaf_chain` and `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._apply_relation_leaf`; the request-wide target hook rows remain shared. A consumer filter or form whose inner range/list field is already present keeps that field and its validators when `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._refresh_materialized_form_comparisons` runs. A projected `method=` list/range leaf has no annotation comparison dependency and follows ordinary sync flat timing.
  - [ ] A queryset alias whose actual start is a Django `Transform`, or a stored field whose actual lookup walk includes a registered integer `Transform`, uses the shipped filter and native lookup/compiler bind ask; a `Count` or `F` alias keeps its existing integer member behavior, and a package `RangeFilter` is judged by the `gte` / `lte` predicates it actually emits.
  - [ ] `FilterSet._leaf_chain` runs a class-level `filter` override on a relation key (to-many at any hop count, forward at two or more hops) once as declared over the set's own rows, and `FilterSet._apply_relation_leaf` keeps the parents it matches that reach, through each hop's visible rows, a visible last-hop row its value names (`filters/sets.py::_KeyMatch`), while `FilterSet._request_chain` runs a value naming no row of the target on the chain's fallback: on a to-many key the override as declared over the set's own rows, its `field_name` an alias of its relation that `FilterSet._relation_view` joins only to the visible related rows (a many-to-many through its through rows onto visible targets, `_through_link`; a first non-null forward key the database enforces joined round its target, `_round_trip`) and its `lookup_expr` the walked lookup, the request's visibility reaching the override only through that `field_name` (a relation its own Python names reads every related row, its own scoping), and a many-to-many whose through key onto its source Django gives no reverse name of its own (an auto-created self-referential one read forward, symmetrical or not) keeping the leaf re-bound to the target key over the visible targets (`_names_every_through`), on a forward key the leaf comparing the stored key it holds, per [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 3.
  - [ ] `FilterSet._publish_filters` refuses every published leaf running a shipped `filter` whose `field_name` and `lookup_expr` Django resolves on no queryset (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::refuse_unresolvable_lookup`, [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor)), beside `refuse_identityless_relation`'s refusal of a GlobalID or model-choice filter on a `GenericForeignKey`: at build for a served set, at first use for an unbound one (its first construction, `FilterSet.__init__`, or the package's first read of it), and at every construction after until fixed.
  - [ ] `FilterSet._publish_filters` refuses, right after that ask, a published `ListFilter` / `ArrayFilter` running a shipped `filter` whose Django lookup compares one value where the filter applies a list (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::refuse_listless_lookup`, [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor)), at the same times; a `ListFilter` / `ArrayFilter` `range` refuses a non-empty list of other than two bounds as its form's `invalid_values` error (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_ElementField.validate`).
  - [ ] A declared leaf typed from a field no `SCALAR_MAP` entry covers (a relation, a `GenericForeignKey`, a `CompositePrimaryKey`, an unregistered column) is refused when the schema is built naming the filter as declared and the remedy its field takes (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_unscalared_field_refusal`, through `_scalar_from_model_field`, [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor)), never with the `DjangoType` advice `scalar_for_field` gives.
  - [ ] The schema build evaluates no deferred `choices` (a callable, a `lazy()` promise, a `SimpleLazyObject`, a choice iterator, a `QuerySet`, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::defers_choices`) whatever form field the filter's class builds: every form field it reads is built on `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::copied_filter`'s copy, which hands a `SimpleLazyObject` or `QuerySet` to Django's own choice form fields unread ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor), `ChoiceFilter` row), so a table the choices read need not exist when `check` or `migrate` builds the schema.
  - [ ] `inputs.py` IS the input-class namespace per [Decision 9](#decision-9--input-class-namespace-vs-typeregistry-and-lifecycle) — generated filter input classes are materialized as real module globals of `django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs` via `materialize_input_class(name, cls)`, keyed by stable class-derived names (e.g., `"GalaxyFilterInputType"`); the private `_materialized_names: dict[str, type]` ledger maps each name to its materialized input class, which is what makes re-materialization idempotent. This module namespace is NOT the same surface as `django_strawberry_framework.registry.registry` (the model-to-`DjangoType` registry); the two cannot collide because their key types are disjoint (Django model class vs string class name). The shape is module-globals-keyed (not dict-keyed) because Strawberry's `LazyType.resolve_type` reads `module.__dict__[name]`, not a sidecar dict.
- [ ] Slice 2: Factories — `FilterArgumentsFactory` BFS
  - [ ] `factories.py` ships `FilterArgumentsFactory` (the `django_graphene_filters/filter_arguments_factory.py::FilterArgumentsFactory` BFS) — Layer 5 of [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline). The walk lives on the shared [`utils/inputs.py::GeneratedInputArgumentsFactory`][utils-inputs] base; `FilterArgumentsFactory` supplies the filter-family caches and `_build_input_triples` (the input fields plus the `and` / `or` / `not` operator bag). Classes are emitted through `filters/inputs.py::build_input_class`, this module's alias for [`utils/inputs.py::build_strawberry_input_class`][utils-inputs] (the Strawberry-adapted analogue of Graphene's `type(name, (graphene.InputObjectType,), fields)`).
  - [ ] `FILTER_DEFAULTS` is a **class attribute on `FilterSet`** (not in `factories.py`), because `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet.filter_for_field()` / `filter_for_lookup()` consult `cls.FILTER_DEFAULTS` during the runtime filter-instance build. It is the Strawberry-adapted analogue of `graphene_django/filter/filterset.py::GrapheneFilterSetMixin.FILTER_DEFAULTS`. `FilterSet` also overrides `filter_for_field()` / `filter_for_lookup()` to apply the conditional Relay-vs-scalar selection per [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor): Relay-Node targets → `GlobalIDFilter` / `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter`; non-Relay targets → `RelationPkFilter` / `RelationPkMultipleFilter`; a forward relation whose link targets columns other than the target's primary key marks its generated identity filter to compile against `<relation>__pk`. The factory then derives the Strawberry input field type from the **resolved filter instances on the FilterSet**, not from a parallel map — input shape stays downstream of filter shape.
  - [ ] `inputs.py` ships the input-data adapters: `_build_logic_fields` (`and` / `or` / `not` self-references — `list[Annotated["Name", strawberry.lazy(...)]] | None` for `and_` / `or_`, `Annotated["Name", strawberry.lazy(...)] | None` for `not_`), `_build_input_fields` (target-filterset references via `strawberry.lazy(...)` over module globals; per-lookup fields named by the executing schema's `NameConverter` from their python attr, the Python-keyword lookup `in` emitted via `strawberry.field(name="in")`), `construct_search` (the `LOOKUP_PREFIXES` map — `^` → `istartswith`, `=` → `iexact`, `@` → `search`, `$` → `iregex` — ported verbatim), `LOOKUP_NAME_MAP` (the `<lookup> → python_attr` map), `filter_lookup_table` (each operator-bag lookup's form key, decided once per filter set), `convert_filter_to_input_annotation(filter_instance, model_field, owner_definition, filterset_cls)` (the named filter-to-Strawberry-input converter), and `normalize_input_value(filter_instance, raw_value, field_name=None)` (the runtime symmetric of the converter). A declared choice filter over a choice column whose own form field accepts no member of the column's enum is refused at build ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor), `ChoiceFilter` row), and so is a declared or `Meta.filter_overrides` leaf applied to its column whose own form and lookup answer no value of it, a CSV `in` / `range` wrapper asked as its member is and a choice form on a column without `choices` asked its own choices' keys ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor), the catch-all row, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_refuse_form_serving_no_value`).
- [ ] Slice 3: Wiring — `Meta.filterset_class` promotion + finalizer phase 2.5 binding
  - [ ] [`django_strawberry_framework/types/definition.py::DjangoTypeDefinition`][definition] carries a `filterset_class` slot, populated by `DjangoType.__init_subclass__` from `Meta.filterset_class`.
  - [ ] [`django_strawberry_framework/types/definition.py::DjangoTypeDefinition`][definition] also carries `related_target_for(field_name) -> tuple[DjangoTypeDefinition, models.Field] | None`. It looks up `field_name` on `self.model._meta` and, for a relation (`ForeignKey`, `OneToOneField`, `ManyToManyField`, reverse FK / OneToOne / M2M), returns the `(target_definition, model_field)` pair, where `target_definition` is the target model's type resolved through `registry.get(target_model)` (primary-first, then the lone registered type). It returns `None` for non-relation fields, unknown names, a `GenericForeignKey`, and an unregistered target, and memoizes per field name once the registry is finalized. It is the per-segment lookup the GlobalID filter's request-time path walk consults; [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor)'s generation conditional reads the key's own relation instead.
  - [ ] `"filterset_class"` is in [`ALLOWED_META_KEYS`][base], not [`DEFERRED_META_KEYS`][base]. [`django_strawberry_framework/types/base.py::_validate_filterset_class`][base] (called from `_validate_meta`) raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] unless the supplied class is a `FilterSet` subclass.
  - [ ] [`django_strawberry_framework/types/finalizer.py::finalize_django_types`][finalizer] runs the filter-binding pass in phase 2.5 (after `apply_interfaces` / `install_relay_node_resolvers`, before phase 3's `strawberry.type` decoration) through [`types/finalizer.py::_bind_filtersets`][finalizer], which configures the shared driver `_bind_sidecar_sets`. The pass runs as ordered subpasses over ALL wired `DjangoType`s, never as one per-type loop: (1) bind every owner via `_bind_filterset_owner` (a filterset declaring no `Meta.model` refused, first-bind model check, strict multi-owner check per [Decision 6](#decision-6--finalizer-phase-25-binding-seam--materialize-before-schema-ordering)); (2) read `filterset_cls._filter_table()` to trigger Layer-4 expansion and publish each set's filter table; (2.5) run the four filter-only audits in `_audit_filterset_subpass_2_5`, the first deep-copying every filter of every published table a wired filterset serves once through `_audit_filter_copies` and refusing the first a deep copy refuses, the fourth refusing a leaf of a connection-served set whose path resolves to a lookup or transform class that is not Django's own; (3) orphan-validate against `_helper_referenced_filtersets`; (4) read `FilterArgumentsFactory(filterset_cls).arguments` to trigger the Layer-5 BFS and materialize each built input class as a module global of [`django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs`][filters] via `materialize_input_class(name, cls)`. Materialization is idempotent for `(name, input_class)` pairs (an `is` identity check against the class the ledger holds) and raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] when a name is claimed by a different input class (per [Decision 9](#decision-9--input-class-namespace-vs-typeregistry-and-lifecycle)). The filter subsystem registers its own teardown at import time via `registry.py::register_subsystem_clear` (owners `filters.input_namespace` and `filters.helper_references`), and `registry.clear()` replays every registered callback, so `registry.py` imports nothing from `filters`. Calling `finalize_django_types()` twice is a no-op via the `registry.is_finalized()` guard.
- [ ] Slice 4: Live HTTP coverage in fakeshop
  - [ ] [`examples/fakeshop/apps/library/filters.py`][fakeshop-library] declares `BranchFilter`, `ShelfFilter`, `BookFilter`, `LoanFilter`, `PatronFilter` (among the app's other filtersets), mirroring the library relation graph with same-module `RelatedFilter("ShelfFilter")`-style references. The M2M side targets `GenreType` (Relay-Node-shaped via `Meta.interfaces = (relay.Node,)` in [`examples/fakeshop/apps/library/schema.py`][fakeshop-library-schema]); `GenreFilter` lives in the **separate module** [`examples/fakeshop/apps/library/filters_genre.py`][fakeshop-library] so `BookFilter.genres = RelatedFilter("apps.library.filters_genre.GenreFilter")` exercises the Layer-2 absolute-import-path branch (same-module unqualified-name resolution is exercised by every other `RelatedFilter("...")` declaration).
  - [ ] [`examples/fakeshop/apps/library/schema.py`][fakeshop-library-schema] declares `Meta.filterset_class = filters.BranchFilter` (etc.) on the corresponding `DjangoType` classes including `ShelfType` (so `BookFilter.shelf` has a target `ShelfFilter` to resolve against). Root-list resolvers (`all_library_branches`, `all_library_books`, etc.) annotate `filter:` as `FilterInput[filters.BranchFilter] | None` per [Decision 11](#decision-11--filter_input_typefilterset-consumer-helper). Each resolver **calls the owning type's `get_queryset(queryset, info)` BEFORE `FilterSet.apply_sync(...)`** per [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation): `queryset = BranchType.get_queryset(models.Branch.objects.order_by("id"), info)` then `queryset = filters.BranchFilter.apply_sync(filter, queryset, info)` (sync because the fakeshop library types use sync `get_queryset`). Skipping the `get_queryset` call would let the filter clause see through the type's visibility hook to hidden rows.
  - [ ] [`examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_library_api.py`][fakeshop-test-library] carries the live `/graphql/` filter contracts listed in the [Test plan](#test-plan): scalar-field filter (`name: { iContains: "..." }`), choice-enum filter, **non-Relay forward-FK filter** against `ShelfType`'s raw `int` key (`shelf: { id: { exact: 1 } }`), **Relay forward-M2M filter** against `GenreType`'s GlobalID, reverse-FK filter, logical-and / logical-or / logical-not, optimizer cooperation (pinned by `assertNumQueries(N)`), the related-queryset boundary through `Shelf.objects.filter(topic="permanent collection")`, the absolute-import-path `RelatedFilter`, nested `RelatedFilter` visibility scoping, flat relation leaves answering like their nested twins and walked leaves answering over the visible rows (`isNull`, `exclude=True`, relation keys), root `get_queryset` honoring (`BranchType.get_queryset` hiding `city == "restricted"`), wrong-`type_name` Relay GlobalID rejection, form-validation rejection through `PatronFilter.email_must_have_at_sign`, and the GraphQL-coercion-layer error shape distinct from `FILTER_INVALID`.
- [ ] Slice 4a: Tree-form logic substrate — `FilterSet.filter_queryset` `django-filter` instance-method override
  - [ ] [`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet.filter_queryset`][filters] applies the flat leaf clauses through `_apply_flat_leaves` (a framework-owned loop mirroring `BaseFilterSet.filter_queryset`, each leaf's cleaned value first held to its integer column's range, [Edge cases](#edge-cases-and-constraints) "A number past an integer column's range") and composes the `and` / `or` / `not` keys on top through `_evaluate_logic_tree`, per [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 8 and [Definition of done](#definition-of-done) item 4(d). The tree keys are read off `self.data` (the keys `_normalize_input` emits at the top level), not `self.form.cleaned_data`, because the auto-built form declares only the leaf filters. Each branch is a sibling `cls(data=child_data, queryset=queryset)` composed as `Q(pk__in=child_qs.values("pk"))`: `and` intersects, `or` unions, `not` negates. A branch whose filters all return their queryset unchanged (no active lookup, a `null` lookup, a declared filter skipping its empty value, a `method=` filter returning the queryset it was given) comes back as the input queryset BY IDENTITY and composes as Django's `Q()` under `and` / `or` / `not`: `not: {}` keeps every row and `or: [{}, real]` is the real arm. A filter set overriding django-filter's `qs` materializes the branch through that `qs`, as at the top level, and composes as `pk__in`. An excluded empty membership (`exclude=True` over `[]`) is a constraint matching every row, never the identity (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_match_none_queryset`). The sibling reuses the parent's already visibility-scoped and `RelatedFilter`-constrained queryset, so visibility-before-filter carries through every recursive level. Recursion is capped at `FilterSet._MAX_LOGIC_DEPTH`, past which a typed [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] is raised.
  - [ ] A set built outside the pipeline with `request=` hands the hooks its leaves and arms reach a `filters/sets.py::_DirectRequestInfo` over that request ([Decision 8](#direct-construction-actor), [Definition of done](#definition-of-done) item 28), pinned live by `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_direct_filter_actor_api.py`.
  - [ ] A set's first construction publishes its filter table (`FilterSet.__init__`, [Decision 8](#direct-construction-actor), [Definition of done](#definition-of-done) item 29): a set no type serves and a plain subclass of a served set answer a walked key under every raw driver, a refused declaration raises at every construction, pinned live by `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_raw_filter_forms_api.py`.
  - [ ] A `RelatedFilter` head raw form data names, own or copied by an expansion, walks its path as a relation key (an override of `filter` under the overridden relation-key rule; through a sliced `queryset=` it admits the visible rows the slice holds, as a nested branch does) and validates its key against the visible rows of its last hop's target (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._flat_leaf_walk`, `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._scope_supplied_heads`; [Decision 8](#raw-form-heads), [Definition of done](#definition-of-done) item 30), pinned live by `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_direct_filter_actor_api.py` and in package rows by `tests/filters/test_sets.py`.
  - [ ] A declared branch's explicit `queryset=` admits the child rows among the rows it holds, read as an `IN` subquery of its keys (`filters/sets.py::_admitted_rows`), whatever `distinct()` the child filters, the target `get_queryset` or the queryset itself reads and however it is sliced or combined; a `queryset=` of another model is refused with `ConfigurationError` and one pinned to an alias the request does not read is refused ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 4a, [Definition of done](#definition-of-done) item 31), pinned live by `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_flat_copy_binding_api.py`, `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_library_api.py` and the sharded `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_multi_db.py`.
  - [ ] Three live tests pin the tree walk over `/graphql/`, since every one of the contracts is reachable through the real `BranchFilter`:
    - `and` intersects — [`examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_library_api.py::test_scalar_and_branch_intersects_matching_rows_live`][fakeshop-test-library]: `and: [{ name }, { city }]` returns only the branch matching BOTH scalar arms;
    - `or` unions — [`::test_scalar_or_branch_unions_matching_rows_live`][fakeshop-test-library]: `or: [{ name }, { city }]` returns the deduplicated union of the two arms;
    - `not` negates — [`::test_library_branches_not_filter_respects_root_visibility_over_http`][fakeshop-test-library]: `not: { name: { exact: ... } }` returns the complement against the root `get_queryset`-scoped parent queryset, and [`::test_library_books_filter_combines_and_or_not`][fakeshop-test-library] pins `not` nested inside an `and`.
    The package tier isolates private continuation counters, exact `Q()` identity and Python request-object identity in `test_evaluate_logic_tree_caps_recursion_depth`, `test_evaluate_logic_tree_and_direct_branch_treat_inactive_values_as_identity` and `test_evaluate_logic_tree_preserves_request_context` in [`tests/filters/test_sets.py`][test-filters]. Optional null and omitted OR arms run over HTTP in `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_filter_apply_arguments_api.py::test_an_inactive_optional_or_arm_does_not_widen_its_real_sibling`. Direct FilterSet excluded-empty and `.qs` customization semantics are pinned by `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_filter_logic_customization_api.py::test_an_authored_excluded_empty_membership_is_a_real_match_all_constraint` and `test_consumer_qs_override_runs_inside_a_logical_arm`; the inert-arm identity is pinned live by [`::test_logical_arm_without_a_constraint_is_the_identity_over_http`][fakeshop-test-library].
- [ ] Slice 5: Docs
  - [ ] [`docs/GLOSSARY.md`][glossary] carries the [`FilterSet`][glossary-filterset], [`RelatedFilter`][glossary-relatedfilter], [`Meta.filterset_class`][glossary-metafilterset_class] and [`filter_input_type`][glossary-filter_input_type] entries (all `shipped (0.0.8)`), listed under the Filtering category; the [Public exports][glossary-public-exports] section does not list them, because it enumerates top-level `django_strawberry_framework` re-exports and per [Decision 2](#decision-2--subpackage-layout-and-public-export-surface) the filter symbols live at `django_strawberry_framework.filters`.
  - [ ] [`docs/TREE.md`][tree] lists `filters/` in the current on-disk layout and the mirrored `tests/filters/` tree.
  - [ ] [`GOAL.md`][goal]'s migration narrative ([`GOAL.md #"migrates with a one-line parent-class swap"`][goal-migration-shape]) states the `django-filter` migration as a one-line parent-class swap to `django_strawberry_framework.filters.FilterSet`.

  - [ ] One in-process test constructs a `DjangoType` with BOTH `Meta.filterset_class` AND `Meta.orderset_class`, calls `finalize_django_types()`, and asserts that each family's input class is published in its own module, materialization ledger and factory cache. Consumer filter/order composition is pinned by `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_library_api.py::test_library_books_filter_and_order_compose`. The private identity row lives at [`tests/orders/test_composition.py`][test-orders]`::test_finalizer_materializes_both_input_families_in_their_own_namespaces` (with `::test_filter_and_order_share_lazy_related_class_mixin_via_neutral_module` pinning the shared [`sets_mixins.py`][sets-mixins] substrate); `tests/filters/test_composition.py` does not exist.

## Problem statement

A Django GraphQL package without a filter surface is not a credible alternative to either upstream: `graphene-django` ships `DjangoFilterConnectionField` + `filterset_class`, and `strawberry-graphql-django` ships `@strawberry_django.filter_type(Model)`. Consumers reach for `Meta.filterset_class = ItemFilter` the moment they wire a real schema.

Filtering is also the foundation for the other Layer-3 sidecars: [`OrderSet`][glossary-orderset] reuses its lazy-resolution architecture, [`AggregateSet`][glossary-aggregateset] is planned to reuse it again, [Meta.search_fields][glossary-metasearch_fields] is planned to reuse the `LOOKUP_PREFIXES` map, and [`DjangoConnectionField`][glossary-djangoconnectionfield] builds its `filter:` argument from the same factory output.

`django-graphene-filters` (the working reference per [`START.md`][start]) solved the hard part — a cycle-safe lazy-resolution pipeline that handles circular `RelatedFilter` references across modules. Layers 1–4 are library-agnostic Python and port verbatim; Layer 5 (the cycle-safe forward reference in the GraphQL schema build) needs Strawberry adaptation; Layer 6 (dynamic `FilterSet` generation) is not ported. [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline) pins this.

## Current state

- [`django_strawberry_framework/filters/`][filters]: `__init__.py`, `base.py`, `sets.py`, `factories.py`, `inputs.py`, with the mechanics shared with the order family single-sited in [`utils/inputs.py`][utils-inputs] and [`sets_mixins.py`][sets-mixins] (see [Decision 2](#decision-2--subpackage-layout-and-public-export-surface)).
- [`django_strawberry_framework/types/base.py::DEFERRED_META_KEYS`][base] holds `"aggregate_class"`, `"fields_class"`, `"search_fields"`; `"filterset_class"` is in `ALLOWED_META_KEYS`.
- [`django_strawberry_framework/types/finalizer.py::finalize_django_types`][finalizer] phase 2.5 runs `apply_interfaces` / `install_relay_node_resolvers`, then `_bind_filtersets`, then `_bind_ordersets`.
- [`examples/fakeshop/apps/library/schema.py`][fakeshop-library-schema] wires filtersets onto its library types (including `Meta.interfaces = (relay.Node,)` on `GenreType` and [`Meta.optimizer_hints`][glossary-metaoptimizer-hints] on `LoanType`) and is the host for the live HTTP filter coverage per [Decision 12](#decision-12--live-http-coverage-strategy).
- Upstream cookbook: `~/projects/django-graphene-filters/django_graphene_filters/` — the six-layer pipeline whose Layers 1–4 port verbatim and whose Layer 5 is Strawberry-adapted.
- Upstream `graphene-django`: `~/projects/django-graphene-filters/.venv/lib/python*/site-packages/graphene_django/filter/` — the parity-floor primitives (per [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor)).
- Upstream `strawberry-graphql-django`: `~/projects/strawberry-django-main/strawberry_django/filters.py` — the decorator-driven implementation. Its runtime pipeline (`process_filters`) is conceptually parallel to `FilterSet.apply_sync(...)` / `apply_async(...)`; the declaration surface (`@strawberry_django.filter_type(Model, lookups=True)` vs `class ItemFilter(FilterSet): class Meta: model = Item`) differs per the package's DRF-shaped positioning.

## Goals

1. Ship `FilterSet` + `RelatedFilter` + per-field `check_*_permission` gates + cross-relation traversal with cycle-safe lazy resolution, porting Layers 1–5 of `django-graphene-filters`' pipeline with Layer 5 Strawberry-adapted.
2. Keep [`Meta.filterset_class`][glossary-metafilterset_class] out of `DEFERRED_META_KEYS` only because the filter subsystem applies the configured class end-to-end — same gate as [`Meta.interfaces`][glossary-metainterfaces].
3. Establish the registration / factory / lazy-resolution pattern the sibling Layer-3 families ([`OrderSet`][glossary-orderset], [`AggregateSet`][glossary-aggregateset], [`FieldSet`][glossary-fieldset], [Meta.search_fields][glossary-metasearch_fields]) reuse without redesign.
4. Stay composable with [`DjangoOptimizerExtension`][glossary-djangooptimizerextension]'s [Queryset diffing][glossary-queryset-diffing] / [Plan cache][glossary-plan-cache] / [FK-id elision][glossary-fk-id-elision] / [Strictness mode][glossary-strictness-mode] — a filter clause is just another `.filter(...)` call on the consumer queryset.
5. Expose enough introspection (`FilterSet.get_filters()`, `FilterArgumentsFactory(cls).arguments`) for a maintainer to ask "what filter surface does this type support?" from the REPL in one call.
6. Earn package coverage through live fakeshop HTTP flows per [Decision 12](#decision-12--live-http-coverage-strategy).

## Non-goals

- **Ordering / aggregation / fieldsets / permissions cascade.** Each is its own card.
- **A `filter:` argument on `DjangoListField`.** [`DjangoListField`][glossary-djangolistfield] exposes `offset` / `limit` / `orderBy`, not `filter:`. Filtered root fields are plain `@strawberry.field` resolvers annotated with `FilterInput[...]` / `filter_input_type(...)`, or a [`DjangoConnectionField`][glossary-djangoconnectionfield], which reads `Meta.filterset_class`.
- **A `Meta.filterset_class = MyDjangoFilter` shape that accepts an arbitrary `django_filters.FilterSet` subclass without re-parenting.** Per [Decision 5](#decision-5--django-filter-as-the-foundation), `FilterSet` IS a `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet` subclass, so a consumer with `class MyFilter(django_filters.FilterSet):` migrates by swapping the parent class to `django_strawberry_framework.filters.FilterSet` and inherits the lazy-resolution + GraphQL-input layers. Accepting a plain `django_filters.FilterSet` would mean two consumer-facing surfaces for one wiring (one with `RelatedFilter` / `check_*_permission` recursion, one without), so the validator requires a package `FilterSet` subclass.
- **Replacing the optimizer's queryset-diffing contract.** Filters land as `.filter(...)` calls before the optimizer walks the selection tree.
- **Auto-generation of `FilterSet` from `Meta.fields` without declaring an explicit class.** Not shipped: the package carries no dynamic-factory machinery (upstream's Layer 6). Every filtered surface, [`DjangoConnectionField`][glossary-djangoconnectionfield] included, reads an explicit `Meta.filterset_class` on the target `DjangoType`.

## Borrowing posture

Filtering is the most heavily-borrowed surface in the package.

### From `django-filter` — embrace as the foundation

`django-filter` is a hard dependency at [`pyproject.toml #"django-filter>=25.2"`][pyproject], and [`GOAL.md #"migrates with a one-line parent-class swap"`][goal-migration-shape] promises consumers a one-line parent-class swap onto `Meta.filterset_class`. The package's `FilterSet` subclasses `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet` and inherits the load-bearing machinery — `declared_filters`, `base_filters`, `.filters`, `.form`, `.qs`, `get_filters()`, `Filter.method`, form cleaning, lookup generation. Per [Decision 5](#decision-5--django-filter-as-the-foundation) the package embraces the dependency.

The cookbook's `AdvancedFilterSet` inherits from `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet`; the package's `FilterSet` mirrors that inheritance. The added value is the cycle-safe lazy-resolution layer (Layers 1–4 of [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline)) and the Strawberry-adapted GraphQL-input layer (Layer 5), neither of which `django-filter` ships.

### From `django-graphene-filters` — borrow heavily (the cookbook is the working reference)

Local source path: `~/projects/django-graphene-filters/django_graphene_filters/`. The goal is to recreate "what the package enables for the schema author," not to port Graphene internals. Layers 1–4 are library-agnostic Python and port on top of the shared `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet` foundation:

- `django_graphene_filters/filters.py::BaseRelatedFilter` → `django_strawberry_framework.filters.base::RelatedFilter` (Layer 1).
- `django_graphene_filters/mixins.py::LazyRelatedClassMixin` → [`django_strawberry_framework/sets_mixins.py::LazyRelatedClassMixin`][sets-mixins] (Layer 2), re-exported through `django_strawberry_framework.filters.base`.
- `django_graphene_filters/filterset.py::FilterSetMetaclass` → `django_strawberry_framework.filters.sets::FilterSetMetaclass` (Layer 3).
- `django_graphene_filters/filterset.py::AdvancedFilterSet` → `django_strawberry_framework.filters.sets::FilterSet`, subclassing `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet` exactly as the cookbook does. `FilterSet.get_filters` runs Layer-4 cycle-safe expansion with the `_expanded_filters` cache and `_is_expanding_filters` re-entry guard read off the class's own `__dict__`.
- `django_graphene_filters/filterset.py::AdvancedFilterSet._apply_related_queryset_constraints` → `django_strawberry_framework.filters.sets::FilterSet._apply_related_constraints` — the explicit `queryset=` boundary that nested filters, and flat filters walking the branch, cannot escape, narrowed to active-branch scoping; a path whose walk crosses no such declaration is not bounded by it.
- `django_graphene_filters/filterset.py::AdvancedFilterSet.check_permissions` → recursion through `RelatedFilter`s into child filtersets' `check_*_permission` methods, narrowed to active-input-only scope ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation)).
- `django_graphene_filters/filter_arguments_factory.py::FilterArgumentsFactory` → the BFS that builds every reachable input class. Its per-lookup `filterset_to_trees` / `try_add_sequence` / `sequence_to_tree` tree-form is not ported: `_build_input_fields` groups by top-level field name (see [Edge cases](#edge-cases-and-constraints)).
- `django_graphene_filters/filterset_factories.py::_dynamic_filterset_cache` → **not ported**. Upstream's Layer 6 (memoized dynamic `FilterSet` generation) serves connection fields declared without an explicit `filterset_class`; the package has no such surface.
- `LOOKUP_PREFIXES` map for `construct_search` (`^` → `istartswith`, `=` → `iexact`, `@` → `search`, `$` → `iregex`) → ported verbatim.

### From `graphene-django` — borrow the user-facing primitives only

Local source path: `~/projects/django-graphene-filters/.venv/lib/python*/site-packages/graphene_django/filter/`. The cookbook builds on `graphene-django`'s filter primitives; the package carries the same parity floor per [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor):

- `graphene_django/filter/filters/array_filter.py::ArrayFilter`, `ArrayFilterMethod`.
- `graphene_django/filter/filters/range_filter.py::RangeFilter`, `RangeField`, `validate_range`.
- `graphene_django/filter/filters/list_filter.py::ListFilter`, `ListFilterMethod`.
- `graphene_django/filter/filters/typed_filter.py::TypedFilter` (base class for the three above).
- `graphene_django/filter/filters/global_id_filter.py::GlobalIDFilter`, `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter` (Relay-aware filtering against `relay.Node`-shaped types).
- `graphene_django/filter/filterset.py::GrapheneFilterSetMixin.FILTER_DEFAULTS` (FK/PK → `GlobalIDFilter`) — the source of the package's `FILTER_DEFAULTS`. The package's FK/PK → `GlobalIDFilter` mapping is **conditional on the target `DjangoType`'s Relay shape** ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor)): the upstream mapping is unconditional because `DjangoFilterConnectionField` (the path `FILTER_DEFAULTS` is tied to) always returns a Relay connection, while the package's `DjangoType` is non-Relay by default.

### From `strawberry-graphql-django` — borrow the runtime-pipeline pattern (not the surface)

Local source path: `~/projects/strawberry-django-main/strawberry_django/filters.py`. The runtime layer is conceptually parallel (`process_filters` compiles input values into queryset arguments); the declaration surface diverges per the package's DRF-shaped positioning:

- `strawberry_django/filters.py::FilterLookup` — the generic typed lookup descriptor (`exact`, `i_exact`, `contains`, ...) is conceptually similar to the package's lookup-bag generation, but the BFS factory drives the lookup expansion.
- `strawberry_django/filters.py::process_filters` — the runtime side of the pipeline. `FilterSet.apply_sync(input_value, queryset, info)` / `apply_async(...)` is the equivalent consumer-facing entry point (the `django-filter` instance method `filter_queryset(self, queryset)` is the tree-form override, with a different signature).
- `strawberry_django/filters.py::FILTERS_ARG` (`= "filters"`) — NOT borrowed. The package ships no argument-name constant; Strawberry derives the GraphQL `filter:` argument from the resolver's Python parameter name.
- `strawberry_django/filters.py::filter_type` decorator — NOT borrowed (the package's `Meta`-driven surface forbids decorator-on-input-type per [`START.md`][start] "Style the maintainer cares about").
- The legacy `filter` alias with `DeprecationWarning` (`strawberry_django/filters.py #"def __getattr__"`) — NOT borrowed.

### Explicitly do not borrow

- **Graphene's `lambda: target_input_type` cycle-safe forward references.** Replaced by `Annotated["{TargetFilterSet}InputType", strawberry.lazy("django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs")]` at Layer 5 (per [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline)). Strawberry's `LazyType.resolve_type` imports the named module and reads `module.__dict__[type_name]` — it does NOT traverse object paths — so the generated input classes MUST be real module globals of `django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs`, not dict entries on a private registry.
- **`graphene-django`'s `setup_filterset` wrapper-avoidance.** Strawberry's annotation resolution does not have the "Graphene{X}Filter" wrapping problem that motivated it.
- **`graphene-django`'s `replace_csv_filters`.** Strawberry's typed input handles `list[T]` natively.
- **`@strawberry_django.filter_type(Model, lookups=True)` decorator surface.** The package's `Meta`-driven shape forbids decorator-on-input-type for consumer-facing classes.

## User-facing API

The consumer surface is re-exported from `django_strawberry_framework.filters` (see [Decision 2](#decision-2--subpackage-layout-and-public-export-surface) for the full `__all__`): `FilterSet`, `RelatedFilter`, the parity-floor primitives, and the resolver-annotation helper in two spellings, [`filter_input_type`][glossary-filter_input_type] and `FilterInput`. [`Meta.filterset_class`][glossary-metafilterset_class] is the wiring on the existing [`DjangoType`][glossary-djangotype] surface; `FilterInput[MyFilter]` / `filter_input_type(MyFilter)` lets a normal `@strawberry.field` resolver accept a `filter:` argument that resolves to the generated input class at schema-build time.

**Resolver-facing API split:** consumers call `FilterSet.apply_sync(...)` from sync resolvers and `await FilterSet.apply_async(...)` from async resolvers per [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation). The `django-filter` instance method `filter_queryset(self, queryset)` is an internal override (tree-form logic) and is NOT the consumer surface. The thin `FilterSet.apply(...)` dispatcher always runs `apply_sync`.

### Default usage — declaring a `FilterSet`

```python
from django_strawberry_framework.filters import FilterSet, RelatedFilter

from . import models

class GalaxyFilter(FilterSet):
    # Reverse FK — referenced lazily by string so Galaxy and CelestialBody
    # filtersets can live in the same file without an import cycle.
    celestial_bodies = RelatedFilter("CelestialBodyFilter", field_name="celestial_bodies")

    class Meta:
        model = models.Galaxy
        fields = {
            "name": "__all__",
            "description": ["exact", "icontains"],
        }

class CelestialBodyFilter(FilterSet):
    # Explicit queryset acts as a scope boundary: nested filters
    # (`galaxy: {name: ...}`) and flat filters walking the branch
    # (`galaxyName`) can narrow it but cannot escape "public galaxies
    # only". It is not a visibility gate: with no `galaxy` predicate in
    # the input (active-branch scoping), parent rows are unconstrained,
    # so hiding rows is the job of GalaxyType.get_queryset.
    galaxy = RelatedFilter(
        GalaxyFilter,
        field_name="galaxy",
        queryset=models.Galaxy.objects.filter(is_private=False),
    )

    class Meta:
        model = models.CelestialBody
        fields = {
            "name": ["exact", "icontains"],
            "body_type": ["exact", "in"],
            "galaxy__name": ["exact"],
        }
```

`FilterSet` subclasses `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet` (per [Decision 5](#decision-5--django-filter-as-the-foundation)), so consumers keep every `django-filter` primitive they know — `declared_filters`, `base_filters`, `Filter.method`, `form`, `qs`, lookup parsing — and add the `RelatedFilter` lazy-resolution layer plus the GraphQL-input layer on top.

### Wiring into a `DjangoType`

```python
from django_strawberry_framework import DjangoType

from . import filters, models

class GalaxyType(DjangoType):
    class Meta:
        model = models.Galaxy
        fields = "__all__"
        filterset_class = filters.GalaxyFilter
```

`Meta.filterset_class` is the only wiring required at the `DjangoType` site. The finalizer-phase-2.5 binding (per [Decision 6](#decision-6--finalizer-phase-25-binding-seam--materialize-before-schema-ordering)) takes care of owner binding, lazy-related-filter resolution, and input-class materialization as module globals of [`django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs`][filters].

### Exposing the `filter:` argument on a resolver

```python
import strawberry

from django_strawberry_framework.filters import FilterInput

from . import filters, models

@strawberry.type
class Query:
    @strawberry.field
    def all_galaxies(
        self,
        info: strawberry.Info,
        filter: FilterInput[filters.GalaxyFilter] | None = None,
    ) -> list[GalaxyType]:
        # Visibility scoping ALWAYS runs before any filter clause (per
        # Decision 8): GalaxyType.get_queryset() applies the
        # request-scoped permissions / staff visibility / per-tenant filtering.
        # Filter clauses then operate on the already-scoped queryset, so a
        # filter cannot see through the visibility gate to hidden rows.
        queryset = GalaxyType.get_queryset(models.Galaxy.objects.all(), info)
        if filter is not None:
            # apply_sync because GalaxyType.get_queryset is sync; an async-only
            # get_queryset hook would require apply_async per Decision 8's
            # sync/async split.
            queryset = filters.GalaxyFilter.apply_sync(filter, queryset, info)
        return queryset
```

`FilterInput[filters.GalaxyFilter]` (equivalently `filter_input_type(filters.GalaxyFilter)`) evaluates to `Annotated["GalaxyFilterInputType", strawberry.lazy("django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs")]`, the canonical Strawberry forward-reference idiom. Strawberry stores the annotation unresolved at `@strawberry.type` decoration time; at `strawberry.Schema(...)` construction, `LazyType.resolve_type` imports `django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs` and reads `GalaxyFilterInputType` from `module.__dict__` — which the finalizer's phase-2.5 pass has materialized by then.

`GalaxyFilter.apply_sync(input_value, queryset, info)` and `GalaxyFilter.apply_async(input_value, queryset, info)` are the **resolver-facing classmethod pair** per [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation). Both carry `info` end-to-end so nested `RelatedFilter` branches can derive their target type's visibility scope. The shared pipeline derives the visibility-scoped querysets of every active `RelatedFilter` branch; normalizes the Strawberry input dataclass into the form-data dict `django-filter` expects (walking nested `RelatedFilter` inputs, mapping `and_` / `or_` / `not_` back to the logical-operator keys, mapping every per-lookup Python attribute back to its `django-filter` lookup name through `LOOKUP_NAME_MAP`); resolves the Django request through `FilterSet._request_from_info(info)` (a delegate to [`utils/permissions.py::request_from_info`][utils-permissions], which accepts `info.context.request`, a bare `HttpRequest` context, a mapping context whose `"request"` is an `HttpRequest`, and Strawberry's Channels context, and raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the unsupported `info.context` shape otherwise); constrains the queryset by every active branch; instantiates `cls(data=data, queryset=queryset, request=request)`; runs the active-input permission pass `_run_permission_checks(input_value, request)` **with active-input-only scope** (per-field `check_<field>_permission` gates fire ONLY for fields present in the input — `check_name_permission` does not raise on `filter: { title: { iContains: ... } }`); **explicitly calls `filterset.form.is_valid()` and raises `GraphQLError("Invalid filter input", extensions={"code": "FILTER_INVALID", "errors": filterset.errors.get_json_data()})` when validation fails**, after refusing a value that holds text the package refuses (a lone surrogate or an embedded NUL) with the same error, and when a cleaned temporal value is one the queryset's database cannot bind ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 6) (`BaseFilterSet.qs` only consults `self.errors` and falls through to `filter_queryset` otherwise; `ErrorDict.get_json_data()` gives consumers the stable `{field: [{"message": str, "code": str}, ...]}` shape); and returns `filterset.qs`.

[`DjangoConnectionField`][glossary-djangoconnectionfield] reads the target type's `Meta.filterset_class`, builds its `filter:` argument through `filter_input_type(...)`, and calls `apply_sync(...)` / `apply_async(...)` itself; the hand-written resolver above is the explicit shape for a plain `@strawberry.field`.

### Per-field permission gates

```python
from graphql import GraphQLError

class GalaxyFilter(FilterSet):
    class Meta:
        model = models.Galaxy
        fields = {"name": "__all__", "description": ["exact", "icontains"]}

    def check_name_permission(self, request):
        """Only staff users may filter by Galaxy.name.

        Active-input-only scope:
        this gate fires ONLY when the consumer's filter input includes a
        ``name:`` clause. A query like ``{ allGalaxies { id name } }`` with
        no ``filter`` argument, OR a query like
        ``{ allGalaxies(filter: { description: { iContains: "..." } }) ... }``
        that filters only by ``description``, does NOT trigger this gate.
        Permissions gate USE of the filter, not its declaration.
        """
        user = getattr(request, "user", None)
        if not user or not user.is_staff:
            raise GraphQLError("You must be a staff user to filter by Galaxy name.")
```

The framework calls `check_<field>_permission(request)` before applying the corresponding filter — **but only for fields present in the input** (active-input-only scope; see [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 5). A gate allows by returning `None` or `True` and denies by raising or by returning anything else: a `GraphQLError` raised inside the gate surfaces as an `errors` entry on the response, and any other return (`False`, `0`, `""`, a truthy non-bool) raises the plain `GraphQLError("Not authorized to use <field> on <FilterSet>.")` from [`utils/permissions.py::invoke_permission_method`][utils-permissions] with the same top-level outcome, so a DRF-style `return user.is_staff` never reads as an allow. The gate must be a plain `def`: an awaitable return is rejected with `SyncMisuseError` by [`utils/permissions.py::invoke_permission_method`][utils-permissions].

### Logical-and / logical-or / logical-not

The factory generates the three logical-operator fields on every `<TypeName>FilterInputType`. Python attribute names use the trailing-underscore convention (`and_`, `or_`, `not_`) because `and` / `or` / `not` are Python keywords and cannot be dataclass field names; the GraphQL surface names are exactly `and`, `or`, `not` via `strawberry.field(name="and")` / `name="or"` / `name="not"`. The same pattern applies to the `in` lookup (Python attr `in_`, GraphQL name `in`) per [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline) Layer 5's `LOOKUP_NAME_MAP`. Consumer-facing GraphQL shape:

```graphql
{
  allGalaxies(
    filter: {
      and: [
        {
          name: {
            iContains: "andromeda"
          }
        }
        {
          not: {
            isPrivate: {
              exact: true
            }
          }
        }
      ]
    }
  ) {
    id
    name
  }
}
```

The self-referential input type is handled by Layer 5's Strawberry-adapted forward reference per [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline):

- `and_: list[Annotated["<TypeName>FilterInputType", strawberry.lazy("django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs")]] | None`
- `or_: list[Annotated["<TypeName>FilterInputType", strawberry.lazy("django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs")]] | None`
- `not_: Annotated["<TypeName>FilterInputType", strawberry.lazy("django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs")] | None`

The `Annotated[...]` MUST wrap the forward-reference string directly (`Annotated["Name", strawberry.lazy(...)]`), not the surrounding `list[...]`. Strawberry's `LazyType.resolve_type` only resolves the annotated argument when that argument itself is a `ForwardRef`; wrapping `list["Foo"]` leaves the inner `"Foo"` unresolved and surfaces a `NameError` from Strawberry's type evaluation.

### Error shapes

- `Meta.filterset_class = <non-FilterSet>` → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`"<Model>.Meta.filterset_class must be a FilterSet subclass; got <value>"`) at type creation.
- `RelatedFilter("UnknownFilter")` that neither Layer-2 attempt resolves → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`"Cannot finalize Django types: filterset <qualified name> references an unresolved related-filter target. <ImportError text>"`) at finalize subpass 2, with the `ImportError` on `__cause__`.
- A `RelatedFilter` whose target model has no registered `DjangoType` → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] at finalize subpass 2.5 naming the filterset and the field.
- A filter of a published table (declared, generated from `Meta.fields`, or published by a `get_filters()` override) that a deep copy refuses (a bound `method=` over an object holding a lock or a Django cache handler, a `functools.partial` over one, such a value in the filter's `extra`, a `Meta.filter_overrides` `extra` included) → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`"FilterSet <qualified name>.<attr>: <FilterClass> cannot be deep-copied (<ExcType>: <message>); django-filter deep-copies every filter for each request, so a filter must hold nothing a deep copy refuses (a lock, a cache handler). <advice>"`, the copy's error on `__cause__`; `<advice>` is `Pass method= as the name of a FilterSet method (method="<name>") or as a plain function.` for a filter whose `method` itself refuses the copy, else `Keep the object off the filter: build it inside the filter's method or the FilterSet's filter_queryset, or hold it on a class.`) at finalize subpass 2.5 for every filter of every published table a wired filterset serves (its own and each set it reaches through a `RelatedFilter`; a filter a `get_filters()` override adds, borrows, renames or edits in place over the expanded surface included), named by the key the table publishes it under, and when a `RelatedFilter` expansion copies it; for a set the audit reaches through a `RelatedFilter` the attribute is followed by `(reached through RelatedFilter <set>.<attr>, then <set>.<attr>)`, the attributes crossed from the served set outermost first, and a filter an expansion copies first names the one declaration it copied through (a wired target another wired set's expansion copies first included). django-filter itself deep-copies every filter in `BaseFilterSet.__init__`, so such a filter could serve no request.
- A `RelatedFilter` whose target `FilterSet`'s `Meta.model` is not one whose rows its `field_name`'s relation correlates (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_require_correlated_target`, reading `django_strawberry_framework/optimizer/predicates.py::correlates_related_rows`) → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`"FilterSet <owner>: RelatedFilter '<attr>' has field_name '<path>', which reaches <Model>, but its target FilterSet <Target> is keyed on <OtherModel>. ..."`, or `"... declares no Meta.model."`) when `FilterSet.get_filters` builds the expansion, which resolves the target whatever form it was declared in.
- Two `RelatedFilter`s on one `FilterSet` naming the same relation → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`"<owner>: RelatedFilters '<attr>' and '<attr>' both declare the relation '<path>'. ..."`) at class creation, an inherited declaration included.
- A `RelatedFilter` whose `field_name` continues another branch's relation into a path that branch's target `FilterSet` reaches → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`"<owner>: RelatedFilter '<attr>' declares the relation '<path>', which continues the relation '<path>' of '<attr>' into '<path>', a path the target <Target> of '<attr>' reaches. ..."`) when `FilterSet.get_filters` builds the expansion.
- `Meta.fields = {"unknown_field": ["exact"]}` (a name that is not a model field, including a model property) → `django-filter`'s own `TypeError("'Meta.fields' must not contain non-model field names: unknown_field")` at `FilterSet` class creation.
- A class body's own `Meta` declaring a key a filterset does not read (`fiels`, `bogus`, `search_fields`) → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`"FilterSet <name>.Meta has unknown keys: [...]."`; a `Meta` that is not a class: `"FilterSet <name>.Meta must be a class; got <value>."`) at class creation, through [`utils/inputs.py::reject_unknown_meta_keys`][utils-inputs], the guard every write flavor runs. The allowed keys are the ones django-filter's `FilterSetOptions` stores (`model`, `fields`, `exclude`, `filter_overrides`, `form`, `unknown_field_behavior`, read from upstream's own options object) plus `filter_fields`.
- A `Meta.exclude` name the set declares, own or inherited, a `RelatedFilter` included (a declared filter is published whatever the exclude says; a tombstone `<name> = None` is no declaration) → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`"FilterSet <qualname>.Meta.exclude names declared filters: [...]. An exclude never removes a declaration: remove it, or disable an inherited one with `<name> = None` in the class body."`), checked first; any other name that is no field of `Meta.model` and no `Meta.fields` entry (`"emial"`, `"pk"`) → `"FilterSet <qualname>.Meta.exclude names unknown fields: [...]. Available: [...]. Exclude only a field of <Model> or a Meta.fields entry."`, the available names being the model's fields and the `Meta.fields` entries; a non-string `Meta.fields` / `Meta.exclude` entry → `"FilterSet <qualname>.Meta.<attr> entries must be field-name strings; got <kind> (<value>)."`. All three raise at class creation on a set with `Meta.model`; a model-less set's entries are checked where its fields expand (`FilterSet.get_fields`).
- `Meta.fields = "__all__"`, or `Meta.exclude` with no `Meta.fields`, on a set without `Meta.model` → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`"<name>.Meta.fields = '__all__' requires Meta.model so the filter field names can be derived from model._meta.get_fields()."`, or the exclude-only spelling) from `FilterSet.get_fields`, not at class creation, so a model-less base whose subclass supplies the model still builds.
- `Meta.filterset_class` naming a `FilterSet` that declares no `Meta.model` → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`"FilterSet <qualname> is declared as the filterset_class of <owner qualname> (model <Model>), but declares no Meta.model. A filterset's filters are typed, validated and applied against its Meta.model, so a filterset a DjangoType serves must declare one: set Meta.model = <Model> (with Meta.fields = [] to publish only its declared filters) on <qualname>, or on a subclass declared as the filterset_class."`) at finalize subpass 1, before any expansion, whatever else its `Meta` declares ([`types/finalizer.py::_bind_filterset_owner`][finalizer]).
- A leaf filter applied programmatically (`apply_sync` / `apply_async`, a consumer's own JSON argument) through an unbound set declaring no `Meta.model` → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`"FilterSet <qualname>: '<attr>' cannot apply: the set declares no Meta.model, which every leaf filter resolves against. Declare Meta.model on the set, or apply a subclass that declares it."`) at request time, masked by the error policy without `DEBUG` ([`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._leaf_form_entries`][filters]); its `RelatedFilter` branches resolve on their child sets' models and apply.
- A `Meta.fields` lookup-bag value that is neither `"__all__"` (a lazy translation string spelling it included) nor a re-readable collection of lookup names (`None`, a number, bytes, a bare string such as `"exact"`, which django-filter would read letter by letter as the lookup `"e"`, a lazy string spelling a lookup, a one-shot generator or `iter(...)`) → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`"FilterSet <qualname>.Meta.fields['<name>'] must be '__all__' or a re-readable collection of lookup-name strings; got <kind> (<value>)."`); a lookup that is no `str` (`[1]`, `[None]`, `[b"exact"]`, `[["exact"]]`, `[gettext_lazy("exact")]`) → `"FilterSet <qualname>.Meta.fields['<name>'] entries must be lookup-name strings; got <kind> (<value>)."`. Both run beside the key check (`django_strawberry_framework/sets_mixins.py::require_lookup_names`, called from `FilterSet.get_fields`), so they raise where it raises.
- `Meta.fields = {"name": ["unsupported_lookup"]}` on a scalar column → `django-filter`'s `FieldLookupError("Unsupported lookup 'unsupported_lookup' for field '<app>.<Model>.name'.")` at class creation. On a framework-owned relation key, any lookup outside `{exact, in, isnull}` → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] at class creation naming the filterset, the field and the lookup. On a Relay owner's own PK (its single-segment key) the same refusal fires at finalize, once the owner is bound; a dotted key ending on the set model's pk (`archive_genres__books__id` on a book set) filters the hop's row and is a plain column filter with every lookup.
- `filter_input_type(<non-FilterSet>)` → `TypeError("filter_input_type() requires a FilterSet subclass; got <value>")`, and `FilterInput[<non-FilterSet>]` → `TypeError("FilterInput[...] requires a FilterSet subclass; got <value>")`, both at evaluation time (validation is eager even though the returned `Annotated[...]` is lazy).
- Logical or related nesting deeper than `FilterSet._MAX_LOGIC_DEPTH` → [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] at request time.
- A filter value holding text the package refuses (a lone surrogate or an embedded NUL in the variables) → `GraphQLError("Invalid filter input")` with `extensions.code == "FILTER_INVALID"` and the field keyed (under its branch and arm path, step 6) to `"Text contains invalid Unicode (unpaired surrogate code points)."` / `"invalid"` (surrogate) or Django's `ProhibitNullCharactersValidator` message / `"null_characters_not_allowed"` (NUL) at request time, before any field cleans (cleaning is then skipped, so only those refusals are reported), on every backend and for every filter, a plain-`Field` relation key on a text primary key included ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 6); a GlobalID filter answers `GLOBALID_INVALID` instead unless a `filter` of its own (a `method=` included, `filters/sets.py::_decodes_before_binding`) replaces its decode, read for a flat copy of a child filter on the child filter the copy runs.
- A range bound whose wall clock in the current time zone leaves years 1..9999, read by a nested `SplitDateTimeWidget` whose `decompress` cannot split it (`0001-01-01T00:30:00+00:00` under `America/New_York`, which the database would bind) → `GraphQLError("Invalid filter input")` with `extensions.code == "FILTER_INVALID"` and the field keyed to `"Enter a value the current time zone can represent."` / `"invalid"`, before the form cleans ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 6).
- A temporal, duration or decimal filter value the scalar parse and the form clean accept but the database the queryset runs on cannot bind → `GraphQLError("Invalid filter input")` with `extensions.code == "FILTER_INVALID"` and the form field keyed (under its branch and arm path, step 6) to `"Enter a value the database can represent."` / `"invalid"`, after the form cleans, never the driver's error ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 6), beside another error when a local or already-cached source permits that bind ask; an uncached bind-only owner waits for an error-free native domain. Both refusals enter the actual form's errors through `django_strawberry_framework/filters/form_binding.py::BindAskingFilterForm` (the bind ask follows its entire consumer clean), so raw form data under the package's `FilterSet` answers them as form errors: `is_valid()` is false and `errors` holds them, `DjangoFilterBackend` answers its 400, a `FilterView` with `strict` lists no row, and a bare `.qs` applies only the leaves that cleaned (django-filter's contract for an invalid form; none once text is refused). A plain `django_filters.FilterSet` parent's form runs no ask, and neither does a form a consumer `form` override builds, which `_validate_form_or_raise` asks itself. The rule is the backend's own, asked of Django rather than modelled (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_unbindable_predicate`): for each filter reaching a temporal or decimal column (a `DateField`, `DateTimeField`, `TimeField`, `DurationField` or `DecimalField`, a queryset annotation's `output_field` included, or an `ArrayField` whose innermost elements are stored through one, `django_strawberry_framework/utils/_array_field.py::array_leaf_field`, its index, slice and `len` leaves included) or an `ArrayField` whose innermost elements are stored through an `IntegerField`, the filter's own `filter` builds its predicate on a copy of the queryset holding no other predicate and the clause is compiled on the queryset's database, no SQL run, so what Django raises building it (`Lookup.get_prep_lookup`: an `OverflowError`, `ValueError`, `TypeError` or `ValidationError`) or compiling it (the adapter, the year bounds: an `OverflowError` or `ValueError`) refuses the value, and so does an integer a lookup of the compiled clause binds outside the range the database stores for that lookup's left-hand side (`django_strawberry_framework/utils/errors.py::binds_integer`, the connection's own `integer_field_range`; `BigIntegerField`'s for a duration's microsecond count; no range for an internal type the backend states none for, as Django holds an `in` member or `range` bound there to none), which Django holds only in an integer comparison (`IntegerFieldOverflow`), never in an `in` list, a `range` bound or a duration; a lookup inside a queryset annotation the leaf reads is the queryset's own and is not read (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_bound_value_lookups`), so a constant there never refuses the filter's value; anything else a compile raises (a consumer lookup's own `as_sql`) propagates as the query would raise it, and Django's naive-datetime warning is emitted as the query emits it.
