On SQLite, MySQL and Oracle that is an aware time, an aware datetime whose projection onto the connection's time zone leaves years 1..9999 (a naive value made aware under a non-UTC `TIME_ZONE`, and a date on a datetime column, whose midnight is made aware there, included) and any aware datetime when `USE_TZ` is false; on every backend a `year` / `iso_year` comparison bound whose first or last instant leaves years 1..9999 (`iso_year` 9999 included) or whose value is not a number, and a datetime on a date column or through a `date` transform whose date in the default time zone (`TIME_ZONE`) leaves years 1..9999; on every backend an integer that an integer transform binds as sent (a `year` / `iso_year` member or bound, an `ArrayField`'s `len`, a transform a project registers with an `IntegerField` output, whatever its column, in a set a project resolver applies; `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_reads_integer_transform`) outside its `IntegerField` output's range (64 bits on SQLite, 32 on PostgreSQL); and on SQLite and MySQL, which bind a duration as its microsecond count, a duration whose microsecond count lies past 64 bits either way (2**63 microseconds is 106751991 days 04:00:54.775808); and on an `ArrayField` of integers every integer a lookup over the array or a slice of it binds for its right-hand side, each `ARRAY[...]` element, slice element and member of `gt`'s list parameter alike (no lookup types a whole-array element as an integer), held to the range the database stores for the element column (`django_strawberry_framework/utils/errors.py::refuses_integer_range`) and refused, never dropped, past it; a slice's bounds are no element, and an index `in` member or an array's `len` is held to its transform's own output range. A plain integer column is not asked: its generated `in` drops a member past its range and its comparisons are Django's own. On PostgreSQL, whose adapters pass datetimes and times through, what each lookup of the compiled clause binds for its right-hand side (`Lookup.process_rhs`, never what a queryset annotation the leaf reads binds) is held to the UTC offsets the server refuses (`django_strawberry_framework/utils/errors.py::refuses_utc_offset`, the one rule no Django call answers): 16:00 or more either way, or one carrying a fraction of a second, when parameters are bound as text (psycopg 2, or psycopg 3 unless the connection binds on the server: `OPTIONS["server_side_binding"]`, or an `OPTIONS["cursor_factory"]` that is not a `ClientCursor`, read from the parameters Django connects with); under server-side binding a datetime travels as a UTC instant and filters at any offset, while a time whose offset is 16:00 or more is still refused. A list parameter (an `ArrayField` lookup binding its list as one, `gt` or `in`) is bound as text in either mode, so each of its elements is held to the text rule, and the list is asked of psycopg 3's own dump of it (`django_strawberry_framework/utils/errors.py::psycopg_refuses_list_parameter`), which picks one element dumper for the whole list and refuses a naive time ahead of an aware one with its `DataError`. On PostgreSQL every decimal a lookup of the compiled clause binds for its right-hand side, each element of a list parameter included, is held to `numeric`'s format (`django_strawberry_framework/utils/errors.py::refuses_numeric_format`): Django's decimal adapter passes the value through and `numeric_in` parses its spelling whatever the column's `max_digits`, so one needing more than 131072 digits before the point or 16383 after (`-1e1000000`, `1e-16384`, `1.` followed by 16384 zeros; django-filter's `NumberFilter` caps only a positive value, at `1e50`) is refused in either binding mode; every other backend binds the decimal it is given, and SQLite compares it as a double (its `NUMERIC` column reads the bound text as one), so a non-zero value below the smallest double (`1e-1000000`) compares as zero and values differing only past a double's 15 to 17 significant digits can compare equal, answering rows rather than an error. Only a `filter` django-filter or this package ships is run to ask (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_runs_shipped_filter`, the module defining the `filter` the class resolves); a filter with a `filter` of its own (a `method=` included) or whose class overrides `filter` owns its value and is not asked, nor are a column that is neither temporal, a duration, a decimal nor an array of integers, an annotation whose output field Django cannot resolve and a path naming neither a model field nor an annotation. A projected flat copy is asked through the child filter it runs, over its child filter set's queryset. A decimal past the range of the integer field a shipped filter compares (an integer transform's output such as `year`, of a model column, a date annotation or a column past a `FilteredRelation` alias alike) is asked as the integer one past the same end (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_held_integer_value`), never converted, so `year__gt=-1e1000000` answers this error at once.

## Architectural decisions

### Decision 1 — Spec filename and canonical naming

The spec file is **`docs/SPECS/spec-027-filters-0_0_8.md`** (this document); the card body's `docs/spec-filters.md` predates the structured name. `docs/SPECS/` is where [`docs/SPECS/NEXT.md`][next] Step 8 archives a completed spec, together with its `-terms.csv` and `-rationale.md` companions under `docs/SPECS/appx/`; the `spec-<NNN>-<topic>-<0_0_X>` stem is the canonical name at either location.

Rationale companion — this Decision's justification and its two rejected alternatives: [Decision 1][rationale-d1].

### Decision 2 — Subpackage layout and public export surface

The filter subsystem is a subpackage at **[`django_strawberry_framework/filters/`][filters]** (NOT a flat single-file module). Five files:

- `__init__.py` — `__all__` is `ArrayFilter`, `ArrayFilterMethod`, `Filter`, `FilterInput`, `FilterSet`, `FilterSetMetaclass`, `GlobalIDFilter`, `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter`, `LazyRelatedClassMixin`, `ListFilter`, `ListFilterMethod`, `RangeField`, `RangeFilter`, `RelatedFilter`, `TypedFilter`, `filter_input_type`, `validate_range`. `Filter` IS `django_filters.Filter` itself — a plain re-export, deliberately shadowing the upstream name so a consumer writing a custom `method=` filter imports one base class from `django_strawberry_framework.filters`. The module also hosts the orphan-tracking ledger `_helper_referenced_filtersets` next to the helper that writes it ([Decision 11](#decision-11--filter_input_typefilterset-consumer-helper)). `FilterArgumentsFactory` is not re-exported; advanced callers import it from `django_strawberry_framework.filters.factories`.
- `base.py` — `TypedFilter`, `ArrayFilter`, `ArrayFilterMethod`, `RangeFilter`, `RangeField`, `validate_range`, `ListFilter`, `ListFilterMethod`, `GlobalIDFilter` (Strawberry-specific: accepts both `str` and `relay.GlobalID` inputs; validates the decoded `type_name`), `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter`, `RelationPkFilter`, `RelationPkMultipleFilter`, `TextPatternFilter` (the generated text-pattern lookup on a non-text column, a `CharFilter` whose leaf is a `String`), `MembershipInFilter` (the generated `in` base, an explicit `[]` being the empty set) with `membership_in_filter_class` (which puts it in front of the `in` class django-filter builds) and its `_MembershipCSVWidget` / `_MembershipCSVField` pair (a raw `?field__in=` reads as omission), the `IntegerInFilter` / `IntegerRangeFilter` reroutes for integer `in` / `range` lookups, the `EnumChoiceFilter` / `EnumLookupFilter` choice-column filters with their `EnumMemberValue` marker, and `RelatedFilter`; it re-exports `LazyRelatedClassMixin` from [`django_strawberry_framework/sets_mixins.py`][sets-mixins].
- `sets.py` — `FilterSetMetaclass`, `FilterSet` (with `Meta.model`, `Meta.fields`, the `FILTER_DEFAULTS` class attribute + `filter_for_field` / `filter_for_lookup` overrides per [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor), the `_owner_definition: DjangoTypeDefinition | None` slot bound at finalizer phase 2.5, the resolver-facing classmethod **pair** `apply_sync(input_value, queryset, info) -> QuerySet` / `async def apply_async(input_value, queryset, info) -> QuerySet` per [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation), and the thin `apply(...)` dispatcher that runs `apply_sync` and rethrows the typed [`SyncMisuseError`][querysets] as a `RuntimeError`); the named helpers the pipeline decomposes into (`_normalize_input`, `_derive_related_visibility_querysets_sync` / `_async`, `_run_permission_checks` — inherited from [`sets_mixins.py::ActiveInputPermissionMixin`][sets-mixins] — `_validate_form_or_raise`, `_apply_related_constraints`, `_branch_visibility_seed`, `_derive_flat_hop_visibility_async`); `get_filters`; `get_fields` (the public `django-filter` classmethod, overridden to expand a per-field `"__all__"` and to pin the whole-set sweep semantics of `Meta.fields = "__all__"` and of `Meta.exclude` with no `Meta.fields` — INCLUDE the PK unless `Meta.exclude` names it, EXCLUDE M2M); `filter_queryset` (the tree-form override); and `check_permissions(self, request, requested_fields=None)`, the public `django-filter`-shaped instance method over the same active-input walk.
- `factories.py` — `FilterArgumentsFactory` (input shape derived from the resolved filter instances through the named converter `convert_filter_to_input_annotation`). `FILTER_DEFAULTS` lives on `FilterSet`, so runtime `django-filter` and the factory read the same resolved filter instances and cannot drift.
- `inputs.py` — the per-module input-class namespace, `INPUTS_MODULE_PATH`, `build_input_class`, `_build_logic_fields`, `_build_input_fields`, `construct_search`, `LOOKUP_PREFIXES` (the `^` / `=` / `@` / `$` search prefixes), `LOOKUP_NAME_MAP` (the `<django-filter-lookup> → python_attr` map — `icontains → "i_contains"`, `iexact → "i_exact"`, `istartswith → "i_starts_with"`, `iendswith → "i_ends_with"`, `iregex → "i_regex"`, `isnull → "is_null"`, `in → "in_"`, `range → "range"`, etc.), the `FilterLookupTable` built by `filter_lookup_table` (each operator-bag lookup's `django-filter` form key, decided once per filter set; see "Per-field form keys via the lookup table" below), the `FieldSpec` dataclass + `_field_specs: dict[tuple[type[FilterSet], str], FieldSpec]` table (carries `(python_attr, django_source_path)` per generated input field; on the filter side `django_source_path` is the field's `check_<path>_permission` gate path), `convert_filter_to_input_annotation` (choice enums, `relay.GlobalID`, raw relation keys, `in` / `range` list shapes, nullable values, decimal / date / datetime / time / UUID, custom `Filter(method=...)` fields), `normalize_input_value(filter_instance, raw_value, field_name=None)` (the runtime symmetric — normalizes Strawberry input values back into the form-data shape `django-filter` expects, under the form key the lookup table holds for each lookup), `materialize_input_class`, `clear_filter_input_namespace`, `_materialized_names`.

**Where the mechanics live.** Several names this Decision pins are **domain-local aliases or inherited mixin members** rather than filter-owned implementations: the generated-input substrate is single-sited so the filter and order families share one implementation, and the `filters/` names stay addressable. `materialize_input_class`, `clear_filter_input_namespace`, `_materialized_names` and `_field_specs` are produced by [`utils/inputs.py::make_set_input_namespace`][utils-inputs], which returns the ledger / field-spec-table / materialize / clear quartet that `filters/inputs.py` binds under those names.

| Name on the `filters` surface | Implementation |
| --- | --- |
| `filters/inputs.py::FieldSpec` | [`utils/inputs.py::GeneratedInputFieldSpec`][utils-inputs] |
| `filters/inputs.py::build_input_class` | [`utils/inputs.py::build_strawberry_input_class`][utils-inputs] |
| `filters/base.py::LazyRelatedClassMixin` | [`sets_mixins.py::LazyRelatedClassMixin`][sets-mixins] |
| `RelatedFilter`'s owner-bind / lazy-target machinery | [`sets_mixins.py::RelatedSetTargetMixin`][sets-mixins] |
| `FilterSet._run_permission_checks` | [`sets_mixins.py::ActiveInputPermissionMixin._run_permission_checks`][sets-mixins] |

Public re-export is opted in by the subpackage's `__init__.py`, NOT by the top-level package: consumers `from django_strawberry_framework.filters import FilterSet, RelatedFilter, FilterInput` (matching the import shape in [`GOAL.md`][goal]'s astronomy showcase). The top-level package's `__all__` does not carry the filter symbols — widening it would affect every consumer, including those who never filter.

Rationale companion — this Decision's justification and its three rejected alternatives: [Decision 2][rationale-d2].

### Decision 3 — Six-layer lazy-resolution pipeline

The pipeline is upstream's six layers, of which the package ships five: Layers 1–4 port from `django-graphene-filters` library-agnostic (with `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet` as the shared foundation per [Decision 5](#decision-5--django-filter-as-the-foundation)), Layer 5 is Strawberry-adapted, and Layer 6 is not shipped.

**Layer 1 — Lazy class references in `RelatedFilter`** — port of `django_graphene_filters/filters.py::BaseRelatedFilter`. `RelatedFilter` accepts the target as a class, an absolute import path string (`"myapp.filters.ManagerFilter"`), an unqualified name (`"ManagerFilter"`), or a zero-arg factory. `_filterset` stores it unresolved; the `.filterset` property resolves it, and a resolved value that is not a `FilterSet` subclass raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`RelatedFilter._validate_target`).

**Layer 2 — Module-fallback resolution** — port of `django_graphene_filters/mixins.py::LazyRelatedClassMixin.resolve_lazy_class`. Two-step resolution: try as absolute path via `django.utils.module_loading.import_string`; on `ImportError`, retry with the `bound_class.__module__` prefix. Handles circular references within one module.

**Layer 3 — Metaclass discovery, deferred expansion** — port of the `django_graphene_filters/filterset.py::FilterSetMetaclass` pattern. The metaclass collects `RelatedFilter` declarations into `cls.related_filters` through the shared [`sets_mixins.py::collect_related_declarations`][sets-mixins] walk and binds each declaration to the new class so the module-fallback resolver knows the owning module (`bind_filterset` is the public spelling of that bind). A set declares each relation once: two `RelatedFilter`s naming one relation (its `field_name`, or its attribute name when it declares none) raise [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] at class creation, since a flat leaf and a flat-path permission gate carry only the ORM path and [`utils/permissions.py::walk_declared_relation_path`][utils-permissions] reads it as the one declaration on the relation. A longer declaration continuing a branch's relation (`shelf__branch` beside `shelf`) stays allowed unless the branch's target `FilterSet` reaches the continuation, by a filter of its own or through its own branches: the walk reads the longest declaration a path starts with, so the branch's flat leaves into that continuation would read the longer declaration's visibility, `queryset=` and gate, and [`sets_mixins.py::reject_captured_branches`][sets-mixins] raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] when `get_filters()` expands the set (both rules pinned for both families in [`tests/test_sets_mixins.py`][test-sets-mixins]). It **does not** expand; expansion is deferred to `get_filters()`.

**Layer 4 — Cycle-safe expansion + cache** — port of `django_graphene_filters/filterset.py::AdvancedFilterSet.get_filters`. The `_expanded_filters` cache and `_is_expanding_filters` re-entry guard are read off the class's own `__dict__` through the shared [`sets_mixins.py::expanded_once`][sets-mixins]. The expansion (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._expand_along`) follows every `RelatedFilter` path from the set being expanded, except through a declaration marked `RelatedFilter(flat=False)` (keyword-only, an exact `bool`, read-only; the `filters/base.py::RelatedFilter` docstring is the consumer contract), which it validates and copies nothing through, and ends a path where it reaches a filter set already on it (found by identity, so a consumer metaclass `__eq__` is never consulted), which contributes its own declared and `Meta.fields` filters there unexpanded, its `RelatedFilter` heads included (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._filters_along`); a target that reaches no set on the path contributes its published table. Every other contribution passes through the contributing set's `get_filters()` (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._reshape`), so a consumer override of it reshapes the surface a parent copies as it reshapes the set's own table. Each set's surface is therefore a function of the set and the declarations it reaches, whichever set the finalizer or a caller expands first, and only a set's own expansion is cached. The first `get_filters()` call that finds the expansion cached publishes the set's filter table (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._publish_filters`): what `cls.get_filters()` makes of the expansion, a consumer override's additions, removals and renames included, stored as `base_filters` and in one `filters/sets.py::ExpansionSnapshot` with its candidate rows, deferred copies and form keys, as django-filter's metaclass stores `get_filters()` as `base_filters`. Every package read of a set's filters reads its published table (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._filter_table`): the generated input and the lookup table, a parent's expansion, a projected leaf's child filter, the gate and visibility walks, each instance's copies and the finalizer audits, so an override runs at publication, never per request, and its filters are the set's own on its input and form, in a nested branch, as flat copies and around a cycle. `get_filters()` returns a fresh map per call, as django-filter's does, so an override editing what `super().get_filters()` returned edits its own copy. The surface grows with the number of paths, factorially in a dense graph of mutually related sets (six sets each declaring a `RelatedFilter` to every other publish about 11,000 flat filters per set, seven about 94,000), so two things bound the build. One build shares an expansion scope (`filters/sets.py::_ExpansionScope`) in which each inner path's expansion is kept by the set and the set of classes on its path, the only inputs it depends on, and served to every other path through the same sets, with no change to the published surface. And a set's flat filters are capped by `DJANGO_STRAWBERRY_FRAMEWORK["MAX_FLAT_FILTERS"]` (read by [`conf.py::max_flat_filters_setting`][conf], default `10_000`, validated by `filters/sets.py::_flat_filter_limit` as a non-negative `int`, never a `bool`; no spelling disables it): since a path's filters are copied onto every set before it, the walk stops at the first path whose flat filters pass the limit and raises a [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the root set, the declaration being expanded and the path, with the two remedies (mark the crossing set's `RelatedFilter` declarations `flat=False`, or raise the limit; a target behind a `flat=False` declaration still builds, and is capped, on its own). The limit bounds each expansion call's net key growth (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._expand_along`): the keys the copies add beyond the set's own population, so a copy replacing an own key adds none and a copied `RelatedFilter` head counts, while a `get_filters()` override's additions on the set itself are outside it. A failed build publishes nothing. The limit holds under `HIDE_FLAT_FILTERS`, which shapes the schema only. Per request of the package's own pipeline (an instance built inside `filters/sets.py::_pipeline_built`: the apply pipeline's instances, built by `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._build_stashed_instance`, and the child a projected leaf runs in, built by `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._projection_child`), `filters/sets.py::FilterSet.__init__` deep-copies the set's own filters and the flat copies its form data names (`filters/sets.py::_named_flat_copies`: a key after the form prefix names exactly the copies whose form field widget reads it, `filters/sets.py::ExpansionSnapshot` `copy_keys` built at publish by `filters/sets.py::_copy_form_keys` from `utils/widgets.py::widget_keys` over the widget of the form field each copy's own class builds, a `field` override included, read on a copy (`filters/sets.py::_widget_keys` through `filters/base.py::copied_form_field`), so `_min`, `_0` or a consumer's `__low` key names its copy and no key's head names another); `filters/sets.py::ExpansionSnapshot` records every copy of the set's own `RelatedFilter` expansion as `deferred_copies`, under whatever key the published table holds it (`filters/sets.py::_is_flat_copy`), a key the set's own `Meta.fields` also generates included (the copy takes that key), while a filter a `get_filters()` override adds is the set's own and never deferred. A flat copy is a spelling of the request, not a field of the parent's form: a copy the data does not name is not on the bound form (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet.get_form_class`, also once a subclass `__init__` has read `self.filters`) and is never applied (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._apply_flat_leaves`, also when a consumer form seam put it on the form, which still validates it, so a `required` one there refuses the request), whatever its absence would do: a `required` or disabled filter, a checkbox or consumer widget, a nonempty `empty_value`, a `ChoiceFilter` whose `null_value` is the empty value or a consumer `filter` acts only on the form of the set that declares it, at that set's root or inside an active nested branch, or when the request names its flat copy. A set with its own `Meta.form` or an override of `filter_queryset`, `qs`, `form`, `get_form_class`, `errors` or `is_valid` follows the same rule, so its code reads a flat copy's cleaned value with `.get`. Running an unnamed copy's absence on the parent was rejected for the row loss Decision 8 rejects for the unconditional branch constraint: `filter: {}` would drop parent rows, or fail, on a leaf the request never named. Any other construction (a django-filter view, DRF's `DjangoFilterBackend`, a consumer's own call) copies every filter as django-filter does, so its form renders every flat field. Reading `filters` copies every deferred filter, in the class's order, so a subclass `__init__` or any reader of `self.filters` sees and edits the copies the request runs; the package's own reads by name copy only the one they name (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._filter_named`). A bound form holds the set's own filters and the named flat copies; an unbound form holds every filter. A call reading a set again while its build runs (a captured-branch check, a target publishing first whose projected copies read the set for their form fields, a second thread) receives the same surface, built again once per build (kept in the build's `filters/sets.py::_ExpansionScope`) and not published; a read of a set's table while the set publishes (a projected copy around a cycle reading back to it) receives the table being published (`filters/sets.py::_publishing`), never a second publication. Two-condition cache write ([`sets_mixins.py::should_cache_expansion`][sets-mixins]): `related_filters` is in the class's own `__dict__` AND no string `_filterset` remains on any related filter. The expansion itself (`filters/sets.py::_expand_related_filter`) copies each child filter onto the parent under `<relation>__<child name>` with `field_name` rebound to the relation path, which reproduces the child filter exactly as long as its behavior is the filter object's own; the copy runs through `filters/base.py::copied_declared_filter`, so a child filter a deep copy refuses raises the same refusal as the deep-copy audit of [Decision 6](#decision-6--finalizer-phase-25-binding-seam--materialize-before-schema-ordering)'s subpass 2.5 (in subpass 2, or wherever `get_filters` expands the set) naming the child set, its attribute and the `RelatedFilter` it was reached through. A child filter whose behavior the child filter set owns (`filters/sets.py::_owner_bound_child`: a filter holding a `filter` of its own, read from its instance `__dict__` by `filters/sets.py::_own_filter` (the `FilterMethod` a `method=` installs, or any callable a consumer's filter `__init__` stores on the instance, which may read the object it was bound to rather than a rebound copy; a class-level `filter` override is the filter object's own and is rebound), every filter of a child that overrides `__init__` — `FilterSet._customizes_instances`, the same seam the row-preserving optimizer refuses — and an already projected copy expanded again) is copied as a `filters/sets.py::ProjectedChildFilter` instead: an instance of the child filter's own class, so input typing, value normalization and family recognition are unchanged, whose form field is the one a child filter set instance builds (one instance per parent filter set instance and child class, built on first use by `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._projection_child` and shared by every projected leaf of that parent; a child `__init__` customization and a request-aware callable `queryset` apply, and the child carries the parent's request and a link back to the parent instance, through which a GlobalID filter running inside it reads the executing schema's published type names) and whose `filter` runs the child filter inside that instance over the branch's visible child rows (the target type's `get_queryset` over the child model's rows on the parent's database alias, as the nested branch scopes them in [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 3), then keeps the parent rows whose relation reaches a matching child row the declaration's explicit `queryset=` admits (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._apply_relation_leaf`, restricting through `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._reaches_hop`, the correlated `EXISTS` the nested branch restricts through). A supplied value runs the child filter once, over the visible child rows; one returning those rows unchanged (django-filter's empty-value skip) leaves the parent unchanged. An absent value (the request carries no key the copy's form field reads, on a set built outside the pipeline, where every copy is on the form) runs the child filter over the child model's unscoped rows only to learn whether it constrains anything, and derives nothing unless it does. A child filter that is itself a flat leaf walking a declared hop of the child set walks it the same way. Each flat leaf stays its own predicate — two owner-bound leaves may be satisfied by different child rows. An expanded copy that is not owner-bound walks, through its rebound `field_name`, the branch it was expanded from, so Decision 8's flat-leaf scoping applies to it by path, and runs as a filter of the last hop's target set: the per-request copy it runs (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._bound_terminal`) is bound as django-filter's `BaseFilterSet.__init__` binds a set's own filters, its `parent` the target set instance and its `model` the target's model together (`filters/sets.py::_bind_filter`, which `FilterSet._copy_filter` binds through too), so a filter resolving its `field_name` against `self.model` while it filters (django-filter's `AllValuesMultipleFilter`, whose `field` `MultipleChoiceFilter.get_filter_predicate` reads per value; a consumer `field` or `filter` calling `get_model_field(self.model, self.field_name)`) answers its nested twin's rows, on a raw construction too. The form field the parent's copy built, which cleaned the value, carries over; the walked copy keeps its own `extra`, so a `field` read there leaves the parent's copy as bound. A `RelatedFilter` entry of the child is a branch, not a flat leaf, and is copied as is.

**Layer 5 — BFS schema build with module-global materialization (Strawberry-adapted)** — the BFS from `django_graphene_filters/filter_arguments_factory.py::FilterArgumentsFactory`, single-sited on [`utils/inputs.py::GeneratedInputArgumentsFactory`][utils-inputs]. The cycle-safe forward reference changes shape because Strawberry's lazy mechanism is module-path-only, NOT object-path:

- Strawberry's `strawberry.types.lazy_type.LazyType.resolve_type` imports the module named in `strawberry.lazy("<module-path>")` and reads `module.__dict__[type_name]`. It does NOT traverse object paths (`module.attr.subattr`) and does NOT look up entries in a sidecar dict.
- Consequence: every generated filter input class MUST be a **real module global** of [`django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs`][filters], not an entry in a private dict.
- The Strawberry idiom is `Annotated["{TargetFilterSet}InputType", strawberry.lazy("django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs")]` — a string forward reference combined with the module-path marker.
- The `Annotated[...]` MUST directly wrap the forward-reference string. For list-typed fields (the `and_` / `or_` operators), push the `Annotated[...]` INSIDE the `list[...]`: `list[Annotated["Name", strawberry.lazy(...)]] | None`, NOT `Annotated[list["Name"], strawberry.lazy(...)] | None`.
- The factory emits the `Annotated[...]` shape in field annotations so cycle-safe references between filtersets keep working; it does **not** write module globals. Materialization is the finalizer's: [`materialize_input_class(name, cls)`][filters] — two arguments; the destination module is always `django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs` — is called once per built class at phase-2.5 subpass 4 (per [Decision 6](#decision-6--finalizer-phase-25-binding-seam--materialize-before-schema-ordering)), whose filter-side entry point is [`django_strawberry_framework/types/finalizer.py::_bind_filtersets`][finalizer] and whose materialize loop is the shared `_bind_sidecar_sets`.
- Per-lookup Python attrs are spelled by `LOOKUP_NAME_MAP` in `inputs.py`, which marks each lookup's word boundary (`icontains` → `i_contains`) so the executing schema's `NameConverter` can name the field from it, as it names every output field: `iContains` under the default config, `i_contains` under `auto_camel_case=False`, a custom converter's spelling otherwise ([`spec-051`][spec-051] owns the naming contract). The map enumerates every supported `django-filter` lookup as `lookup_name → python_attr`:

  | django-filter lookup | Python attr (dataclass field) |
  | --- | --- |
  | `exact` | `exact` |
  | `iexact` | `i_exact` |
  | `contains` | `contains` |
  | `icontains` | `i_contains` |
  | `startswith` | `starts_with` |
  | `istartswith` | `i_starts_with` |
  | `endswith` | `ends_with` |
  | `iendswith` | `i_ends_with` |
  | `regex` | `regex` |
  | `iregex` | `i_regex` |
  | `gt` / `gte` / `lt` / `lte` | `gt` / `gte` / `lt` / `lte` |
  | `isnull` | `is_null` |
  | `in` | `in_` |
  | `range` | `range` |
  | `date` / `year` / `month` / `day` / `week_day` / `quarter` / `hour` / `minute` / `second` | mirror (`week_day`) |

  `in` is a Python keyword, so its attr is `in_` and it is the one lookup pinned, through `strawberry.field(name="in")`; it publishes `in` whether or not `auto_camel_case` is on, and a converter overriding `get_graphql_name` respells it as it respells any explicit name.

  Both the factory (`_build_input_fields`) and the runtime input-data normalizer (`FilterSet._normalize_input`) read one per-class lookup table, `filters/inputs.py::filter_lookup_table`, which names each lookup's Python attr through `LOOKUP_NAME_MAP` and holds that lookup's `django-filter` form key, so the GraphQL surface and the form-data conversion never disagree. `_build_input_fields` emits each lookup field with no `name=` except the keyword `in` (`filters/inputs.py::lookup_leaf_names`), and an `in_` value lands under the `in` filter's own form key when the form-data dict is built.

**Per-field form keys via the lookup table.** `Meta.fields = {"galaxy__name": ["exact"]}` renders as the flat GraphQL field `galaxyName: { exact: ... }`. `filters/inputs.py::filter_lookup_table` groups the set's published filter table (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._filter_table`) by head, once per published table: a declared filter, and a `RelatedFilter` expansion of a child's declared filter, is its own head (its name is its form key even when it ends in its own lookup token, `name__exact`); a generated `<path>__<lookup>` filter joins the `<path>` head under `<lookup>`, where `<lookup>` is the whole `lookup_expr`, a transform chain included (`year__gt`), less the trailing `__exact` django-filter's `get_filter_name` drops. Each head maps every lookup to `(form key, filter)`, where the form key is the filter's own name in the published table, and the table is keyed by both the head's spelling (`galaxy__name`) and its flattened input attr (`galaxy_name`). The form-data dict for an active `galaxyName: { exact: ... }` therefore carries `{"galaxy__name": <value>}`, NOT `{"galaxyName": <value>}` or a reverse-mangled `{"galaxy_name": <value>}`, and a head that is both declared and generated (`name = CharFilter(lookup_expr="iexact")` beside `Meta.fields = {"name": ["icontains"]}`) binds `iExact` to `name` and `iContains` to `name__icontains`. A hand-built mapping passed to `apply_sync` / `apply_async` reads the same table, under either spelling, whether or not the input class was ever built, and names a bag's lookups by their python attrs (`{"occurred_at": {"year_gt": 2020}}`), never by the django-filter form key (`occurred_at__year__gt` is no input field).

The generated-input substrate's [`utils/inputs.py::GeneratedInputFieldSpec`][utils-inputs], re-exported as `FieldSpec` by `filters/inputs.py` (and by `orders/inputs.py`), still records each generated input field:

```python
# django_strawberry_framework/utils/inputs.py
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class GeneratedInputFieldSpec:
    # dataclass field name on the Strawberry input class (e.g., "galaxy_name"); the
    # executing schema's NameConverter names the wire field from it ("galaxyName" by default)
    python_attr: str
    # filter side: the check_<path>_permission gate path (e.g., "galaxy__name")
    django_source_path: str
```

`_build_input_fields` fills the `FieldSpec` table as it builds the input fields, keyed by `(filterset_class, python_attr)`. On the filter side `django_source_path` is read only by the permission gates (`utils/permissions.py::active_permission_targets`): a declared head, and an expansion of a child's declared filter, gates on its own name, and any other head gates on its filter's `field_name`, so `name` and `name__icontains` share the `name` gate. A walked leaf then fires exactly the gates its nested twin fires, whatever lookup, `isnull` or `exclude` it applies. The gate path fires the owner's own gate and, when it is an ORM path, through `utils/permissions.py::_fire_flat_relation_path_gates`, the gates `utils/permissions.py::relation_path_gates` reads off it (a declared head's name, a flat copy of a child set's declared filter included, is no ORM path and walks nothing: `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._gate_path_spells_relations`, the seam `utils/permissions.py::run_active_input_permission_checks` reads, which an order set answers yes for every source path; so `shelf__named` beside `real_shelf = RelatedFilter(ShelfFilter, field_name="shelf")` fires no `check_real_shelf_permission`, and neither does a declared `shelf = CharFilter(field_name="title")` there; a leaf that is no declared head walks its `field_name` even where a declared head's name spells the same string, `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._gate_path_walk_gates`): each branch `utils/permissions.py::walk_declared_relation_path` walks (keyed on the public attr, as `check_<branch>_permission`), then the last resolved target set's gate for the path the walk leaves there, the gate path the twin's leaf on that set carries. That remainder may cross an undeclared relation and gates on the whole remainder (`property__name`), and a relation-key path leaves none, so its target gate is the target's primary key: `Meta.fields` key `shelves` beside `visible_shelves = RelatedFilter(ShelfFilter, field_name="shelves")` fires `check_visible_shelves_permission` and `ShelfFilter.check_id_permission`, as `visibleShelves: { id: ... }` does. A leaf whose gate path is not its ORM path also fires its nested twin's gates through `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._permission_walk_gates`, read by `utils/permissions.py::run_active_input_permission_checks`, which collects `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._leaf_gates` per supplied leaf: an expanded copy, projected or rebound, fires its branch's gate and every gate its child filter fires supplied on the child set (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._supplied_leaf_gates`: the child filter's gate path, `filters/inputs.py::leaf_gate_path`, that path's walk and the child filter's own walk's, recursively), read from the `ChildProjection` the expansion stamps on the copy (`filters/sets.py::_expansion_record`), never from the key that holds it (a rebound copy borrowed onto a set that does not expand that branch into that child set is that set's own filter, `filters/sets.py::ChildProjection.holds_in`; a borrowed `ProjectedChildFilter` there has no branch to run its child filter across and is refused with a `ConfigurationError` when the set publishes its table, `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._reject_foreign_projection`; a set declares a deep copy of any filter another set owns, `filters/sets.py::FilterSetMetaclass`, so the owner's object never changes under it), and any other leaf walking declared hops (`FilterSet._flat_leaf_walk`, the hops its row scoping reads) fires `relation_path_gates` over its own `field_name`. So `inSeriesHandle`, the copy of `SeriesFilter.handle` under `PrintingFilter.in_series` (a branch over `series`, so the key's own name walks no hop), fires `check_in_series_permission` and `SeriesFilter.check_handle_permission`, as `inSeries: { handle: ... }` does. So `ShelfFilter.home_branch` over `branch` fires `check_home_branch_permission`, `check_branch_permission` and `BranchFilter.check_id_permission`, and `GenreFilter.without_book_titled` over `books__title` fires `check_without_book_titled_permission`, `GenreFilter.check_books_permission` and `BookFilter.check_title_permission`. A filter declared on the parent holding a `filter` of its own (`filters/sets.py::_own_filter`: a `method=`, or a callable stored on the instance) walks no hop and fires only its own gate (its Python owns its scoping; [`GOAL.md`][goal] "Trust boundary"). Every gate shares the per-class dedup, so each fires at most once per request. Form keys never come from it.

**`HIDE_FLAT_FILTERS` — the one setting attached to the flat-field contract.** The flat rendering above is the default. A consumer who wants only the nested `RelatedFilter` surface sets `DJANGO_STRAWBERRY_FRAMEWORK["HIDE_FLAT_FILTERS"] = True`, read through [`conf.py::hide_flat_filters_setting`][conf] (default `False`). When set, `_build_input_fields` leaves out every lookup that is a **flat copy** of a declared `RelatedFilter`'s expansion (`category__name`, `entries__property__category__name`), at every depth: a lookup carrying the `ChildProjection` the expansion stamped naming one of the set's own declarations (`filters/sets.py::_is_flat_copy`), under whatever key the set's filter table holds it, so a copy a `get_filters()` override holds under a key of its own (`PatronFilter.borrowed_title`) is hidden too. A head left with no lookup is not emitted; a head holding both copies and the set's own lookups keeps the own ones (`PrintingFilter`'s `editionImprint` keeps its own `iStartsWith` and drops the `exact` and `iContains` copies of `EditionFilter.imprint`). Nothing becomes unreachable: each hidden copy stays filterable through its nested branch, and every filter the set holds itself stays flat — a same-model path, an explicit `Meta.fields` traversal that the target set does not itself publish (when it does, the expansion's copy takes the key), with or without a `RelatedFilter` of that name (`LoanFilter`'s `book__loans__patron__email`, `PrintingFilter`'s `edition__printings__co_publishers`), a declared filter, and a filter a `get_filters()` override adds. The setting shapes the schema only; it is not a visibility control, since a shown flat field walking a declared branch answers over the same visible rows its nested spelling reads ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 3), and every lookup it keeps behaves exactly as it does with the setting off. The key's name and default come from `django-graphene-filters`, where it hides the flat connection arguments; upstream never publishes the expanded copies at all (`django_graphene_filters/connection_field.py::_get_trimmed_filterset_class` drops every key under a `RelatedFilter`'s name from those arguments with its `is_expanded_child` test whatever the key says, and its `filter` input renders a `RelatedFilter` as a reference to the target's input), so the shown flat expanded fields are this package's own surface. Hiding them leaves the expansion and `MAX_FLAT_FILTERS` in force, since a form-data client can still send a flat key on an enabled declaration.

Graphene's `graphene.InputField(lambda tn=target_name: input_object_types[tn])` and the Strawberry idiom (`Annotated["...", strawberry.lazy("...")]` over a module-global namespace) are conceptual twins — both defer the type reference until schema walk; only the lookup mechanism differs (dict-keyed for Graphene; `module.__dict__` keyed for Strawberry).

**Layer 6 — Memoized dynamic FilterSet generation** — **not shipped.** Upstream's `django_graphene_filters/filterset_factories.py::_dynamic_filterset_cache` memoizes a generated `FilterSet` per `(model, fields, extra_meta)` for connection fields declared without an explicit `filterset_class`. Every filtered surface in the package names `Meta.filterset_class`, so there is nothing to generate and no cache to key.

**`Meta.fields = "__all__"` scope and intentional override of django-filter's default.** The shorthand expands to the model's scalar fields and forward FK / OneToOne / `ForeignObject` relations plus the primary key (a `ForeignObject` leaf matches the target's pk through every link column), under the conditional `FILTER_DEFAULTS` per [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor). Relation traversal under `"__all__"` is **explicitly excluded** — a relation becomes a nested branch only when the consumer declares `RelatedFilter(...)` for it. `Meta.exclude` with no `Meta.fields` is the same whole-set sweep: `django-filter` resolves it to `"__all__"`, and `FilterSet.get_fields` keys both deltas on that effective sweep, never on the literal string, so the two spellings publish the same filters (an empty `Meta.exclude` included). A name `Meta.exclude` lists is never a filter key, the primary key included. This overrides `django-filter`'s `django_filters.utils.get_all_model_fields` in two ways:

- `django-filter` **excludes** `AutoField` primary keys; `FilterSet.get_fields` **adds** the primary key (with `exact`) unless `Meta.exclude` names it, because filtering by ID (`GlobalIDFilter` on a Relay owner, a scalar key otherwise) must be reachable through the shorthand.
- `django-filter` **includes** M2M relations; `FilterSet.get_fields` **removes** every many-to-many field the sweep added (the model's `Options.many_to_many`, which is where upstream's sweep reads them, so a third-party M2M field that does not subclass `ManyToManyField` goes too), because traversing a M2M without an explicit target `FilterSet` would either auto-generate one (the [Non-goals](#non-goals) "no implicit `FilterSet` generation" rule forbids this) or expose a bare-key filter the consumer did not ask for.

`FilterSet.get_fields` — the public `django-filter` classmethod — carries both deltas, and also expands a per-field `"__all__"` value (`{"name": "__all__"}`) to the field's concrete lookups, which upstream would otherwise pass through as a literal lookup expression. The set is read from the field instance's own registry on every expansion (`Field.get_lookups()` on the instance, read by `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_lookups_for_field`: the class registrations along the MRO plus the instance's own), so a Django lookup class registered on a field class publishes on every field of that class (under whatever name it is registered), an instance registration on that field alone, each wherever and whenever it is made before the set builds; a lookup class that is not Django's own (`django_strawberry_framework/utils/querysets.py::_type_is_genuinely_django`: a project's or a third-party app's, a subclass of a Django lookup) is left out on every set, served or not, as the visibility boundary a connection's `filter:` runs inside refuses it, and is published only when `Meta.fields` names it, where the served-leaf audit refuses it on a set a connection serves ([Decision 6](#decision-6--finalizer-phase-25-binding-seam--materialize-before-schema-ordering)); a `GeneratedField`'s registry is its `output_field` instance's, the one Django's query resolves the column's lookups on (`django_strawberry_framework/utils/relations.py::lookup_registry`). Django's text-pattern lookups (`iexact`, `contains` / `icontains`, `startswith` / `istartswith`, `endswith` / `iendswith`, `regex` / `iregex`, matched by lookup class so `ArrayField`'s own `contains` is not one) join that set only on a text column, one whose class-level default filter is `CharFilter` (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_is_text_column`, read off the package's `FILTER_DEFAULTS` table, never off `Meta.filter_overrides`): each matches the column's text rendering against a string, which an input typed by a date, time, number, Boolean or UUID column cannot express. Every other column keeps `exact` / `in` / `isnull` and its comparisons. The rule binds only where the column's filter is the package's own default (`FilterSet._origin_for_selection_class` is `framework_default`): a column whose filter the consumer supplies (`Meta.filter_overrides`, a `FILTER_DEFAULTS` shadow), including one with no package default made filterable that way (a `JSONField`, a `FileField`, an `ArrayField`), keeps the whole set, since its filter class owns the lookups it accepts, and the schema build refuses a lookup a served set's input cannot type: over an `ArrayField` a list filter types only `exact`, `contains`, `contained_by` and `overlap`, and a `BooleanFilter` the null test ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor)), so a served set's per-field `"__all__"` there (`{"tags": "__all__"}` under `Meta.filter_overrides = {ArrayField: {"filter_class": ArrayFilter}}`) is refused naming the first lookup it cannot type (`iexact`), and the consumer lists the lookups it wants (`{"tags": ["exact", "contains", "contained_by", "overlap"]}`). A lookup the generated set leaves out is not a filter, so django-filter form data naming it is ignored as any unknown key is. Before any of it, the method reads `Meta.fields` / `Meta.exclude` as plain `str` text (see the `str`-subclass row under Edge cases). Tests pin both `"__all__"` deltas: the PK IS generated AND a M2M relation is NOT generated without an explicit `RelatedFilter`; an exclude-only set publishes the `"__all__"` keys with the same exclude, and an excluded primary key is never added back.

Rationale companion — this Decision's justification and its five rejected alternatives: [Decision 3][rationale-d3].

### Decision 4 — Upstream-primitives parity floor

The package ships parity equivalents for every upstream filter primitive:

- `ArrayFilter(TypedFilter)`, `ArrayFilterMethod(FilterMethod)` (from `graphene_django/filter/filters/array_filter.py`).
- `RangeFilter(TypedFilter)`, `RangeField(Field)`, `validate_range` (from `graphene_django/filter/filters/range_filter.py`).
- `ListFilter(TypedFilter)`, `ListFilterMethod(FilterMethod)` (from `graphene_django/filter/filters/list_filter.py`).
- `TypedFilter(Filter)` (from `graphene_django/filter/filters/typed_filter.py`) — base class for the three above.
- `GlobalIDFilter(Filter)`, `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter(MultipleChoiceFilter)` (from `graphene_django/filter/filters/global_id_filter.py`).

The `FILTER_DEFAULTS` map is a **class attribute on the package `FilterSet`** (not a factory-side parallel map) so the `django-filter` runtime AND the Strawberry input factory agree on the chosen filter primitive. The relation mapping is **conditional on the target `DjangoType`'s Relay shape**:

- An FK / PK / M2M whose target type — `registry.get(target_model)` of the key's own relation, the terminal relation of a dotted `Meta.fields` key, bound or not — implements `relay.Node` (per `Meta.interfaces = (relay.Node,)`) → `GlobalIDFilter` for a single-valued relation, `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter` for a multi-valued one; the input accepts the Relay GlobalID string and the filter decodes it before applying the queryset clause.
- An FK / PK / M2M whose target type is **non-Relay** (the default, e.g. `ShelfType` in the fakeshop library app), or whose target model no `DjangoType` exposes → `RelationPkFilter` (single-valued `exact`) / `RelationPkMultipleFilter` (`in`, and `exact` on a multi-valued relation), which accept the target's raw primary key typed from its key column (typically `int`, sometimes `uuid.UUID` or `BigInt`).
- The owner's **own primary key**, named by its single-segment key, under a Relay-Node owner → a GlobalID filter typed to the owner. A dotted key ending on the same column filters the hop's row by its plain column.
- On every relation key `isnull` stays a boolean filter: the class django-filter selects off the `BooleanField` entry of the merged `FILTER_DEFAULTS` + `Meta.filter_overrides` map (a consumer `BooleanField` override owns it), and for a relation class that map does not name (a forward `ForeignObject` of any link width, its reverse, a `GenericRelation`), where django-filter returns `(None, {})` before its `isnull` branch, the pair that branch builds for a `ForeignKey` (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._null_test_filter_for_relation`); a map with no `BooleanField` entry leaves such a key unrecognized under `Meta.unknown_field_behavior`. On a framework-owned relation key, `exact` / `in` map to the identity filters above and any other lookup raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] at class creation. A consumer-selected override (`Meta.filter_overrides` or a shadowed `FILTER_DEFAULTS`) is returned unchanged and is never subject to this policy.
- A relation whose link onto the target reads columns other than the target's primary key (a `ForeignKey` / `OneToOneField` with `to_field=`, a `ForeignObject` whose `to_fields` are not the pk, a forward or reverse many-to-many whose through model's foreign key onto the target has a `to_field`) stores and joins on those columns, while a raw-pk value, and a Relay GlobalID of a target whose id is its primary key, both carry the target's primary key, so `filter(<relation>=<pk>)` would compare the wrong column (Django's own many-to-many lookup compares the through foreign key's `to_field`). `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet.filter_for_field` marks the generated identity filter (`GlobalIDFilter` / `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter` / `RelationPkFilter` / `RelationPkMultipleFilter`) when `django_strawberry_framework/utils/relations.py::non_pk_forward_link` answers a link for the relation's link onto its target: that link's target columns are not the target model's single primary-key field. The link is the relation itself for a forward single relation and, for a many-to-many, the through model's foreign key onto the target (`django_strawberry_framework/utils/relations.py::m2m_through_link_fields`; a `to_field` on the foreign key onto the source never enters the comparison). A marked filter compiles against `<field_name>__pk`, derived from its live `field_name` at filter time so a `RelatedFilter` expansion that rebases `field_name` keeps the qualification. A GlobalID filter whose target type's id is another column compiles on that column instead (below), so its mark is not read. An FK onto the pk, an M2M whose through foreign key onto the target reads its pk, reverse FK and one-to-one relations and a `GenericForeignKey` keep the raw `<relation>` predicate.

**What is owner-dependent.** Only the first binding is stored, so an axis is owner-dependent when its answer differs between two owners of one filterset. There are three: the owners' table (a path re-entering the filterset's table, the same `_meta.concrete_model`, reads the type it is bound to), the own-PK GlobalID identity (`_is_own_pk_under_relay_owner` reads the owner's `origin`) and the visibility hook a `RelatedFilter` branch is scoped through (`_target_type_for_related_filter` reads the child filterset's bound owner). A relation key's target type is not one: it is `registry.get(target_model)` (primary-first) of the key's own relation, keyed on the target model, never on the owner.

**Multi-owner reuse — strict.** Only the first binding is stored, so a second owner may bind the same `FilterSet` only when nothing the shared filterset reads at query time would differ. The rule for second-owner binding (applied at finalizer phase-2.5 subpass 1 per [Decision 6](#decision-6--finalizer-phase-25-binding-seam--materialize-before-schema-ordering)):

- The owner's model must BE the filterset's `Meta.model` or derive from it (checked on every binding); a filterset declaring no `Meta.model` is refused on its first binding, so it is never shared.
- Both owners must read one table (the same `_meta.concrete_model`): a path re-entering the filterset's table reads the type it is bound to, so a multi-table-inheritance parent and child owner are refused in either declaration order, while owners over one model or its proxies share the table and are accepted.
- Both owners must agree on own-PK Relay identity: both non-Relay, or both Relay with the same `graphql_type_name`.
- Neither owner may carry a custom `get_queryset` (function identity is not visibility equivalence, so any custom hook fails closed).
- Any divergence raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming both owners. Consumers wanting per-owner surfaces declare two separate `FilterSet` subclasses (`PublicBookFilter` / `AdminBookFilter`); the input-class namespace is name-keyed, so the two generate two distinct input types per [Decision 9](#decision-9--input-class-namespace-vs-typeregistry-and-lifecycle). A subclass of a bound filterset (`class AdminBookFilter(BookFilter)`) is such a separate set: its binding is the class's own `_owner_definition` ([`utils/permissions.py::set_bound_definition`][utils-permissions], the one read the binder and every owner-dependent axis compose), never one inherited from its base, so each binds its own owner in either declaration order, and a subclass no `DjangoType` wires is unbound like any other unwired filterset. Re-binding the same `(filterset, definition)` pair is idempotent.

**Where the conditional runs.** The conditional runs on the `FilterSet`, NOT inside the factory: the factory only controls the Strawberry input shape, while `django-filter` instantiates the actual filter instances earlier via `filterset.BaseFilterSet.filter_for_field()` / `filter_for_lookup()`, which read `cls.FILTER_DEFAULTS`. A factory-only conditional would let the GraphQL input expose a GlobalID for `BookFilter.genres` while the runtime filter instance is whatever vanilla `django-filter` generated. The shape:

- `FilterSet.FILTER_DEFAULTS` is the dict shape `django-filter` consumes (`{django.db.models.Field: {"filter_class": ..., "extra": ...}}`), the package's own per-entry copy of `django-filter`'s `FILTER_FOR_DBFIELD_DEFAULTS`, taken at import, so a later mutation of that process-shared table never reaches it; an entry a consumer changed in it before the import is used as given but never counts as the package's default.
- `FilterSet.filter_for_field` / `FilterSet.filter_for_lookup` override the `django-filter` classmethods. For relation fields they read the related target `DjangoType` as `registry.get(target_model)` of the field upstream resolved (the terminal relation of a dotted key, so a leaf on a hop resolves on the hop's model, never by its bare name on the set's model; no owner binding is read), then check `implements_relay_node(target_type)` (the predicate [`finalize_django_types`][finalizer]'s phase 2.5 uses for Relay-Node injection) to pick the GlobalID branch vs the raw-key branch. For a non-relation field, `filter_for_lookup` returns `TextPatternFilter` (a `CharFilter`) for a text-pattern lookup listed explicitly on a non-text column when the package's own default filter governs the column (`FilterSet._origin_for_selection_class`): its operand is a string matched against the column's text rendering, so it is cleaned as text, never as the column's value. A `Meta.filter_overrides` selection is returned unchanged, and a column with no default filter keeps django-filter's unrecognized-field contract.
- The chosen primitive lands in the FilterSet's `declared_filters` / `base_filters` through the standard `django-filter` machinery; `get_filters()` (Layer 4) expands them with the cycle-safe guard.
- The factory derives the Strawberry input field type **from the resolved filter instances** via the named converter `convert_filter_to_input_annotation`, NOT from a parallel `FILTER_DEFAULTS` lookup.

**`convert_filter_to_input_annotation(filter_instance, model_field, owner_definition, filterset_cls)` contract.** Standard `django-filter` filters do not expose an `input_type` attribute; they expose Django form fields through `filter_instance.field`. The converter walks `inputs.py`'s `_FILTER_INPUT_KIND_TYPES` (most-specific first, shared with `normalize_input_value` so the two cannot disagree):

| `filter_instance` shape | Strawberry input annotation |
| --- | --- |
| `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter` / `GlobalIDFilter` | `list[str]` / `str` (the GlobalID wire form); runtime accepts `str` and `relay.GlobalID` objects; a declared one whose `field_name` names a relation Django joins no rows through (a `GenericForeignKey`; its reverse `GenericRelation` is accepted) is refused when its set publishes its filters, at schema build for a served set and at first use for an unbound one (its first construction or the package's first read of it; `ConfigurationError` naming the set, the filter and the field, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::refuse_identityless_relation`), unless it declares a `method`, which django-filter dispatches the value to in place of the relation |
| `RelationPkMultipleFilter` / `RelationPkFilter` | `list[<key scalar>]` / `<key scalar>`, typed from the column the predicate compares (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_compared_columns`): the target's key column for a generated leaf, the column a declared unmarked leaf compares: a forward `ForeignKey`'s `to_field` (`InterlibraryLoan.lender` publishes `Branch.isil`'s `String`), the link's column for a `ForeignObject` off the pk or a many-to-many through a `to_field`; a declared one on a relation linked by more than one column is refused at build (`ConfigurationError` naming the column count) |
| consumer `ModelMultipleChoiceFilter` / `ModelChoiceFilter` | the identity scalar of the column its form field cleans (a list for the multiple form): the `to_field_name` field, the primary key when unset or `"pk"` (Django's `to_field_name or "pk"`, `django_strawberry_framework/utils/relations.py::choice_key_field`), of the model it looks each value up in (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::model_choice_model`): a model `queryset`'s model; for a callable `queryset`, which names its model only once a request arrives, the model the `field_name` path Django walks over the set's model ends on (the last relation's target, the set's own model for a path crossing none, so `field_name="branch__name", to_field_name="name"` reads `Branch.name`; a path ending on Django's `pk` alias ends on the model the part before it reaches, as `<relation>__id` does, never on a multi-table-inheritance child's parent link); for an unset `queryset`, the target of a `field_name` ending on a relation (a `pk` path ends on the key's column, so it is refused). A `to_field_name` naming no column of that model (nothing, or a reverse relation), a callable `queryset` whose `field_name` is no path of fields on the set's model (a name naming no field, a queryset annotation, a `GenericForeignKey`, a set with no `Meta.model`) and an unset one whose `field_name` ends on no relation are refused at build (`ConfigurationError` naming the set and the filter as declared, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_model_choice_identity_annotation`). One whose `field_name` names a `GenericForeignKey` (on the set's model or past a relation, django-filter's CSV wrapper and a `Meta.filter_overrides` selection included), whatever its `queryset`, is refused before that, when its set publishes its filters (at schema build for a served set, at first use for an unbound one; `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::refuse_identityless_relation`, in the GlobalID filter's words), since Django filters no queryset through one; a `method` or a `filter` override of its own filters as it chooses and is not asked |
| `BaseCSVFilter` family (`in` / `range` declared through `Meta.fields`: `MembershipInFilter` in front of the `in` class django-filter builds, `IntegerInFilter` for an integer column's `in`, which drops each member the queryset's database cannot bind or the column's integer range there cannot hold (a fractional member names no row and is dropped, through an integer transform too) and, running none of the column's validators, matches a member they would refuse exactly as `exact` does, and for the `in` through an integer transform of a column (`<column>__year__in`, `iso_year`, `month`, an `ArrayField`'s `len`, a transform a project registers with an `IntegerField` output in a set a project resolver applies) binds each member as Django's lookup prepares it for the transform's `IntegerField` output (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_reads_integer_transform`, the walk Django's query runs, `django_strawberry_framework/utils/relations.py::lookup_operand`), never coerced through the column, so an integer transform's member past the integer range the queryset's database stores for that output is `FILTER_INVALID` as on a declared `in` over the transform and a year no row holds matches nothing, `IntegerRangeFilter`, whose `gte` + `lte` pair applies through the lookup's transform (`<column>__year__gte` + `<column>__year__lte`); an explicit `[]` on each `in` matches no rows) | `list[<element>]`; a declared or `Meta.filter_overrides` CSV filter applied to its column with no transform whose member form and lookup answer no value of the column (`class TimesInFilter(BaseInFilter, TimeFilter)` on a `DateTimeField`; a `DateRangeFilter` inside a CSV `in` or `range` on any column, whose own `filter` cannot look up a list; a `MultipleChoiceFilter` member, which reads no single value; a `ChoiceFilter` member whose own keys, none declared or disjoint ones, spell no value of a typed column) is refused at build, as the catch-all row says; the `range` wrapper's `BaseRangeField` and the `in` wrapper's `BaseCSVField` clean each member through the classes after them, so each is asked as its member is; a named queryset `ExtractYear` / `ExtractMonth` alias is a native `Transform` start whose `in` members are bind-asked, while a registered integer `Abs` transform is detected through the actual lookup walk and `Count` and `F` aliases retain their existing integer member/drop behavior |
| `MultipleChoiceFilter` family (django-filter's `MultipleChoiceFilter`, `TypedMultipleChoiceFilter`, `AllValuesMultipleFilter`, declared or selected by `Meta.filter_overrides`; last before the catch-all, so an `in` wrapper around one keeps the `BaseCSVFilter` row and a consumer class pairing one with a range, list, array or choice filter keeps that filter's row) | `list[<element>]`, the element typed from the model column as a `ListFilter`'s is (the column's enum on a choice column, its scalar otherwise, the form field's `str` for a `method=` filter with no column), never from `TypedMultipleChoiceFilter.coerce`, except where its own form types the value as the catch-all's does (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_form_cleaned_value_field`: over a `JSONField` or its key path the element is the `str` its `MultipleChoiceField` cleans); runtime hands the form the list, each enum member unwrapped to its value, and the filter's own `MultipleChoiceField` validates every value against its `choices` (a value off them is `FILTER_INVALID` `invalid_choice`; one over a choice column whose own choices accept none of the column enum's members is refused at build, as the `ChoiceFilter` row says). `conjoined`, `null_value`, `distinct` and the empty list constraining nothing are django-filter's. An `AllValuesFilter` / `AllValuesMultipleFilter` builds its choices from the model's default manager, as django-filter does, so a value held only by rows the type's `get_queryset` hides answers no rows while a value no row holds answers "Select a valid choice" (`invalid_choice`); subclass it with a `field` that reads `self.parent.queryset`, or declare its choices, when a hidden row must not be told apart from a missing one. An `AllValuesMultipleFilter(method=...)` builds no form field unbound: over a column it is typed from that column, as every `method=` filter keeps its column (its plain twin publishes the same list unless a `lookup_expr` transform types that one by the output; its form field, built per request, validates each value against the column's stored values before the method receives the cleaned list, so a value no row holds is `invalid_choice`), and with no column it is refused with the `method=` filter's [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] |
| `RangeFilter` (declared; the package's or django-filter's) | a Strawberry input dataclass `{start: T \| None, end: T \| None}`; runtime emits the start and end under the two form-data keys the filter's own widget reads: the widget of the form field the filter's own class builds, a `field` override included, read on a copy (`filters/base.py::copied_form_field`, through `filters/inputs.py::range_form_keys`) and asked for its keys (`utils/widgets.py::multi_widget_keys`): `<field>_0` / `<field>_1` for the package's `RangeFilter` (Django's `MultiWidget`), `<field>_min` / `<field>_max` for django-filter's `RangeFilter` (`RangeWidget`), `<field>_after` / `<field>_before` for its date and time families (`DateRangeWidget`), and a consumer widget's own `suffixes`; a widget that does not read exactly two keys, or a field the filter builds only inside a bound set, is refused when the schema is built with [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror]. Each bound of the package's `RangeFilter` that is not `null` is cleaned by the form field of the column's own `gte` filter (`filters/base.py::_column_form_field`; for a `field_name` ending on `pk`, the column the key stores, as in the typing rule below; where a transform stands in the `field_name` or the `lookup_expr`, its output's, the field the bound is published as, `filters/base.py::_bound_value_field`, while a `method=` filter keeps its column's), and a lone bound applies through every transform Django's query applies ahead of the lookup (`filters/base.py::_lookup_lhs_path`: `year__range` and a bare trailing `year`, Django's implicit `exact`, both as `year__gte` / `year__lte`; walked over the queryset the filter runs on, so a `FilteredRelation` alias or annotation path walks too, and a `lookup_expr` no walk from the column accepts raises `LookupValidationError` unapplied), so a bound the column cannot hold (a non-finite decimal, a number past django-filter's `1e50` cap) is `FILTER_INVALID` as it is on `gte`, a naive datetime bound is made aware and a text bound stripped; a comparison field and not `exact`, whose `ChoiceField` on a choice column would refuse a bound off the choices that the range compares like any other; a relation's lone bound under a lookup Django applies to the relation itself (`RangeFilter(field_name="shelf", lookup_expr="gte")`) is cleaned as the target column the relation compares on (its `to_field`, else the primary key), as `<relation>__<that column>` cleans it (`filters/base.py::_compared_value_column`), never by django-filter's own form field for a relation (`ModelChoiceField`), which would look each key up and tell a missing row from a hidden one, and a `range` over a relation, or a `method=` filter on one, cleans no bound, since Django registers no `range` lookup on a relation and a method applies no lookup of the filter's. The cleaning holds under any django-filter `FilterSet` parent, the package's or django-filter's own (a `DjangoFilterBackend` view's), where a refused bound is that form's error, which the backend answers with its 400. django-filter's `RangeFilter` keeps its own range field's cleaning (`RangeField`'s two plain `DecimalField` bounds, with no cap; a date or time family's own date or time fields), and its bounds reach the ORM as django-filter binds them, so over an integer column a two-bound `range` with a bound past the backend's integer range answers what Django answers there (on SQLite a masked error). Over an integer column with no transform between it and the lookup the package's `RangeFilter` applies the two bounds as `gte` + `lte` in one predicate, as the generated `range` does (`filters/base.py::IntegerRangeFilter`), so a bound past the column's range widens or matches nothing as it does on `gte`, never a raw `BETWEEN` the backend cannot bind; a `pk` whose key stores an integer column is one, a multi-table-inheritance parent link or a one-to-one primary key included, for which Django has no `range` lookup. otherwise each element that is not `null` is cleaned by the form field of the column's own `gte` filter (an `ArrayField`'s innermost `base_field`'s for an array column, `django_strawberry_framework/utils/_array_field.py::array_leaf_field`, a nested array's list or tuple members descended at any depth, so a flat list of its leaves or a deeper nest is cleaned alike; a relation's elements, where Django applies the lookup to the relation itself (`ListFilter(field_name="shelf", lookup_expr="in")`), are cleaned as the target column the relation compares on (its `to_field`, else the primary key), as `<relation>__<that column>` cleans it (`filters/base.py::_compared_value_column`: the target column Django's related lookups prepare the value through), never looked up as rows, which would tell a missing row from a hidden one, so an element the key cannot hold is that form's error where Django's related lookup would raise; The cleaning holds under any django-filter `FilterSet` parent, as the package's `RangeFilter` cleaning does, where a refused element is that form's error. Raw form data (a querystring through `DjangoFilterBackend`, a `QueryDict`) reads as the generated `in` reads it (`filters/base.py::_MembershipCSVWidget`): `?name=a,b` is the list `a`, `b`, an empty `?name=` applies no constraint, and only a list or tuple is an explicit `[]`. the column's own validators are not applied, so an element they would refuse still matches a row holding it; over a path the declaring set's model names no field for and the queryset handed to the set (`filter.parent.queryset`, read uncached per request by `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_bound_lookup_operand`, under any django-filter `FilterSet` parent) resolves (an annotation, a `FilteredRelation` alias and the columns past it: `ScalarSpecimenParentStampFilter.parent_lag_among` / `child_count_among`), each element is cleaned as that queryset compares it (the annotation's output field, the column past the alias, the alias's target key), an ordinary integer one's `in` dropping each element naming no row (a stored `GeneratedField` whose native `Col` exposes its output field included) and holding a decimal past its range; the elements stay `String`, a `method=` filter receives them as sent, and a set handed no queryset leaves the path to Django's `FieldError`; the lookup takes the list (`in`, `range` of exactly two members, a transform ending in either, `has_keys` / `has_any_keys`, a `JSONField`'s, `RangeField`'s or `CompositePrimaryKey`'s own comparisons, an `ArrayField` column's whole-array lookups; | not `required=True` | the chosen annotation wrapped in `T \| None` |

A leaf is typed by the value Django compares on the right-hand side of its lookup: the column's value for a lookup applied to the column, text for a text-pattern lookup, whatever the column, the kind the filter's form cleans over a `JSON`-scalar column that converts nothing it is handed (`django_strawberry_framework/types/converters.py::holds_values_as_given`), and the output of the transforms between the column and the lookup where there are any, generated or declared, in the `lookup_expr` or the `field_name` (`Meta.fields` `year` / `year__gt` / `year__in` on a datetime column are `Int` / `Int` / `[Int!]`, `date` a `Date`, a declared `DateTimeFilter(field_name="occurred_at", lookup_expr="date")` a `Date`, an `ArrayField`'s `len` an `Int`), read by `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_filterset_value_field` (the walk Django's query runs from the column, `django_strawberry_framework/utils/relations.py::lookup_operand`, the field the value is cleaned and bind-asked as, `filters/base.py::_bound_value_field`); a `method=` filter keeps its column. With no transform a declared filter or a `Meta.filter_overrides` selection keeps the column's type whatever its form cleans, and the build asks its own form and lookup whether any value of the column answers (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_refuse_form_serving_no_value`): each sample of the published scalar (one per stock scalar, `True` and `False` for `Boolean`), each member of the published choice enum or, on a column without `choices`, each key of the filter's own choice form when its choices are eager and its `validate` and `valid_value` are Django's or django-filter's (its empty and null keys included; a form declaring none answers no value; any other choice form is asked the scalar's samples), handed over as the arm hands a value (one value, two CSV members, the two range axes), is cleaned by the filter's own form field with its validators and `validate` off (the kind only, no query: a model-choice form field, which cleans by querying its queryset, is not asked) and applied by the filter's own `filter` to a fresh queryset of the declaring set's model, which prepares it through the column without SQL (`Lookup.get_prep_lookup`). A leaf no sample answers (the form refuses each, the column refuses what it cleans, or it cleans to no constraint) raises `ConfigurationError` naming the declaration, `Model.column`, the form field class and the lookup; one any sample answers builds (a `DateFilter` on a `DateTimeField`, a `CharFilter` on an integer). A column converting in its own `to_python` / `get_prep_value` (a relation through the keys it points at), and a lookup preparing in its own `get_prep_lookup` (each read by MRO for the module defining it) are not judged: the leaf builds; a lookup no field registers never reaches the probe, since its set refuses it when it publishes (the next paragraph). A leaf over a column whose `SCALAR_MAP` scalar is `JSON` and that converts nothing (a `JSONField`, or a subclass that overrides neither `to_python` nor `get_prep_value`) is typed by its form fields before any other rule, a `method=` filter's included: `CharFilter(field_name="payload__n")` publishes `String` and compares the text `"5"`, `CharFilter(field_name="payload")` and a `has_key` `CharFilter` `String`, a `NumberFilter` `Decimal` under a text lookup (`payload__n__icontains`), a `MultipleChoiceFilter` `[String!]` (only over such a column: past a converting transform a `MultipleChoiceFilter` keeps the output's scalar, `occurred_at__month` `[Int!]`); only a form passing every value through keeps `JSON` (a bare `Filter` or `TypedFilter`, which hands the column the JSON value as sent), and a subclass that converts (overrides `get_prep_value`) is typed by the column as any converting column is; every column registered under another scalar keeps it (a consumer column converting in `get_db_prep_value` registered as `int` stays `Int`), and one no entry covers stays refused. A package `ListFilter` / `RangeFilter` / `ArrayFilter` over such a column is typed by the comparison form it cleans each value with, the `Meta.filter_overrides` entry's where one covers the column (`filter_overrides={JSONField: {"filter_class": CharFilter}}` gives `[String!]` and `String` axes; with none each value passes through and keeps `JSON`, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_column_cleaned_scalar`). A null test is `Boolean` past a transform too (`year__isnull`, `date__isnull`): its operand is a value of no field, read off the terminal lookup that walk resolves beside that output (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_filterset_lookup_operand`), by its class (Django's `IsNull` or a subclass), so a consumer lookup registered as `isnull` on a transform's output takes the operand its declared filter's form cleans (in a set a project resolver applies; a connection-served set refuses it at build). A declared filter whose own form cleans its value (the catch-all row, a bare `TypedFilter`, each member of a `BaseCSVFilter`, each axis of a django-filter range filter, read off the `MultiValueField` axes that clean them, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_form_value_fields`) is typed by a converting output only where every such form field cleans the scalar django-filter's own filter for the output cleans, or a subclass of it, or passes any value through (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_passes_value_through`: Django's bare `forms.Field`, the field of a bare `Filter` or `TypedFilter`) (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_form_cleaned_value_field`, asking `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::comparison_form_field`): a declared `NumberFilter` on `occurred_at__year` stays `Int`, a bare `Filter` or `TypedFilter` with `lookup_expr="year"` or `field_name="occurred_on__year"` stays `Int` and a `DateTimeFilter` with `lookup_expr="date"` stays `Date`, while a `CharFilter` on either spelling publishes the `String` its form cleans, a `DateFilter(field_name="occurred_on", lookup_expr="year")` or a `TypedFilter` subclass whose `field_class` is a `DateField` the `Date` its form binds, a `class DatesInFilter(BaseInFilter, DateFilter)` on `year__in` `[Date!]` and a `DateFromToRangeFilter(field_name="occurred_on__year")` `Date` axes, since an `Int` would reach a form that cannot clean it; a django-filter range filter whose two axes clean different kinds is refused with `ConfigurationError` when the schema is built (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_range_axis_scalar`), since its range input carries one type for both bounds; a generated `year__in` is built from the output, so it stays `[Int!]`.
