The package's `RangeFilter` / `ListFilter` / `ArrayFilter` clean each value with the output's own comparison form field (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_ColumnCleanedFilter`), so they keep the output. The model field is the source of truth for the element type: the field the filter's `field_name` names on its FilterSet model (`filters/base.py::_filterset_model_field`), where Django's `pk` alias names the model's primary key at any segment, and a path ending on `pk` (`pk`, `<relation>__pk`) names the column that key stores, a relation-valued key (a multi-table-inheritance parent link, a one-to-one primary key) followed to the related key's column, so a declared filter over `pk` is typed, cleaned and range-checked as the same filter over that column (`Int` over an integer key, under a Relay owner too, as a declared filter over `id` is, so a malformed or out-of-range value is GraphQL's coercion error); a declared `field_name` that continues past its column through the column's transforms names no model field, a transform or key sharing a sibling column's name included (`BooleanFilter(field_name="payload__flag")` is a key of the `payload` JSON column, never the `flag` column, where django-filter's `get_field_parts` stays on the model past a non-relation part and answers that column; `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_model_resolved_field` keeps django-filter's answer only where its walk crossed a relation at every earlier part), so it is split as Django splits it (`Query.solve_lookup_type`, the column `django_strawberry_framework/utils/querysets.py::lookup_target_fields` names) and typed by the output field of the last transform, read with the filter's `lookup_expr`: `occurred_at__year` is `Int`, an `ArrayField` index (`counts__0`) its `base_field` and a slice (`counts__0_2`) the array, whose list filter then takes the array's elements below; over an `ArrayField` column only a list filter (`ArrayFilter` / `ListFilter`, or a `BaseCSVFilter`) whose lookup takes one array of the column's elements (`exact`, `contains`, `contained_by`, `overlap`) builds, each member typed from the array's `base_field` (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_array_element_column`, through `django_strawberry_framework/utils/_array_field.py::array_element_field`) as the read side converts it: an `ArrayFilter` over `ArrayField(IntegerField())` takes `[Int!]`, element `choices` give the enum shared with a `DjangoType` reading the column, the array's own `choices` are never read (the read side refuses such a column, so it stays off the type and the filter still filters it by element), and a nested array is refused with the read side's "Nested ArrayField on <Model>.<field> is not supported." (`django_strawberry_framework/types/converters.py::refuse_nested_array`); any other filter or lookup but the catch-all null test applied to an `ArrayField` column itself (a transform spelled in the `field_name`, `len`, an index or a slice, or a `len` in the `lookup_expr`, is typed from its output as above; an index or slice opening the `lookup_expr`, `NumberFilter(field_name="ints", lookup_expr="0")`, names no GraphQL input field and is refused with `ConfigurationError` at build, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_unnameable_lookup_refusal`) (a scalar filter, an `in`, a comparison, a text pattern, a `MultipleChoiceFilter`, a `ChoiceFilter`, whose enum would come from the array's own `choices`, a `RangeFilter`) is refused with `ConfigurationError` when the schema is built (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_array_column_refusal`), since no input type carries its operand, the advice naming the four `ArrayFilter` lookups above; the null test is the catch-all's: a `BooleanFilter` (`BooleanFilter(field_name="tags", lookup_expr="isnull")`, the filter django-filter generates for `isnull`) or any scalar filter naming `isnull` (`CharFilter(field_name="tags", lookup_expr="isnull")`, a `NumberFilter`) types it `Boolean` over the array as over any column, while a list, CSV, typed or choice filter testing `isnull` publishes the column's elements where the test takes one `Boolean`, so it is refused, its advice naming `BooleanFilter(lookup_expr="isnull")`; beyond the declared-form rule above, the form field types the leaf for a `method=` filter with no backing model field (so a CSV / list `method=` filter yields `list[str]` unless it is backed by a model field), read through the one form-field table the form-mutation converter reads too (`django_strawberry_framework/forms/converter.py::FORM_FIELD_SCALARS`, by the field class's real MRO: a `forms.JSONField` form publishes `JSON`, a consumer `field_class` subclassing a registered class that class's scalar) and falling back to `String` for a form field no row covers (a django-filter form cleans the text of a querystring, where the form-mutation converter refuses such a field) and for a path Django resolves to no column of the FilterSet model (a `FilteredRelation` alias only the queryset the filter runs on holds, `parent_day_of` over `par__occurred_at`). Every `field` read the build makes on a class-level declaration (the `method=` refusal's, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_refuse_method_without_form_field`, the widget-keys refusal's, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_refuse_widget_reading_other_keys`, and the `method=` fallback's, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_declared_form_field`, and an unbound projected child leaf's, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::ProjectedChildFilter.field`) runs on a deep copy of it (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::copied_form_field`), the copy django-filter makes per request: the filter class's own `field` builds it, a consumer override included, and the declaration every request copies keeps nothing. Django's own choice form fields (`ChoiceField`, `TypedChoiceField`, `MultipleChoiceField`, `TypedMultipleChoiceField`, which a `TypedChoiceFilter`, a `TypedMultipleChoiceFilter` and a consumer `field_class` build) normalize `choices` when they are built, forcing a `SimpleLazyObject` and running a `QuerySet`, so the copy (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::copied_filter`, which the no-value ask's `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_answers_sample` shares) hands such `choices` over in a subclass of Django's own `CallableChoiceIterator`, the lazy wrapper `normalize_choices` makes of callable choices and leaves unread, that also answers `len()` by iterating and stays true unread (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_UnreadChoices`): building the field evaluates nothing unless consumer code reads the choices, each request's own copy keeps the `choices` as declared. `filterset_cls` qualifies a nested `RangeFilter` sub-input class name, stemmed on the value path, the `field_name` followed by every transform Django's query applies ahead of the lookup, a bare trailing one included (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_filterset_lookup_lhs_path`; a path no query of the model resolves, an annotation or a `FilteredRelation` alias, is stemmed on its `field_name` and the `lookup_expr` parts ahead of the last) (`f"{filterset_cls.__name__}{Pascal(value_path)}RangeInputType"`), so two filtersets sharing a `field_name` mint two GraphQL type names and one set's `RangeFilter(field_name="occurred_at", lookup_expr="range")` (`DateTime` bounds, `<Set>OccurredAtRangeInputType`) and `RangeFilter(field_name="occurred_at", lookup_expr="year__range")` (`Int` bounds, `<Set>OccurredAtYearRangeInputType`, the name a bare `lookup_expr="year"` and `RangeFilter(field_name="occurred_at__year", lookup_expr="range")` spell too) mint two; a set/field split that still spells one name (`A` over `b_c` and `AB` over `c`) falls to the generated type-name claim (Edge cases). A choice enum a leaf mints first is prefixed with the owning type's published name, else, for a set no type owns, with the set's own class name (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_enum_prefix`), so two unowned sets over same-named choice columns of two models mint two names.

**A declared predicate Django resolves on no queryset is refused when its set publishes.** Beside the refusal of a GlobalID or model-choice filter on a relation no row identity is compared on (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::refuse_identityless_relation`), `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._publish_filters` asks every leaf it publishes whose `filter` is shipped code (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_runs_shipped_filter`: never a `method=` filter or a `filter` override) whether Django resolves its `field_name` and `lookup_expr` on any queryset (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::refuse_unresolvable_lookup`, a set a type serves when the schema builds, an unbound set at its first use). An unbound set's first use is its first construction (`FilterSet.__init__` publishes a set no snapshot holds yet, so a raw `FilterSet(data=...)`, `DjangoFilterBackend` and django-filter's `FilterView` publish it before django-filter copies `base_filters`) or the package's first read of it (`get_filters()`, `apply_sync` / `apply_async`), and a refused set never publishes, so every later construction asks again and raises again. The predicate is split as `Query.build_filter` splits it over a fresh `Query` of the model (`django_strawberry_framework/utils/querysets.py::split_lookup`) and the leftover walked from where the path ends (`django_strawberry_framework/utils/relations.py::lookup_transform_parts`): a path ending on a column refuses a leftover the column's walk refuses (`NumberFilter(field_name="signed_big__gt")`, `occurred_at__year__gt`, a `lookup_expr` no field registers such as `unaccent` without `django.contrib.postgres`, `parent__label__icontains`); a path ending on a relation, to-one or to-many, refuses a leftover the field Django's query asks it of refuses: the last join's field (`Query.names_to_path`'s final field: the relation itself, a reverse relation's `ForeignObjectRel`, a many-to-many's through key onto its target, so a many-to-many registers only the related lookups too), whose lookups every request applies whether or not a hop is walked (`FilterSet._leaf_chain` folds a to-one key into them and builds a to-many key's predicate as declared before the target's key answers it) (`parent` with `icontains` or `unknown`, a reverse one-to-one with `icontains`, `children`, a many-to-many either way or a `GenericRelation` with `icontains`, `startswith` or `unknown`); a filter applying the relation's `range` as `gte` / `lte` bounds (`IntegerRangeFilter`, django-filter's `RangeFilter`) is asked for `gte`, and the package `RangeFilter` judged on the column path it applies (`_ResolvedField.path`, so `RangeFilter(field_name="pk")` over a relation-valued key builds). The refusal of a relation's leftover also names the related column spelled past the relation (`field_name 'children__id'`, `'editions__isbn_13'` for a text key), whose own lookups Django answers alike walked or not, unless the relation compares more than one column or a column that is itself a relation. A lazy `field_name` or `lookup_expr` is read as the text it renders, as django-filter formats the argument. It raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] chained from the walk's `LookupValidationError`, naming the set, the filter class and name, the `field_name`, the `lookup_expr`, the refused part and the field with its class, with three remedies: spell the transforms and lookup the field registers, register a project lookup at startup (`Field.register_lookup` in `AppConfig.ready`), or read the value in `method=`. Nothing is kept between sets, so a registration in place when a set publishes is seen and one made after it is not. Not judged: a first name the model does not carry (a queryset annotation, a `FilteredRelation` alias: Django's `FieldError` at the split) and a name past a relation the related model lacks (a walked hop reads it on the rows its type's `get_queryset` shapes); a set declaring no `Meta.model` has no model to resolve a path on and is not asked either: a type refuses it when it binds, and applied unbound it refuses a leaf naming the missing `Meta.model` ([Error shapes](#error-shapes)). A raw `FilterSet(data=...)` keeps Django's `TypeError` for a model-choice `in` handed one row on a to-many key (a to-many key's walked hop builds the declared predicate first, so it raises what the unwalked request raises), whose text key a walked hop never iterates as characters.

**A list filter whose lookup compares one value is refused when its set publishes.** A `ListFilter` or `ArrayFilter` publishes a list and hands the whole list to its lookup, so right after `refuse_unresolvable_lookup` the same `FilterSet._publish_filters` ask (same leaves, same times) refuses one whose lookup takes no list (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::refuse_listless_lookup`). The lookup is the one Django's query resolves past the transforms (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_filterset_lookup_operand`), and it takes the list when Django's `get_db_prep_lookup_value_is_iterable` is true (`in`, `range`, a transform's or a relation's), when it is a `has_keys` / `has_any_keys` (`JSONField`'s `HasKeys`, `HStoreField`'s), or when it is one Django prepares through a `JSONField` (the list is one JSON array), a `RangeField` (two bounds) or a `CompositePrimaryKey` (one member per column: `TupleExact`, `TupleGreaterThan`; its `isnull` is refused) (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_takes_list`); django-filter's default `exact`, a comparison, a text pattern, `isnull` and a transform ending in one (`year`, an `ArrayField`'s `len` or index) are refused, a `Meta.filter_overrides` `ListFilter` for a column's `exact` or `gte` included. The [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] names the set, the filter class and name, the `field_name`, the `lookup_expr` and the Django lookup class and name it applies, with the remedies: `in`, `range` or a transform ending in either (`year__in`), `method=`, or listing only those lookups in `Meta.fields` for an override; over a relation it spells the related column past it (`field_name 'parent__id'`). Not judged: a `method=` filter (its method receives the list) and a `filter` override, a lookup no walk resolves, a lookup a project registers, a column whose field class is project or third-party code (it owns how it prepares a value, a list stored as text included), a value of an `ArrayField` column (the input build admits its four whole-array lookups by name) and a stock django-filter `FilterSet`, which publishes through no package table. A `range` list's form field refuses a non-empty list of other than two members with django-filter's `BaseRangeField` message and code (`Range query expects two values.`, `invalid_values`), `FILTER_INVALID` on the wire, so Django's `Range` never reads one or three (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_ElementField.validate`); `[]` keeps its meaning.

`normalize_input_value(filter_instance, raw_value, field_name=None)` is the runtime symmetric: it consumes the Strawberry-shaped input value and produces the form-data shape `django-filter`'s `BaseFilterSet.form.is_valid()` expects. `field_name` is the form-data key the caller resolved for this branch and supplies the `<field>` prefix in the multi-key shape below; its one call site, `sets.py::FilterSet._normalize_input`, passes the form key the lookup table holds for the lookup. It returns one of **three** shapes:

- a **scalar**, when the filter consumes a single form-data key — an Enum member normalizes to `enum.value` (a member of an `EnumChoiceFilter` / `EnumLookupFilter` input normalizes to `EnumMemberValue(enum.value)` instead); a `relay.GlobalID` object normalizes to its base64 **wire string**, re-encoded rather than pre-decoded to a bare `node_id`, so the bound `GlobalIDFilter` can validate the `type_name` before any decode;
- a **`list`**, for `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter` / `RelationPkMultipleFilter` / the `MultipleChoiceFilter` family / `ListFilter` / `ArrayFilter`, and for the `BaseCSVFilter` family `django-filter` generates from a `Meta.fields` `in` / `range` lookup. The `BaseCSVFilter` entry precedes the `RangeFilter` entry in `_FILTER_INPUT_KIND_TYPES`, so a `range` lookup declared through `Meta.fields` takes this **list** shape while a filter declared as `RangeFilter` takes the **dict** shape below — one lookup name, two normalized shapes, decided by how the filter was declared;
- a **`dict` patch the caller merges into the form-data dict**, when the filter consumes the two form-data keys of a range widget — the range dataclass normalizes to `{<start key>, <end key>}` (`{<field>_0, <field>_1}` for the package's `RangeFilter`, the `RangeFilter` row above for the rest), `<field>` being the `field_name` argument. `None`-valued axes are omitted, so a start-only range yields `{<start key>}`, an end-only range `{<end key>}`, and an all-`None` range `{}`. Form validation then cleans each present bound, with the `gte` form field of the value the range compares (the column's, or a transform output's) for the package's `RangeFilter` and with its own range field for django-filter's (the `RangeFilter` row above). The value is read as a mapping or input object carrying only `start` / `end`; anything else (a list, a scalar, an arbitrary object, or a mapping with another key) raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the filter, its form key and the value, instead of normalizing to a patch that constrains nothing.

**Strawberry-specific `GlobalIDFilter`.** The ported `GlobalIDFilter` / `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter` are NOT verbatim copies of `graphene_django/filter/filters/global_id_filter.py` — the graphene primitive parses a raw string and discards the decoded `type_name`, so a `GenreType` filter accepting `relay.GlobalID(type_name="LoanType", node_id="42")` would silently filter `Book.genres__id=42`. The Strawberry adaptation:

- Accepts both raw `str` and `strawberry.relay.GlobalID` objects: a `relay.GlobalID` short-circuits the parse; a string goes through `relay.GlobalID.from_id(value)`.
- Validates the decoded `type_name` against the accepted set for the target definition — the relation target the path Django walks for the filter reaches (its `field_name` and every relation its `lookup_expr` continues across, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_filterset_walked_lookup`, each segment read through `owner_definition.related_target_for`), or the owner itself for its own PK; the Phase 2.5 encode-only audit (`django_strawberry_framework/types/finalizer.py::_audit_globalid_filter_strategies`) resolves the target where the request decodes the leaf, through the request's own routing (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._globalid_decode_sites`) and the same resolver (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_globalid_target`) — which is strategy-aware per [`docs/SPECS/spec-031-globalid_encoding-0_0_9.md`][spec-031] Decision 13. A mismatch raises `GraphQLError("GlobalID type mismatch: filter expects <expected> but received <actual>")` before any queryset clause runs. A target on an encode-only (`callable` / `custom`) strategy is rejected at finalize ([Decision 6](#decision-6--finalizer-phase-25-binding-seam--materialize-before-schema-ordering) subpass 2.5), with a coded `GLOBALID_UNVALIDATABLE` reject at request time for a filterset no wired type serves.
- The form field cleans the wire string; the filter's `filter()` decodes it and applies the predicate with the decoded `node_id`, coerced against the target type's id slot (`django_strawberry_framework/relay.py::_node_id_slot`, the field `node(id:)` coerces against: the primary key, or the column a `relay.NodeID[...]` annotation names). A target whose id is its primary key keeps the filter's own `<field_name>` predicate (or the `__pk` mark above). A target whose id is another column compiles `<row path>__<id_attr>` (the bare `id_attr` on the owner's own id), the row path being the segments of the walked path that reach the target's rows (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_globalid_target`), so the id compares the column it carries on the own id, an own-id leaf through a hop, a forward, `to_field` or to-many key, and both `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter` arms. An unbound filter set (a `RelatedFilter` target no `DjangoType` wires, a subclass of a wired set included) resolves its relation leaves' target from its model's registry primary, the type those leaves are generated from, so a wrong-type id is a type mismatch as on a wired set; its own key (its own declaration, nested or as a hop's flat copy) keeps the upstream scalar shape and is never validated as that primary's id, while a leaf another set declares ending on its model's primary key past a declared hop into it (`shelf__id`) is a relation leaf: it validates against the type registered for the relation's target, as the relation's key does (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_key_leaf_of`). A hop into a model no `DjangoType` exposes does not end the walk, nor does a leaf walked over an undeclared hop into one: the target is the terminal relation's `registry.get` (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_resolve_globalid_target`), as generation types the leaf. Both reads wait for a stable registry (finalized, or every type collected for the finalizer's GlobalID audit, which resolves the same target and refuses an encode-only one at build); a filter set applied before finalization reads these leaves node-id-only. A node id that column cannot hold is `GLOBALID_INVALID` (`... is not a valid <Model> id value`; a primary-key slot keeps `... primary-key value`), and a valid one no row carries matches nothing, as `node(id:)` returns `null` for it. A `relay.NodeID` over an attribute no model column holds (one the type's `get_queryset` annotates) compiles only on the rows that hook returns: the type's own `id` key, a `RelatedFilter` hop into its filter set, nested or flat, a leaf ending on the type's primary key past an undeclared hop whose rows the type answers for or past a declared hop into a set no type wires, and a relation key into the type (`Printing.series`, `shelf__books`; forward or to-many, declared or generated), which routes as its trailing-pk twin does (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._routed_leaf_chain`): re-bound to the type's primary key over the visible rows the walk reaches, with no to-many pre-build and no stored-key fold, so it compares the annotation in the statement its twin runs. A relation key or a leaf ending on the type's primary key whose walk reaches rows another type answers for (`shelf__books` or `shelf__books__id` on a set bound to a secondary over the type's model, a declared last hop into a set such a secondary wires) answers `GLOBALID_UNVALIDATABLE`, and so does a relation key whose class overrides `filter`: consumer code that runs as declared, across the relation, on rows the hook never touched.
- `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter` validates every element of the list independently; a single wrong-type element rejects the whole input with the offending index named in the error.
- Rejects an input that does not decode to a usable identifier with the coded error `GraphQLError(..., extensions={"code": "GLOBALID_INVALID"})` — the uniform coded error the node-refetch path also emits (`relay.py::_decode_or_graphql_error`). Two well-formedness rejections share this code: (a) a **malformed wire string** (unparseable base64 / not a `type_name:node_id` payload), surfaced instead of leaking Strawberry's raw `GlobalIDValueError`; and (b) a **well-formed GlobalID whose decoded `node_id` is empty** (e.g. `to_base64("BookNode", "")`). Accepting an empty decoded id would silently widen a restrictive predicate (a scalar no-op, or a dropped list element turning `{in: ["type:"]}` into an unfiltered query). Both rejections apply to the scalar and the multiple filter; for the multiple filter the offending list index is named (the `index=` argument threaded through `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_decode_and_validate_global_id`). Only a `None` value (omission) and an explicit `[]` (match-none, as on every generated membership lookup) remain non-error paths. This is a general GlobalID input contract, independent of the row-preserving to-many predicate machinery ([`docs/GLOSSARY.md`][glossary], "Row-preserving to-many predicates (Part 1)") that shares the same decode boundary. The empty-decoded-`node_id` rejection is pinned live in [`examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_library_api.py`][fakeshop-test-library] by `test_library_genres_filter_empty_id_scalar_global_id_raises_globalid_invalid`, `test_library_genres_filter_empty_id_in_list_raises_globalid_invalid_at_index_0`, and `test_library_genres_filter_mixed_empty_id_in_list_rejects_whole_input_at_index_1`.

**Why this matters.** A blanket "FK/PK → `GlobalIDFilter`" mapping (mirroring `graphene-django`'s unconditional shape) would break non-Relay filters: a consumer filtering `Book.shelf` by ID against a non-Relay `ShelfType` would have to pass a GlobalID-encoded string even though the exposed GraphQL type uses an integer ID. `graphene-django`'s filter-connection defaults are unconditional because that path is Relay-shaped by construction; the package's default is non-Relay (`Meta.interfaces = (relay.Node,)` is opt-in), so the conditional shape matches the GraphQL surface the consumer sees.

**Name-collision note:** `graphene_django/filter/utils.py::get_filterset_class` and `django_graphene_filters/filterset_factories.py::get_filterset_class` are different upstream functions with the same name — the cookbook's is a `BaseFilterSet`-aware get-or-create helper, graphene-django's an explicit-vs-dynamic dispatcher pinned to connection-field declaration. The package ships neither: every filtered surface reads an explicit `Meta.filterset_class`.

Rationale companion — this Decision's justification and its two rejected alternatives: [Decision 4][rationale-d4].

### Decision 5 — `django-filter` as the foundation

The package's `FilterSet` **subclasses `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet`** and `django-filter` is a **hard dependency** at [`pyproject.toml #"django-filter>=25.2"`][pyproject]. The cookbook's `AdvancedFilterSet` is built on `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet` and reuses its load-bearing machinery (`declared_filters`, `base_filters`, `.filters`, `.form`, `.qs`, `get_filters()`, `Filter.method`, form cleaning, lookup generation); porting it without that inheritance would mean re-implementing all of those layers.

Pinned import shape (`django_filters.BaseFilterSet` does NOT exist at the top-level import path; the class lives at `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet`):

```python
from django_filters import filterset

class FilterSetMetaclass(filterset.FilterSetMetaclass):
    ...

class FilterSet(..., filterset.BaseFilterSet, metaclass=FilterSetMetaclass):
    ...
```

An implementer who reaches for `from django_filters import BaseFilterSet` gets an `ImportError` at module import — the cookbook's `from django_filters import filterset` + `filterset.BaseFilterSet` shape is the one that resolves. `FilterSet`'s other bases are the shared set-family mixins (`ClassBasedTypeNameMixin`, `ActiveInputPermissionMixin`).

Consumer migration shape: a consumer who has `class MyFilter(django_filters.FilterSet)` writes `class MyFilter(FilterSet)` and inherits the package's `RelatedFilter` / lazy-resolution surface. The dependency is hard, so no `ImportWarning` branch exists on the filter path.

Rationale companion — this Decision's justification and its three rejected alternatives: [Decision 5][rationale-d5].

### Decision 6 — Finalizer phase-2.5 binding seam + materialize-before-`Schema` ordering

The filter subsystem's lazy-resolution pipeline runs inside [`django_strawberry_framework/types/finalizer.py::finalize_django_types`][finalizer]'s phase 2.5, after `apply_interfaces` / `install_relay_node_resolvers` and before phase 3's `strawberry.type` decoration. [`types/finalizer.py::_bind_filtersets`][finalizer] and the order side's `_bind_ordersets` both configure one shared driver, `_bind_sidecar_sets`, through a `_SidecarBindingSpec`, so the subpass order is single-sited across the two families.

**Ordered subpasses.** A single per-`DjangoType` loop that binds an owner and then calls `get_filters()` is order-dependent: if `BookFilter.get_filters()` expands `RelatedFilter("GenreFilter")` before the loop reaches `GenreType`, `GenreFilter._owner_definition` is still unset, so that expansion builds `GenreFilter`'s own-PK key in the upstream shape an unbound set keeps instead of its Relay owner's GlobalID (a relation key reads no binding either way). The pass therefore separates owner binding from expansion, and each subpass completes across ALL wired `DjangoType`s (definitions not yet finalized) before the next begins:

**Subpass 1 — Collect and bind every owner.** For every `definition` whose `definition.filterset_class is not None`:

1. Check the owner's model: a filterset declaring no `Meta.model` raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the filterset, the owner type and the owner's model, with the remedy (declare `Meta.model = <owner model>`, with `Meta.fields = []` to publish only the declared filters), since every filter of a served set is typed, validated and applied against that model ([`types/finalizer.py::_bind_filterset_owner`][finalizer]); otherwise `definition.model` must BE the filterset's `Meta.model` or derive from it, else [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] names the owner type, owner model, filterset and filterset model.
2. **Bind the owner.** Set `filterset_class._owner_definition = definition`. If the filterset's own binding (`utils/permissions.py::set_bound_definition`, never one inherited from a base filterset) is already a different definition, apply the strict multi-owner check from [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor) (the owners' table, own-PK Relay identity, custom `get_queryset`); a mismatch raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming both owners. The owner slot keeps the FIRST binding; re-binding the same pair is idempotent.

No `get_filters()` call happens in subpass 1.

**Subpass 2 — Expand every filterset.** For every filterset bound in subpass 1, read `filterset_cls._filter_table()` (`types/finalizer.py::_expand_filterset`), which triggers Layer-4 expansion and publishes the set's filter table. With every wired filterset bound, `filter_for_field` / `filter_for_lookup` consult the owner-scoped lookup regardless of expansion order (a filterset no `DjangoType` wires, a subclass of a wired one included, keeps the registry fallback). An `ImportError` from an unresolved lazy target rewraps as [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the filterset (the `ImportError` on `__cause__`); any other exception, a [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] included, propagates unchanged.

**Subpass 2.5 — Filter-only audits.** [`types/finalizer.py::_audit_filterset_subpass_2_5`][finalizer] runs four audits over the wired filtersets, in this order:

1. **Deep-copy audit** ([`types/finalizer.py::_audit_filter_copies`][finalizer]). Every set a wired filterset serves (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_served_reach`: the wired set, and each set it reaches through a `RelatedFilter`, which a nested branch or a projected leaf builds an instance of) has each filter of its published table (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._filter_table`, the table every request copies: what `get_filters()` made of the expansion, a consumer override's additions, borrowings, renames and in-place edits included, its `RelatedFilter` heads and flat copies too) copied once through `filters/base.py::copied_declared_filter`, before input building or any request copies one. django-filter deep-copies every filter into each `FilterSet` instance, so a filter a deep copy refuses (a bound `method=` over an object holding a lock or a Django cache handler, a `functools.partial` over one, a lock in `extra`, a request-aware `queryset=` callable bound to one) could serve no request; the first one raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the filterset, the key it is published under, the filter's class and, for a set the audit reaches through a `RelatedFilter`, the attributes it was reached through (a filter an expansion copies first names the one declaration it copied through), with the copy's error on `__cause__`, and advises the two `method=` spellings a copy keeps (a FilterSet method's name, a plain function) when the filter's `method` itself refuses the copy, else keeping the object off the filter. A lock on a service's class, a logger and a widget whose `__deepcopy__` is shallow all copy and pass. A filter the class-creation `base_filters` holds but the published table does not (an override dropping it once the surface is expanded) is copied by no request and is not asked. A surface a child contributes along a path it reaches back into publishes no table of its own; the expansion copy refuses its filter (Layer 4 of [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline)). A filterset nothing the schema serves reaches is never asked.
2. **Registered-`RelatedFilter`-target audit** (`_audit_unregistered_related_filter_targets`). Every reachable `RelatedFilter` target must resolve to a registered `DjangoType`. A related branch's visibility scoping runs the target type's `get_queryset` (step 3 of [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation)), so an unregistered target makes the branch unfulfillable even though its input field would be materialized. The walk is transitive with a visited set — cyclic cross-references (`BookFilter` <-> `ShelfFilter`) are legal — and raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the filterset and the field. The runtime sibling of this error lives in `FilterSet._branch_visibility_seed`, the one resolution an active nested branch and every flat leaf walking the branch share.
3. **Encode-only-GlobalID-target rejection** (`_audit_globalid_filter_strategies`). Every `GlobalIDFilter` / `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter` a wired filterset serves — its own and those of every set it reaches through a `RelatedFilter`, wired or not (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_served_reach`, the reach the visibility-seal audit reads) — resolves the target definition a request decodes it against: the request's own routing binds it where it runs (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._globalid_decode_sites`: over a declared hop's target set, an undeclared hop's face, or the set itself, a to-many key's declared predicate included) and the runtime resolver reads the definition there (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_globalid_target`), the owner itself for the own-PK branch (on an undeclared hop's face, the type registered for the face's model, as the unwalked relation's leaf reads it, whichever type the face's rows answer for), the relation target otherwise; a to-many key's copy over the target rows, and the copy of a leaf ending on the target's primary key past a declared hop into a set no type wires, read their leaf's (`filters/base.py::_key_leaf_of`). A leaf whose value consumer code reads (a `method=` or a class-level `filter` override: `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_decodes_before_binding`) is skipped, and a projected copy is asked in its child set. When that target's effective GlobalID strategy is `callable` or `custom`, the strategy is **encode-only**: there is no decode path, so a typed filter input could never validly consume the IDs the target emits, and the audit raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the wired filterset and the request key, and for a reached leaf its declaration and the `RelatedFilter` path; refusing the schema also removes the `null` and `[]` values such a leaf answers without decoding.

4. **Served-leaf visibility-seal audit** (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_refuse_unsealable_served_leaves`). A connection (a `DjangoConnectionField`, or the `<relation>Connection` a Relay type grows for a to-many relation) runs its `filter:` argument inside the visibility boundary, whose seal admits only Django's own node classes (`django_strawberry_framework/utils/querysets.py::_type_is_genuinely_django`, spec-045 Decision 2), so a leaf whose path resolves to any other transform or lookup class (a project `Transform`, `Lookup` or `Func`-based transform, a subclass of a Django class, a third-party app's lookup; registered on a field class, on one field instance or on a transform) would be published and refused on every request. Every wired filterset a connection serves (`_connection_served_filtersets`, recorded by `django_strawberry_framework/connection.py::_synthesized_signature` for a root connection and for every nested `<relation>Connection` the finalizer synthesizes before phase 2.5; a connection declared once the registry is finalized is checked there, where it is declared), and every set it reaches through a `RelatedFilter`, through a branch the set's published filter table still holds (the declared `RelatedFilter` itself or one held under its declared name), or a flat copy the schema publishes (each copy's child set, unless `HIDE_FLAT_FILTERS` hides the copy, read through the input builder's own rule `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_is_hidden_flat_copy`), so a `get_filters` override dropping the branch and its copies serves no request through it (each such set's own filter table, its expansion copies skipped, so a leaf is judged once on the set declaring it, under that set's `filter`, and named with the `RelatedFilter` path it was reached through, whatever flat copies a parent's `get_filters` keeps), has each leaf's path walked as Django's query walks it (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_filterset_lookup_operand`: `get_transform` / `get_lookup` from the column, a reverse relation key from the relation) and every class the walk resolves asked of the seal's own predicate; the first that is not Django's own raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the declaring set, the filter class, the path, the lookup and the class, with the remedies: a Django class registered under any lookup name, a set no connection serves applied from a project resolver (`FilterInput[...]` and `apply_sync`, never sealed), or `Meta.relation_shapes = {"<relation>": "list"}` on the type whose to-many relation grows the connection. A set a project resolver applies that a connection also serves is refused like any served set: its input type is shared. Generated leaves, declared leaves, `Meta.filter_overrides` selections, `Meta.fields` relation paths, `in` / `range` / `isnull` leaves and relation keys are all asked (a `RelatedFilter` expansion copy is asked as its child leaf, on the child set); a `method=` filter and a `filter` override (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_runs_shipped_filter`) own their query and a queryset annotation has no walk from the model, so the seal judges those per request; a predicate Django resolves on no queryset never reaches the audit, since its set refused it when it published ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor), `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::refuse_unresolvable_lookup`).

The deep-copy audit runs first, so a filter no request can copy is refused before any other audit asks it anything. The related-target audit runs before the GlobalID audit: an unregistered relation target makes the relation head unresolvable, so ordering it first keeps the GlobalID audit operating on a validated relation graph. All four are filter-family audits with no order-family twin.

**Subpass 3 — Orphan helper-reference validation.** Compare `_helper_referenced_filtersets` (every `FilterSet` passed to `FilterInput[...]` / `filter_input_type(...)` per [Decision 11](#decision-11--filter_input_typefilterset-consumer-helper)) against the filtersets bound in subpass 1. One orphan raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] with the message `"FilterSet '<Name>' is referenced via FilterInput[...] / filter_input_type(...) but never assigned to a DjangoType via Meta.filterset_class. Add 'filterset_class = <Name>' to the relevant DjangoType's Meta."`; several orphans are reported in one message, sorted by qualified class name.

**Subpass 4 — Materialize input classes.** For every wired filterset, read `FilterArgumentsFactory(filterset_cls).arguments` to trigger the Layer-5 BFS, then call `materialize_input_class(name, cls)` for every class in the factory's `input_object_types` ledger, which sets the class as a module global of [`django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs`][filters] and records `name → input class` in `_materialized_names` (per [Decision 9](#decision-9--input-class-namespace-vs-typeregistry-and-lifecycle)). `.arguments` is read for its side effect: it returns the **root** filterset's own input class ([`utils/inputs.py::GeneratedInputArgumentsFactory.arguments`][utils-inputs]), while the loop iterates the whole ledger, so every class built for a reachable `RelatedFilter` target is materialized too — and a sibling root's factory sees those builds through the class-level cache.

**Subpass 3 runs BEFORE subpass 4, and that order is load-bearing.** Orphan validation can only fail; materialization writes module globals and ledger rows. Validating first means an orphan failure leaves no partial state in `_materialized_names` or the factory's built-class ledger, so a re-run after fixing the orphan does not meet stale entries.

By phase 3, every referenced filter input class is a module global of `django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs`, so by `strawberry.Schema(...)` time every `filter:` annotation on a root resolver resolves through `LazyType.resolve_type`.

Materialization MUST land before `strawberry.Schema(...)` runs. Injecting a `filter:` argument into an already-decorated `@strawberry.field` resolver is not a real path: Strawberry collects field arguments at `@strawberry.type` decoration time, before `finalize_django_types()` runs. The ordering is: resolvers annotate `filter:` with the helper (a string forward reference) at module-load time → `@strawberry.type` collects the lazy annotation → `finalize_django_types()` materializes the input class → `strawberry.Schema(query=Query, ...)` resolves the lazy reference → schema build succeeds. [`docs/README.md`][docs-readme]'s schema setup section pins `finalize_django_types()` before `strawberry.Schema(...)`.

Rationale companion — this Decision's justification and its four rejected alternatives: [Decision 6][rationale-d6].

### Decision 7 — `Meta.filterset_class` promotion gate

`Meta.filterset_class` is in `ALLOWED_META_KEYS` (out of [`DEFERRED_META_KEYS`][base]) because the subsystem applies it end-to-end: the class hierarchy (Slices 1 + 2) and the finalizer-phase-2.5 binding (Slice 3) exist, and the validator-acceptance test (`tests/types/test_base.py::test_meta_filterset_class_is_allowed_not_deferred`) pins the promotion. Same gate as [`Meta.interfaces`][glossary-metainterfaces] — a deferred `Meta` key is accepted only when its subsystem applies it end-to-end.

Rationale companion — this Decision's justification and its two rejected alternatives: [Decision 7][rationale-d7].

### Decision 8 — Relation-permission cascade + `get_queryset` cooperation

A `FilterSet`'s `check_<field>_permission(request)` gates compose with the target type's [`get_queryset`][glossary-get_queryset-visibility-hook] hook and with `FilterSet._apply_related_constraints` (which applies the explicit `queryset=` parameter on a related filter — a **scope boundary** that nested filters, and flat filters walking the branch, can narrow but cannot escape; it bounds only the paths whose walk crosses that declaration (step 3), and it is not a visibility gate, because it constrains parent rows only while a predicate through the branch is active, so hiding rows is the job of `get_queryset`). The composition flows through the resolver-facing classmethods `FilterSet.apply_sync(input_value, queryset, info)` and `FilterSet.apply_async(input_value, queryset, info)`. Resolvers pick the variant matching their own sync/async shape. `FilterSet.apply(...)` is a thin dispatcher that runs `apply_sync` and translates the typed [`SyncMisuseError`][querysets] — raised when a target's `get_queryset` turns out to be async-only — into a `RuntimeError` that tells the consumer to use `apply_async`.

**`GraphQLError` import path.** Every `GraphQLError` raised in this Decision and in [Decision 11](#decision-11--filter_input_typefilterset-consumer-helper) is `from graphql import GraphQLError` (the class Strawberry's response builder honors for the `extensions` payload), NOT `strawberry.exceptions.StrawberryGraphQLError`.

**Internal decomposition.** `apply_sync` / `apply_async` decompose into named helpers so each seam is testable:

- `_derive_related_visibility_querysets_sync(input_value, info, *, hop_visibility, parent_db=None)` / `_derive_related_visibility_querysets_async(...)` — for every ACTIVE `RelatedFilter` branch, read the target type's visibility-scoped queryset from the request-wide hop map `hop_visibility` (`FilterSet._declared_hop_rows` / `FilterSet._await_declared_hop_rows`, the one derivation of a declared hop's rows, which a flat leaf walking the branch reads too: on the hop's first use in the request it runs the target type's `get_queryset` through [`django_strawberry_framework/utils/querysets.py::apply_type_visibility_sync`][querysets] / [`::apply_type_visibility_async`][querysets], the package-wide visibility-hook dispatch, and stores the rows), then narrow those rows by the child filterset's `apply_*`, which receives the same map (`_hop_visibility`). The parent queryset's database alias is threaded through so each child hook sees the parent's shard.
- `_derive_flat_hop_visibility_async(input_value, info, into, *, parent_db)` — the flat-leaf twin of the async derivation: for every flat leaf of one input level the form data names (`FilterSet._named_leaf_filters`: read through `filters/inputs.py::filter_lookup_table`, the one input-leaf to filter mapping, a leaf whose normalized value writes at least one form-data key, the key-based reading of a supplied leaf `FilterSet._leaf_absent` and `filters/sets.py::_named_flat_copies` share; a range naming no bound writes none), awaits the target visibility of every hop the leaf walks into the request-wide hop map `into`: a declared hop keyed by `(id(<RelatedFilter declaration>), <database alias>)` (`FilterSet._await_declared_hop_rows`, the key a nested branch's derivation fills too, so a hop a nested branch already awaited is not awaited again), an undeclared hop and each intermediate model of a multi-relation declaration (`FilterSet._branch_via_types`, for the leaf's hops and for every active nested branch of the level) keyed by `(<scoping DjangoType>, <database alias>)` (`FilterSet._await_scoped_rows`). The hop-awaiting loop is `FilterSet._await_hop_visibility_async(hops, info, into, *, parent_db)`, which `apply_async` also runs over the hops of every absent walking leaf whose filter acts on its absent value (`FilterSet._probe_absent_relation_leaves`); same keys, a key already in the map awaited once.
- `_normalize_input(input_value) -> dict` — Strawberry input dataclass → `django-filter` form-data dict; reads each lookup's form key from `filters/inputs.py::filter_lookup_table` and converts each value through `normalize_input_value(filter_instance, raw_value, field_name)` per [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor).
- `_branch_visibility_seed(field_name, related_filter, parent_db)` — the one resolution of a declared branch's target type (`FilterSet._target_type_for_related_filter`), child filterset and unscoped base rows (`utils/querysets.py::base_queryset(child_model, using=parent_db)`), read by the first derivation of the branch's hop rows (`FilterSet._declared_hop_rows` / `FilterSet._await_declared_hop_rows`), which the nested branch and every flat leaf walking the branch (`_hop_visible_rows`) share; a target that does not resolve raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the branch as "active in the filter input (nested, or walked by a flat leaf)".
- `_apply_related_constraints(input_value, parent_qs, child_qs_by_branch) -> QuerySet` — applies explicit `RelatedFilter(queryset=...)` constraints under active-branch scoping, intersected with the visibility-scoped child querysets, through `filters/sets.py::_restrict_through_branch`: `filters/sets.py::_admitted_rows` keeps the branch's child rows among the rows its explicit `queryset=` holds, read as an `IN` subquery of that queryset's keys and never combined with Django's `&` (which refuses a sliced, `distinct()` or combined side and two sides that disagree on `distinct()`), so a nested branch under a sliced, `distinct()` or `union()` `queryset=`, or over child rows a `distinct=True` filter or a `distinct()` `get_queryset` reads, answers the visible rows the queryset holds (the queryset's model must be the target filterset's own, else `ConfigurationError`; one pinned by `.using` to an alias the request does not read is refused as Django refuses a subquery across databases), and `filters/sets.py::_restrict_to_related` keeps the parents reaching one of them through `optimizer/predicates.py::related_rows_exist`, the one correlated `EXISTS` every declared branch restricts through (a flat leaf walking the branch reaches the same two helpers through `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._reaches_hop`). **This helper runs BEFORE the FilterSet is instantiated** so the constrained queryset becomes the instance's stored `self.queryset` and propagates to `.qs`; `django-filter`'s `.qs` reads `self.queryset`, not any local the caller might mutate afterwards.
- `_run_permission_checks(input_value, request)` — runs the per-field `check_<field>_permission(request)` gates ONLY for fields present in the input; recurses through active `RelatedFilter` branches and the `and` / `or` / `not` arms into child filtersets.
- `_validate_form_or_raise(filterset)` — explicit `filterset.form.is_valid()`, which reads the refusals the form's own clean adds (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/form_binding.py::BindAskingFilterForm`: the unstorable-text refusal and a widget's refusal of the value it reads before any field cleans, the refusal of a temporal, duration or decimal value the queryset's database cannot bind after), the same asks run here for a form a consumer `form` override builds, and the `GraphQLError` raise.

`_apply_common_prelude` (normalize, request, constraints) is shared by both colors. Sync apply uses `_apply_common_finalize` (build, permission pass, form validation, absent-leaf probe, lazy `.qs`). Async apply stages each root or logical-arm instance after its permission pass in a sync worker, discovers necessary source dependencies on the actual form, awaits missing async hooks outside the worker, and validates that same form once in a resumed worker. It then probes absent walking leaves and reads `.qs` in a worker. A nested `RelatedFilter` child runs the same async pipeline on its own instance.

**The pipeline**, as both `apply_sync` and `apply_async` run it (the async color may stage, await and resume a known initial annotation owner before field cleaning, and may await an absent walking leaf after validation):

1. **The consumer resolver calls `<OwnerType>.get_queryset(queryset, info)` first** on the consumer-shaped root queryset — visibility scoping happens BEFORE any filter clause runs: `queryset = BranchType.get_queryset(models.Branch.objects.order_by("id"), info)` THEN `queryset = BranchFilter.apply_sync(filter, queryset, info)` (or `await BranchFilter.apply_async(...)` from an async resolver). Skipping the `get_queryset` call lets the filter clause see through the visibility gate to hidden rows.
2. The consumer resolver then calls `FilterSet.apply_sync(input_value, queryset, info)` or `await FilterSet.apply_async(input_value, queryset, info)`.
3. **Nested `RelatedFilter` visibility scoping.** For each ACTIVE `RelatedFilter` branch (its key is present in the input), the pipeline derives the child visibility queryset by calling the **target type's `get_queryset(child_model._default_manager.all(), info)`**, where the target type is the child filterset's bound owner (`FilterSet._target_type_for_related_filter`; `registry.get(child_model)` when unbound). It runs first, before normalization, so the recursion-depth cap is checked at the earliest point. Both colors derive every `and` / `or` / `not` arm's related branches here too, in tree order (`FilterSet._iter_logic_arms`, read by `FilterSet._collect_nested_visibility_querysets_sync` and `FilterSet._collect_nested_visibility_querysets_async`), before the permission pass (step 5) and form validation (step 6), so an input holding an arm branch error beside a form error or a permission denial answers the same first error on both. The child visibility queryset is what the parent's related constraint uses as its right-hand side, so a nested filter cannot use a hidden related row to make the parent match: without this step, `{ branches(filter: { shelves: { code: { exact: "secret-code" } } }) { id } }` would match `Branch` rows owning a `Shelf` that `ShelfType.get_queryset` hides. **A flat leaf walking a declared branch is scoped the same way.** A flat leaf whose ORM path walks one or more declared `RelatedFilter` hops (`FilterSet._flat_leaf_walk`; the path is the one Django walks for the leaf, its `field_name` and every relation its `lookup_expr` continues across, `ListFilter(field_name="shelf", lookup_expr="tier__in")` walking `shelf__tier`, read at each use through `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_filterset_walked_lookup`, never written back onto the filter), into a target column or onto the last hop's own key, answers as Django's ORM answers the same lookup in a world where every row a hop's target type hides, and every row a hop's explicit `queryset=` excludes, does not exist. A positive lookup keeps a parent with a visible chain whose terminal matches, which is its nested twin's answer: `shelvesCode` as `shelves: { code }`, `bookShelfTopic` as `book: { shelf: { topic } }`. The hops are `utils/permissions.py::walk_declared_relation_path`'s, the one reading of which declared branches a flat path walks, which the flat-path permission gates fire along too: each hop is the declaration whose `field_name` matches the next path segments (the longest multi-segment match wins), and the declared walk stops at the first segment no declaration claims or after a hop whose target does not resolve. The filter reads the walk with `undeclared=True`: past the last declared hop every further relation segment is an undeclared hop (`utils/permissions.py::UndeclaredHop`), whose rows answer for `utils/querysets.py::relation_target_type` of its model (the type the walking set is bound to when the path re-enters that set's table, `registry.get` otherwise; one table is one `_meta.concrete_model`, so a proxy shares its concrete model's table while a multi-table-inheritance parent and child do not) and are scoped by that type's `get_queryset` when it declares one of its own (`UndeclaredHop.scope`); the undeclared hops are `utils/relations.py::leading_relation_hops`'s, `classify_path`'s hops over the longest leading part of the rest of the path, so each reaches the model Django's join reaches (a `pk` segment on a model keyed by a one-to-one or a parent link is that relation), and the unconsumed segments are evaluated on the deepest target, by a per-request copy of the leaf bound as a filter of that target's set (the last declared hop's target set instance, or the face an undeclared hop's rows offer, `filters/sets.py::_UndeclaredHopSet`): its `parent`, its `model` and its rebound `field_name` all name that target (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._bound_terminal`), so a filter reading `self.model` or `self.field` while it filters (django-filter's `AllValuesMultipleFilter`) resolves its path there, whether it is a flat copy or the set's own leaf over a relation path. A `RelatedFilter` whose `field_name` is not a path of relations (a column such as `title__shelf`, a missing name, a `GenericForeignKey`) raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] when its filter set's expansion builds (`filters/sets.py::_require_relation_path`, reading the parent model only, so a lazily resolved target is never looked up). So `altShelves` (`BranchFilter` declares no branch over `alt_shelves`), `branchAltShelves` / `branch: { altShelves }` on shelves and `itemsName` on categories answer over the shelves, and items, their types let the viewer see. A declaration's explicit `queryset=` bounds exactly the paths whose walk crosses that declaration: its nested branch, its flat copies, a `Meta.fields` key or relation-key leaf walking it, a raw head, and a path entering it through an outer declared branch. A path reaching the same rows another way crosses no declaration and answers for its hops' target types alone: another set's dotted key (`branch__shelves__topic` on a `Shelf` set declaring no `branch`) matches through a shelf outside `BranchFilter.shelves`' `queryset=`, as a different relation to the same model (`alt_shelves__topic`) and the same relation reached by another route (`alt_shelves__branch__shelves__topic`) do; rows no route may reach are hidden by the target type's `get_queryset`. A leaf none of whose hops is declared or scoped (every relation reaches an unregistered model or a type keeping the identity hook) is not walked: it compiles as Django's raw relation, routed where it qualifies. An undeclared hop has no branch gate: the gates stay `relation_path_gates`'s, the last declared target's gate covering the whole remainder. At every hop the target type's `get_queryset` over `base_queryset(<target model>, using=<parent alias>)` (`FilterSet._hop_visible_rows`; an unscoped undeclared hop reads every row through the model's `_base_manager`, as Django's join does) and a declared hop's explicit `queryset=` apply, and a declared hop whose `field_name` spans several relations (`CardFilter.milestone` over `target_version__milestone`) passes only through the visible rows of each intermediate model (`FilterSet._via_rows`, the `via` rows of `related_rows_exist`), nested and flat alike; the leaf runs over the last hop's visible rows as a copy with `field_name` rebased onto the target model, the target filter set as its `parent` (a leaf ending on the target's primary key past a declared hop into a set no type wires validates what its leaf as declared validates, `filters/base.py::_key_leaf_of`) and the target model as its `model` (for an undeclared last hop, `filters/sets.py::_UndeclaredHopSet`, which carries the hop's model, whose registered type a `GlobalID` value validates against as the unwalked relation's leaf does (a to-many key's copy over the target rows validates what its leaf as declared validates, `filters/base.py::_key_leaf_of`), the definition of the type its rows answer for, the request and the walking set, whose request info names the schema), and each hop folds outward through `FilterSet._reaches_hop` (the hop's `_admitted_rows`, then `optimizer/predicates.py::related_rows_exist`) onto the previous hop's visible rows, then onto the parent (`FilterSet._apply_relation_leaf`): one correlated `EXISTS` per hop, built from the visible rows, never re-reading the parent table or embedding another leaf's restriction, with no framework `DISTINCT`. An `isnull=True` lookup also keeps a parent with no visible related row at some hop (Django's `LEFT JOIN` null extension over the visible rows: a `NULL` key, an absent row and a hidden row alike), where the nested twin stays existential (`shelf: { topic: { isNull: true } }` needs a visible shelf with no topic); `isnull=False` stays positive. A declared `exclude=True` filter keeps the parents its positive twin (the same filter with `exclude` off) does not keep, negated at the outermost parent, as a `not:` arm around the positive twin does, including through an expansion or a projection (`GenreFilter.without_book_titled`, projected as `genresWithoutBookTitled` on books and `bookGenresWithoutBookTitled` on loans, matches a book with no visible genre); the negation is `NOT EXISTS`, which a `NULL` link column cannot empty the way it empties a `NOT IN`. A filter holding a `filter` of its own (`filters/sets.py::_own_filter`: a consumer `method=`, or a callable stored on the instance) stays positive whatever its `exclude`, which its own `filter` applies (negating it again would double the negation); its `isnull=True` reading still null-extends. Flat and nested twins therefore agree on every lookup except `isnull=True` and `exclude=True`. A relation-key leaf (nothing after the last declared relation: a `Meta.fields` key over a relation a renamed declaration claims, such as `shelves` beside `visible_shelves = RelatedFilter(ShelfFilter, field_name="shelves")`, or a declared model-choice filter over a declared relation, such as `ShelfFilter.home_branch` over `branch`) walks that last hop too: `isnull` tests whether a visible target row exists, a primary-key, `GlobalID` or model-choice value naming a hidden row matches nothing, a forward key keeps comparing the stored key and also requires it to reach a visible row (a book on a hidden shelf reads as having no shelf), and a to-many key compares the visible target rows' own key column once Django builds the declared predicate (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._leaf_chain`), so it answers only the lookups and values the relation accepts. A `GlobalID` key whose target's id an annotation of the target type's `get_queryset` holds, forward or to-many, does neither: it compares that annotation on the visible target rows, as its trailing-pk twin does. A relation-key leaf whose class overrides `filter` (consumer code, `filters/sets.py::_runs_shipped_filter`) on a to-many path, or on a forward path two or more hops long, is never re-read onto the target key when its value names rows of the relation's target: its override runs once, as declared, receiving its own `field_name`, the value its form cleaned (a model-choice filter's instances) and the rows of the set declaring it, as when no hop is walked, and a parent it keeps must also reach, through each hop's visible rows, a visible last-hop row the value names (`filters/sets.py::_KeyMatch`: a model-choice value names its rows, instances Django's relation lookup accepts for the target, every one of them when the filter is `conjoined`; a raw key or a `GlobalID` names the rows the nearest shipped `filter` of the leaf's class keeps on the column Django's relation lookup compares, a single non-key `to_field` included), so a value naming a hidden row matches nothing. Its value names the rows it keeps: an override whose rows are not those rows answers the rows both keep, and its negation is `exclude=True`, which the package applies at the parent, handing the override `exclude` off (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._apply_relation_leaf`); a path its own Python reaches beyond its value (another related column, a row it picks whatever the value) is its own scoping, as for a `method=` filter. A value that names no row of the relation's target (an empty or absent model-choice value, instances or a key of another model, a value the shipped comparison skips or refuses) runs on the chain's fallback (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._request_chain`). On a to-many key the override runs once, as declared, over the rows of the set declaring it, its `field_name` a `FilteredRelation` alias of its relation (`<relation>_visible`, numbered past a name the query or the model already holds) that joins, through every relation the walk crosses, only the related rows each hop's type lets the request see and its `RelatedFilter(queryset=...)` admits (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._relation_view`; a many-to-many joins its through rows by an alias of their own conditioned on the visible targets, `filters/sets.py::_through_link`, so a through row onto a hidden target is no row of the view; the round trip: a first non-null forward key the database enforces is joined round its target (the key, then its reverse relation back to the parent, an outer join, then the key again, `filters/sets.py::_round_trip`), so the view keeps a parent whose related row is hidden; a `related_name="+"`, bare `ForeignObject`, `db_constraint=False` or multi-table-inheritance-child key keeps Django's inner join; a many-to-many whose through key onto its source Django gives no reverse name of its own (an auto-created self-referential one read forward, symmetrical or not) keeps the leaf re-bound to the target key over the visible targets, `filters/sets.py::_names_every_through`), and its `lookup_expr` the lookup the walked path applies where it ends (a `lookup_expr` crossing a relation is part of the path the alias joins): a default it reads (a key, model instances, a queryset, one instance, a GlobalID), a path it continues past the relation (`<field_name>__pk__in`, `<field_name>__code`, `<field_name>__isnull`), `exclude` and `conjoined` answer as unwalked over the visible related rows, and a value Django refuses raises Django's own error. The request's visibility reaches the override only through its `field_name`: a relation its own Python names reads every related row and is its own scoping, as for a `method=` filter. On a forward key two or more hops away the leaf compares the stored key on the rows holding it and keeps those reaching a visible last-hop row. An `isnull` override runs over the related rows the walk keeps visible, re-bound onto the target's primary key. A class-level override on a path into a target column runs over the last hop's visible target rows with the path left on that target as its `field_name` and the lookup applied where the walked path ends as its `lookup_expr`; the override running as declared receives the pair it was declared with, a crossing pair included. Coverage is decided by path, not by how the leaf was generated: an expanded copy of a child filter, an owner-bound `ProjectedChildFilter` (its child filter runs over the hop's visible rows inside the child filter set), and a plain `Meta.fields` traversal under a declared root (`LoanFilter`'s `book__loans__patron__email` walks `book`, `loans` and `patron`) all count. A relation-key leaf over an undeclared relation reads the same way (`altShelves: { exact: [<pk of a secret shelf>] }` matches nothing, like a pk no row carries). Two leaf shapes are not walked: a leaf whose relations no declaration claims and no type scopes (above), and a filter declared on the parent holding a `filter` of its own (a consumer `method=`, or a callable stored on the instance), whose Python receives the parent queryset and owns its own scoping ([`GOAL.md`][goal] "Trust boundary"); such a filter's bound receiver would keep the parent's path if a hop rebound its copy's `field_name` (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._bound_terminal`). A supplied leaf (any form-data key its widget reads is present, `""` included; `FilterSet._leaf_absent`) derives its hops, then runs its filter once, over the last hop's visible rows; a filter returning those rows BY IDENTITY (django-filter's empty-value skip) constrains nothing, so a supplied-but-inactive leaf derives its hops once and constrains nothing (`FilterSet._apply_relation_leaf`). Because a supplied leaf derives its hops before its filter runs, a supplied empty value (`""`) on `apply_sync` answers every hop failure its nested twin and `apply_async` answer: a hop whose target has no registered `DjangoType` raises the branch's [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror], a hop through a `RelatedFilter(None, ...)` placeholder raises the same `ConfigurationError` (its target filter set does not resolve), and a hop whose target type's `get_queryset` is async-only raises [`SyncMisuseError`][querysets] (which `FilterSet.apply` rethrows as the `RuntimeError` naming `apply_async`). An absent leaf, or an absent `ProjectedChildFilter`, runs its filter with the value its field cleans an absent key to over the last hop target's unscoped rows (for a relation-key leaf on one forward hop, whose chain walks no hop, over the rows the filter set was built over, never the rows the leaves before it left, so the answer does not depend on when it is asked) only to learn whether it constrains anything (`FilterSet._absent_leaf_applies`): one returning its input BY IDENTITY derives nothing and runs no `get_queryset`; one that constrains (a field `empty_value`, an unchecked checkbox's `False`, a consumer `filter` reading an absent value as a default) derives its hops and runs again over the visible rows. Both colors ask that question once the form validates, before the `.qs` read, on the request's root set (`FilterSet._probe_absent_relation_leaves`) and on every `and` / `or` / `not` arm's sibling, which the probe builds as the `.qs` read will, over the rows the root set was built over, and validates (`FilterSet._probe_logic_arms` / `FilterSet._probe_logic_arms_async`); an arm starts from the answers of the set holding it and asks only a leaf they do not answer for the value it cleaned to there (a verdict is per (leaf, cleaned value): a leaf a consumer `__init__` adds from the arm's data, or one a consumer form `clean` cleans to another value). `apply_async` awaits the hops of each leaf that constrains, root or arm, and the `.qs` read reuses each arm's sibling (`FilterSet._q_for_branch`, through `FilterSet._probed_arms`), so a consumer `__init__` and form `clean` run once per arm and the `.qs` read reads the recorded answers (`FilterSet._absent_leaf_verdicts`) rather than asking again; an arm whose `__init__` replaced the queryset handed to it is built again over the arm's rows at the `.qs` read, keeping the probe's answers, since what it narrowed cannot be carried onto other rows. So a filter reading queryset state for an absent leaf is asked over the rows the root set was built over, never the rows an arm's own constraints left, and the filter and hook run as often on both colors for a set whose `.qs` runs the package's flat-leaf loop; a `qs` / `filter_queryset` override that skips the flat-leaf loop still sees the probe's one run, and one that never composes the `and` / `or` / `not` keys still has every arm's sibling built, validated (a malformed arm raises `FILTER_INVALID`) and probed. Only a run tells a filter that skips an absent value from one that acts on it. Hop visibility is one request-wide map, `FilterSet._flat_hop_visibility`, keyed by `(id(<RelatedFilter declaration>), <database alias>)` for a declared hop and by `(<scoping DjangoType>, <database alias>)` for an undeclared hop or intermediate model (`FilterSet._scoped_rows`), created by `apply_sync` / `apply_async` before the first nested branch derives (a nested child's `apply_*` receives its parent's map through `_hop_visibility`) and shared with every nested branch at every level, every logical-arm sibling and every flat leaf, so a declared hop's `get_queryset` runs once per request however many nested branches, leaves and arms walk it (`FilterSet._declared_hop_rows` / `FilterSet._await_declared_hop_rows`); a declared hop and an undeclared relation reaching the same type are two hops, one run each. A projection child instance (`FilterSet._projection_child`) reads no hop: the set holding a projected leaf reads every hop of the leaf's chain. A hop of a supplied leaf (a projected copy's child-set hops included) whose target has no registered `DjangoType`, or whose target filter set does not resolve (a `RelatedFilter(None, ...)` placeholder), raises the nested branch's [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] (`_branch_visibility_seed`) before the leaf's filter runs over the visible rows, projected or plain; an absent leaf reaching such a hop raises when its filter, run over the unscoped rows, constrains.
4. The pipeline normalizes the Strawberry input dataclass into the form-data dict (walking nested `RelatedFilter` input objects, mapping `and_` / `or_` / `not_` back to the logical-operator keys, mapping per-lookup Python attrs through `LOOKUP_NAME_MAP`, converting values through `normalize_input_value`) and resolves the Django request through `_request_from_info(info)` ([`utils/permissions.py::request_from_info`][utils-permissions]: `info.context.request`, a bare `HttpRequest` context, a mapping context whose `"request"` is an `HttpRequest`, or Strawberry's Channels context; any other shape raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror]).
4a. **Apply related constraints to the queryset BEFORE constructing the FilterSet instance.** `_apply_related_constraints(input_value, queryset, child_qs_by_branch)` runs `filters/sets.py::_restrict_through_branch` once per active branch: it AND-intersects the branch's explicit `RelatedFilter(queryset=...)` constraint (a callable `queryset=`, django-filter's request-aware form, is called with the filter set's request through `RelatedFilter.get_queryset`, on a flat leaf's walk as on a nested branch; the explicit queryset, sliced, `distinct()` or combined alike, is read as an `IN` subquery of the rows it holds, so child rows read `distinct()` intersect with it too) with its visibility-scoped child queryset, then keeps the parent rows reaching a row of the intersected queryset through one correlated `EXISTS` built from those rows (`optimizer/predicates.py::related_rows_exist`, correlated on the relation's own link columns). `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet.qs` derives from the instance's *stored* `self.queryset`, so constraining BEFORE construction is what carries the constraints through to `.qs`. The pipeline then instantiates `cls(data=normalized_data, queryset=constrained_queryset, request=request)`.
5. The pipeline calls `cls._run_permission_checks(input_value, request)` — a denial (a gate that raises, or returns anything but `None` / `True`) raises `GraphQLError(...)` before any filter clause touches the queryset. The per-field gate runs **only for fields present in the input** (active-input-only), NOT for every declared field. This diverges from the cookbook's `AdvancedFilterSet.check_permissions(request)`, which walks every declared filter: permissions gate USE of the filter, not its declaration. The check recurses through `RelatedFilter`s into child filtersets' gates for active branches only, each gate firing at most once per class. A nested child filterset's own `apply_*` (run during step 3's derivation) passes `run_permissions=False`, so nested gates fire once, from this top-level pass.
6. **The pipeline explicitly calls `filterset.form.is_valid()`** and raises `GraphQLError("Invalid filter input", extensions={"code": "FILTER_INVALID", "errors": <serialized filterset.errors>})` when validation fails. `django_filters.filterset.BaseFilterSet.qs` only consults `self.errors` and falls through to `filter_queryset` regardless, so without the explicit raise invalid input would degrade into a partially-filtered or unfiltered queryset. The `extensions.errors` payload is `filterset.errors.get_json_data()`, giving `{<field_name>: [{"message": <str>, "code": <str>}, ...], ...}`. Every key, here and in the refusals below, is the form field's name under the filterset's path from the request root (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_branch_key`, the one spelling of a child's name seen from its parent, which also names a `RelatedFilter`'s flat copies): a root leaf's key is its own, a nested `RelatedFilter` branch's leaf is `<branch>__<field>` with the declared `RelatedFilter` names composing (`shelf__branch__name`, the key the branch's flat copy of the same leaf answers), and a logical arm is a segment of its own, the operator's wire name and the arm's position in the list sent (`or_0`, `and_1`; `not` bare; `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::LogicOperatorDescriptor.arm_key`), nesting either way (`or_0__shelf__branch__name`, `shelf__or_0__branch__name`, `or_0__or_1__title`); a nested form's non-field error is `<path>____all__`. The path rides each nested filterset as `_branch_path`, threaded where the recursion budget travels, so the synchronous and asynchronous pipelines answer one key. The refusals below are the form's own errors: `FilterSet.form` builds a `django_strawberry_framework/filters/form_binding.py::BindAskingFilterForm` holding the set's asks (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_unstorable_text_errors`, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_unbindable_value_errors`), whose `full_clean` adds each through `add_error`, so every driver reading the form sees them, raw form data included ([Edge cases](#edge-cases-and-constraints)); a form a consumer `form` override builds holds no asks, so `_validate_form_or_raise` runs them itself, in the same order and with the same answers. Before any field cleans, a field whose value holds text the package refuses (a lone surrogate or an embedded NUL, which a JSON `\ud800` or `\u0000` escape in the variables carries; `django_strawberry_framework/utils/errors.py::unstorable_text`, the rule the write decode applies) answers the same error with `{<field_name>: [{"message": <str>, "code": <str>}]}` for each such field, and cleaning is skipped, so only those refusals are reported (`"Text contains invalid Unicode (unpaired surrogate code points)."` / `"invalid"` for a surrogate, Django's `ProhibitNullCharactersValidator` message / `"null_characters_not_allowed"` for a NUL), since a model-choice field's `clean` and every lookup's query would hand it to the driver, whose `UnicodeEncodeError` (surrogate) or PostgreSQL `DataError` (NUL) is no validation error; a plain-`Field` filter (a relation key on a text primary key, a consumer `field_class`) has no validator of its own, so the refusal is what answers on every backend. A value a resolver hands in as a Django lazy proxy (`SimpleLazyObject`) is read as the text it stands for. A widget refusing the value it is handed as the form reads it (a `ValidationError` from its `decompress`: an aware datetime whose wall clock in the current time zone leaves years 1..9999, which a nested range bound's `SplitDateTimeWidget` cannot split, `django_strawberry_framework/utils/widgets.py::_decompressed`) is that field's error in the same shape, `{<field_name>: [{"message": "Enter a value the current time zone can represent.", "code": "invalid"}]}`, the refusal `Form._clean_fields` keys to the field for the same read. A `disabled` field is skipped (it cleans to its initial value, never to what was sent), and so is a GlobalID filter's value: its decode refuses it as `GLOBALID_INVALID` ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor)) unless a `filter` of its own (a `method=` included) replaces that decode.
