Each value the form cleans is then held to the database its queryset runs on, whatever error another field's cleaning raised, so the payload is the union of the form's errors and these refusals (`queryset.db`, resolved only for a temporal, decimal or integer-array leaf): each cleaned value whose filter reaches a temporal or decimal column or an `ArrayField` of integers (`django_strawberry_framework/utils/querysets.py::lookup_target_fields`, the path resolved over the filterset's queryset as `Query.build_filter` resolves it: `Query.solve_lookup_type` reads a name the queryset annotates or aliases as that expression's `output_field`, and `Query.names_to_path` follows relation hops, `pk`, a `FilteredRelation` alias and a concrete parent's fields; an `ArrayField` read through to its innermost elements' column) is applied by the filter's own `filter` and the clause it adds compiled on that database (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_unbindable_predicate`), and a value Django's conversion for the lookup, that connection's own adapter, Django's year bounds, the integer range the database stores for the lookup's type or an integer array's element, PostgreSQL's UTC offset range or PostgreSQL `numeric`'s format refuse (every member of a list and bound of a range, as the filter applies them) answers the same error with `{<form field>: [{"message": "Enter a value the database can represent.", "code": "invalid"}]}`, since the driver's `OverflowError` / `ValueError`, or PostgreSQL's `DataError`, at evaluation would escape as an unexpected error (see [Error shapes](#error-shapes) for the populations). Each value is asked held to its integer field's range (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_held_integer_value`, step 8): a decimal past it is asked as the integer one past the same end, never converted. A named queryset integer `Transform` alias is bind-asked through the actual shipped filter and Django-selected lookup: `ExtractYear` / `ExtractIsoYear` comparisons and package `RangeFilter` bounds can invoke native year-bound preparation, while their `in` and a native CSV `range` remain numeric; `ExtractMonth` membership beyond the database integer range is refused. A registered integer `Abs` lookup is read through the actual lookup walk even when its output field is the stored column field. Ordinary integer columns, `F` aliases and `Count` annotations keep their existing member/drop and comparison behavior.
   For complete native or consumer-owned annotation fields, the root and every logical descendant in this one apply input finish their entire actual consumer `full_clean` first. An ordinary field error anywhere suppresses every new bind-only annotation owner across that input. On native success, one postclean phase asks only active final `cleaned_data` values, including a complete disabled initial and values added, repaired or removed by `Form.clean` or post-super code; async awaits outside the worker and finishes those same forms without a second constructor or clean. A missing automatic list/range inner parser alone retains its after-gate preclean source dependency. Cached/local bind refusals can still join ordinary form errors, and a raw invalid `.qs` omits only a leaf whose uncached bind source is unavailable while keeping safe siblings.
7. **Active-branch related-queryset scoping — applied in step 4a.** The constraint applies ONLY when the related branch is present in the input; an empty `filter: {}` or `filter: { name: { iContains: "..." } }` (no `shelves` branch) leaves the parent queryset unconstrained; `filter: { shelves: { code: { iContains: "..." } } }` activates it, and an active flat leaf walking the branch (`shelvesCode`) applies it inside step 8. The cookbook's `_apply_related_queryset_constraints` applies all declared constraints unconditionally; the package narrows that to active branches so an empty filter never pre-excludes parent rows.
8. The instance's `.qs` property runs `filter_queryset(queryset)` (the tree-form override: flat leaves, then the `and` / `or` / `not` composition) and the pipeline returns `filterset.qs` to the resolver. A flat leaf walking a declared hop (step 3) applies its branch restriction inside this read (`_apply_flat_leaves` → `_apply_relation_leaf`), deriving each hop's visibility on first use on the sync path, and is never routed through the correlated `EXISTS` adapter: routing ([`FilterSet`][glossary-filterset] "Row-preserving to-many predicates") covers only framework-generated leaves the step-3 scoping does not walk. A walking leaf is row-preserving too, since each hop it walks is a correlated `EXISTS`. Before a leaf applies, its cleaned value is held to the range of the integer field its shipped filter compares on the queryset's database (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_held_integer_value`; `django_strawberry_framework/utils/errors.py::integer_past_range`, the range `connection.ops.integer_field_range` states, Django's `IntegerFieldOverflow`'s): each `Decimal` whose whole part lies past it becomes the integer one past that end, so every verdict the decimal gives is kept (a comparison matching nothing or every row, an `in` member dropped, a `range` bound widening or emptying, the step-6 ask refusing) while Django's `int()` of it, quadratic in its exponent, never runs. The integer field is the column, an integer transform's output or an integer `ArrayField`'s elements (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_integer_compared_column`), read over the queryset handed to the set for a path its model names no field for (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_bound_lookup_operand`), or, on a path only the queryset the leaf is applied to resolves (a consumer `filter_queryset` annotating before it calls `super()`), an integer annotation, a column past a `FilteredRelation` alias or a transform of either (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_queryset_integer_column`); only Django's own lookup preparing its value through that field is held (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_django_prepares`: `Lookup.prepare_rhs` on a class Django defines; `iexact`, the text patterns, `isnull` and a project lookup a `register_lookup` adds (in a set a project resolver applies) receive the value as cleaned, since only Django's own comparisons read no more of a number than which side of the range it lies on); a projected copy is held over its child filter set's queryset; a `method=` filter or a class-level `filter` override receives the value its form cleaned, a class-level override on a walked relation key whose value names rows of the relation's target with its declared `field_name` and its own set's rows (step 3).
   A projected child constructed before an annotation hook keeps its own queryset membership after the active hook supplies comparison context: the terminal filter and outward correlation use the child-owned narrowed rows, while `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._declared_hop_rows` still caches the raw hook rows once per request. A projected `ListFilter` or `RangeFilter` with `method=` keeps its sent-value cleaner and derives its target hook only during this step. An existing filter or materialized form field with an owned inner comparison field keeps that inner field and its validators.
9. The optimizer ([`DjangoOptimizerExtension`][glossary-djangooptimizerextension]) walks the selection tree on the post-filter queryset; [Queryset diffing][glossary-queryset-diffing] cooperates with any consumer `select_related` / `prefetch_related` work already present.

<a id="sync-async-api-split"></a>**Sync / async API split.** A single `FilterSet.apply(input_value, queryset, info)` cannot serve step 3: a target `DjangoType.get_queryset(...)` can be async-only (returns a coroutine), and a sync resolver cannot synchronously await it. The shape (matching `apply_type_visibility_sync` / `apply_type_visibility_async` in [`utils/querysets.py`][querysets]):

- `FilterSet.apply_sync(input_value, queryset, info) -> QuerySet` — the sync-resolver entry point. Dispatches step 3 through [`apply_type_visibility_sync`][querysets], as does a flat leaf's first-use hop derivation inside the step-8 `.qs` read (one derivation per declared hop, `FilterSet._declared_hop_rows`, so a hop step 3 already derived is read, not derived again); if a target's `get_queryset` returns a coroutine, it raises [`SyncMisuseError`][querysets] — a typed marker subclassing BOTH [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] and `RuntimeError`, so a consumer catching either still matches — whose message names the filter recourse (`filters/sets.py::_FILTER_ASYNC_RECOURSE`: await `FilterSet.apply_async` from an async resolver, or make the hook sync), never the Relay node defaults'.
- `async def FilterSet.apply_async(input_value, queryset, info) -> QuerySet` — the async-resolver entry point. Dispatches step 3 through `_derive_related_visibility_querysets_async` (and `_collect_nested_visibility_querysets_async` for the `and` / `or` / `not` arms), awaiting the target's `get_queryset` regardless of shape, once per declared hop, into the request-wide hop map (`FilterSet._await_declared_hop_rows`). It also awaits, through `_derive_flat_hop_visibility_async`, the ordinary resolved declared hops a flat leaf the form names walks, at the top level and in every `and` / `or` / `not` arm (`_collect_nested_visibility_querysets_async(..., flat_hop_visibility=...)`), into the request-wide hop map the sync `.qs` read then finds pre-filled, so an async-only `get_queryset` never runs inside the sync boundary. The pre-pass runs before any form exists and reads a supplied ordinary resolved leaf by its normalized form-data key; complete unresolved annotation fields wait for the postclean phase: a supplied ordinary resolved value the filter then skips (an empty string) costs one awaited `get_queryset` and constrains nothing, as a present-but-empty nested branch does; an unsupplied leaf, a range naming no bound (`{start: null, end: null}`, `{}`) included, costs the pre-pass nothing. It then runs step 4 (`FilterSet._apply_common_prelude`, which calls a callable `RelatedFilter` `queryset=`) and stages the built filterset after its permission pass in a `utils/querysets.py::run_in_one_sync_boundary` worker. An actual disabled form field with a known nonempty initial value may expose an annotation owner that no wire key named. A missing automatic inner cleaner is installed from the declaring set's filter policy after awaiting its owner outside the worker and before the one native clean. A complete field is cleaned on the same form first, then an active final value receives its postclean bind ask after every logical form's native-clean barrier succeeds. Root and logical-arm siblings each follow this stage → await → resume sequence for missing parsers, then complete native cleaning across the input before any new bind-only await. A nested `RelatedFilter` child runs its own `apply_async`. The validated instance then asks each absent walking leaf (a flat walk or a `ProjectedChildFilter`, a leaf a consumer `__init__` adds included) whether its filter constrains on the value it cleaned to (`FilterSet._probe_absent_relation_leaves`) — in a `utils/querysets.py::run_in_one_sync_boundary` worker, so a consumer's filterset constructor, `check_<field>_permission` hook, custom `method=` filter body or leaf-clause ORM evaluation never blocks the event loop. The probe asks the root set's absent walking leaves and those of every `and` / `or` / `not` arm's sibling, which it builds as the `.qs` read will (`FilterSet._probe_logic_arms_async`). The hops of every absent leaf that constrains are awaited into the hop map (`FilterSet._await_hop_visibility_async`) and a later worker reads `.qs`, which reuses the arms' siblings and reads the recorded answers rather than asking again. So an absent leaf derives its hop only when its filter constrains on the absent value, as on `apply_sync`, and an async-only `get_queryset` scoping that hop is awaited, never run inside the sync boundary; the hook and the filter run as often as on `apply_sync`, which runs the same probe, for a set whose `.qs` runs the package's flat-leaf loop, and a `qs` / `filter_queryset` override that skips the flat-leaf loop still sees the probe's one run and awaited hook. An absent leaf inside an `and` / `or` / `not` arm is asked by the probe unless the set holding the arm answered for the value it cleaned to there, so a leaf a consumer `__init__` adds from the arm's data or a consumer form `clean` cleans to another value has its hops awaited too; a leaf an arm names had its hops awaited by the pre-pass. A nested `RelatedFilter` child runs its own `apply_async`.
- `FilterSet.apply(input_value, queryset, info)` — a thin dispatcher that calls `apply_sync(...)` inside `try / except SyncMisuseError as exc:` and re-raises `RuntimeError("FilterSet.apply called against async get_queryset; use apply_async instead.") from exc`. The dispatch is **class-based, never a substring match**; the message carries no interpolated target type because `from exc` already puts the original [`SyncMisuseError`][querysets] on `__cause__`. Any other `RuntimeError` or `ConfigurationError` propagates unchanged. **Partial side-effect semantics:** On `apply_sync`, an active nested `RelatedFilter` branch invokes its target hook in step 3, before the top-level permission pass and form validation; a nested child entered with `run_permissions=False` remains part of that step-3 derivation. A missing automatic list or range inner parser needs the final annotation owner before its field cleans. A complete native or consumer-owned field supplies its final cleaned value first; its bind ask uses the final owner after whole-form cleaning. `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._named_annotation_hops` follows the actual bound child filters through every projected, declared, and scoped undeclared hop using `FilterSet._leaf_chain`, and asks Django to resolve the final output path and lookup. Only an unresolved source needed to install a missing automatic inner parser runs between the step-5 permission pass and native field cleaning. A complete-field bind-only owner runs in the postclean phase after the root and all logical-arm forms in this apply input have finished native cleaning without an error rejected by the actual consumer validation policy; a consumer `Form.is_valid()` accepting selected native field errors retains its active annotation constraints and postclean owner acquisition. An absent leaf whose native filter returns its rows by identity acquires no owner. The constructed instance's queryset alias keys both async readiness and owner acquisition, including a consumer constructor selecting another database; denial and invalid native fields acquire no new bind-only owner. `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._necessary_annotation_hop` is the same final-owner check for named, raw-widget, and disabled-initial values; `django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._install_source_comparison` asks the actual final Query and the declaring consumer set’s django-filter policy for a missing list or range inner cleaner. An earlier hop on the same flat path stays at step 8 unless a nested branch already needed it. The request-wide visibility map supplies the same owner rows to validation and `.qs`, so that hook runs once. The bind ask reads those cached rows and does not derive another hook or timing. It resolves the actual terminal expression over those rows for eligibility by native transform node/start evidence, not raw field identity, then asks the filter's own emitted predicate; scoped package ranges construct their integer `gte` / `lte` pair from the queryset they receive, as handed ranges do. An async-only owner needed for a missing automatic inner parser can still raise `SyncMisuseError` before that field cleans. For a complete field, native field errors win over a new bind-only owner and over its `SyncMisuseError`, including a later logical arm's error. Every ordinary sync flat leaf with model-resolved output still derives its hop at the step-8 `.qs` read, after permissions and form validation. `apply_async` preawaits ordinary resolved flat hops; it stages missing parser owners after the gate and awaits complete-field bind-only owners after native cleaning on the same actual forms. The shared map is reused during validation and `.qs`; its nested branches remain step-3 derivations. The thin `apply` dispatcher translates a `SyncMisuseError` to the actionable `RuntimeError`. Neither sync entry mutates module globals or the input-class namespace, and earlier visibility querysets are discarded on error.

Consumer pattern: sync resolvers call `BranchType.get_queryset(qs, info)` and `BranchFilter.apply_sync(filter, qs, info)`. Async resolvers `await` the same hook through [`apply_type_visibility_async`][querysets] (a `DjangoType.get_queryset` may be declared `async def`; there is no separate `aget_queryset` name) and then `await BranchFilter.apply_async(filter, qs, info)`. Every fakeshop library type uses a sync `get_queryset`, so the hand-written library resolvers call `apply_sync`; the async color is pinned live by `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_library_api.py::test_genres_connection_filter_applies_async` on `/graphql-async/`, and an async-only `get_queryset` target is exercised in [`tests/filters/test_sets.py`][test-filters] (see [Test plan](#test-plan)).

<a id="direct-construction-actor"></a>**A set built outside the pipeline.** `DjangoFilterBackend`, django-filter's `FilterView` and a consumer's own `FilterSet(data, queryset, request=...)` construct the set, and a set's first construction publishes its filter table (`FilterSet.__init__` calls `_filter_table()` before django-filter copies `base_filters`): a set no type serves and a plain subclass of a served set (which publishes its own table, its expansion following the path-relative cycle rule by its own identity) copy every flat filter their `RelatedFilter` declarations expand to, so a walked key walks its hop on the first request, never ignored as an unknown key, while a key only a `flat=False` declaration's copy would register is ignored, as django-filter ignores any unknown key; a construction finding the set unable to publish yet (a read re-entering its build, a second thread mid-build, a set with no `Meta.model` whose `RelatedFilter` target is still a lazy string) copies the table that read answers, never the class-creation `base_filters`. A `base_filters` entry assigned on the class before its first use is replaced by the published table, as a served set's is at build; a `get_filters()` override is the seam for adding filters. A walked key whose hop's target has no registered `DjangoType` raises the walked-key `ConfigurationError` of `FilterSet._branch_visibility_seed`, as under `apply_sync`. They run no resolver, so no Strawberry `Info` exists: Strawberry builds one per resolver call around graphql-core's `GraphQLResolveInfo`, which only the executor fills. Such a set built with a request holds `filters/sets.py::_DirectRequestInfo` as its resolver `info` (`FilterSet.__init__`, never on an instance the pipeline builds, which stashes the resolver's own), and every target type's `get_queryset` its walked leaves, hops, intermediate models and `and` / `or` / `not` arms reach (steps 3 and 8, and the arms' siblings, which share it) receives it: its `context` (`filters/sets.py::_DirectRequestContext`) holds the set's request as Strawberry's `StrawberryDjangoContext` holds the GraphQL request, read as `.request`, `["request"]` or `.get("request")`, writable as that plain dataclass is (a hook may memoize on it), with `response` `None` (no sub-response exists to merge), so a hook reads the actor as `info.context.request.user` on either path (GOAL.md "Trust boundary"). Under DRF the request is DRF's own `Request`, never the Django request under it, so `request.user` is the user DRF's authentication answers. It carries no schema, field or selection, so a GlobalID type-name check there reads no schema. A set built with no request hands its hooks `None`.

<a id="raw-form-heads"></a>**A raw form's `RelatedFilter` head.** GraphQL names no head, but raw form data does: `shelf=<pk>` for `BookFilter.shelf`, `shelves__books__shelf=<pk>` for the head an expansion copies into `BranchFilter`. Such a head is a relation-key leaf (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._flat_leaf_walk`): it walks its path as the relation key `shelf` does, every hop's target `get_queryset` (step 3) and every declaration's `queryset=` scoping it, a copied head's hops above it included, and on the bound form its `ModelChoiceField` validates the key against its own queryset (`filters/base.py::RelatedFilter.get_queryset`) within the rows its last hop's target type lets the request see (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._scope_supplied_heads`, reading the request-wide hop map the walk reads, so each hop's hook runs once). A hidden row's key is refused as `invalid_choice`, exactly as a key naming no row is, as DRF's `PrimaryKeyRelatedField` answers both alike. A head under a `null_label` walks its null value as the relation's null test over the rows each relation shows and each declaration's `queryset=` admits (`filters/sets.py::_head_null_test`, django-filter's `<relation>__exact=None`), so a parent whose related rows are all hidden or outside that `queryset=` matches as one with none. A to-many head keeps its `Select` widget's last value. Three heads run as declared, over the declaration's queryset with django-filter's choices: a head the form data leaves out (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._walks_on_request`; no hook runs), a `method=` head (its Python owns its scoping), and a head whose walk crosses, at any hop of its path, a declared hop with no resolved target (`filters/sets.py::FilterSet._branch_target`: no `DjangoType` registered for its model, or no child filterset), a shape only a set no type serves holds (the registered-target audit refuses a served one at build): the walk does not read it, so it keeps its declaration's own scope only, no hop's visibility and no `queryset=` of a declaration above it; the walked key through such a hop raises `ConfigurationError` (step 3). A declared `ModelChoiceFilter` keeps its own choices. A head overriding `filter` follows the overridden relation-key rule ([Edge cases](#edge-cases-and-constraints)): a to-many head, or a forward one two or more relations away, runs once as declared and keeps only the parents that also reach a visible row its value names, and a forward head one relation away compares the stored key it holds; under a `null_label` each receives its own null value (`filters/sets.py::_head_null_test` is asked only for a head running shipped code, `filters/sets.py::_runs_shipped_filter`), which names no row (`filters/sets.py::_KeyMatch.named_rows` on a to-many path, `filters/sets.py::_head_null_view` on a forward one), so the override runs as declared over its relation joined only to the rows each relation shows and each declaration's `queryset=` admits (`_LeafChain.view`), a parent whose related rows are all hidden or outside that `queryset=` reading as one with none. A sliced `queryset=`, which Django can no longer filter, keeps its choices unnarrowed; a walk through its declaration admits the visible rows the slice holds (`filters/sets.py::_admitted_rows` reads its `queryset=` as an `IN` subquery of its keys), a walked key through it and a nested branch under it included. A consumer `form` override that skips `super()` still walks the head (no row leaks) but its form validates the key over the declaration's queryset, so a hidden row's key is a valid choice matching nothing.

<a id="related-queryset-boundary-scope"></a>**`_apply_related_constraints` scope.** The cookbook ancestor (`AdvancedFilterSet._apply_related_queryset_constraints`) filters the parent queryset by the related constraint; it does NOT filter the nested relation resolver's output. A `Branch` with one shelf inside the constraint AND one outside passes the parent filter and still surfaces BOTH shelves through `BranchType.shelves`. The contract is: "branches without any matching constrained shelf are excluded from the parent result; nested filter clauses, and flat leaves walking the branch (`shelvesTopic`), cannot use shelves outside the constraint to make a branch match." It is **not** "shelves outside the constraint never appear in the response." Output-shape scoping at the nested-relation level is out of scope. The active-branch refinement further narrows the contract: the constraint applies ONLY when a predicate through the branch is active, nested or flat.

Composition: [`apply_cascade_permissions`][glossary-apply_cascade_permissions] runs inside step 1 (the `get_queryset` hook); [Per-field permission hooks][glossary-per-field-permission-hooks], when they ship, slot into the field-resolver layer that runs after step 8.

Rationale companion — this Decision's justification and its five rejected alternatives: [Decision 8][rationale-d8].

### Decision 9 — Input-class namespace vs `TypeRegistry` and lifecycle

**The input-class namespace is the [`django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs`][filters] module's own global namespace**, separate from the model-to-`DjangoType` registry at [`django_strawberry_framework.registry.registry`][registry] (which powers [`Meta.primary`][glossary-metaprimary]).

Every generated filter input class is a real module global of `django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs`, set at finalize time. The class names are stable and class-derived (`f"{FilterSet.__name__}InputType"` → `"GalaxyFilterInputType"`). The "registry" IS the module's `__dict__` — there is no separate private dict registry, because Strawberry's `LazyType.resolve_type` reads `module.__dict__` directly and cannot traverse a sidecar dict. A lifecycle ledger (`django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs._materialized_names`, a private `dict[str, type]`) maps `class_name → input_class` — each value the materialized Strawberry input class — so re-materialization is idempotent and clears cleanly. Source-class collision detection (two filtersets claiming one name before materialization) is the factory's separate `_type_filterset_registry`. A generated name two declarations derive from different names (another set, another family, a nested operator bag or range) is the separate cross-family type-name claim at [`utils/inputs.py::claim_generated_type_name`][utils-inputs] (Edge cases).

The two namespaces (model-to-`DjangoType` registry and input-class module globals) are disjoint by key type (Django model class vs string class name).

Lifecycle contract:

- **Registration is idempotent for the same `(name, input_class)` pair.** Calling `materialize_input_class("GalaxyFilterInputType", input_a)` twice with the same `input_a` is a no-op: the check is `is` identity against the class the ledger already holds under that name.
- **Registration raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] when the same name is claimed by a DIFFERENT input class**, with both input classes' qualified names in the message and rename advice naming the filterset. Because each name derives from the owning `FilterSet`'s `__name__`, this catches two same-named `FilterSet`s in different modules.
- **`registry.clear()` resets the filter input lifecycle ledger and every piece of generated-build state hanging off it.** `clear_filter_input_namespace()` is the family wrapper over the shared heavy clear at [`utils/inputs.py::clear_generated_input_namespace`][utils-inputs]: it empties `_materialized_names` and `_field_specs`, clears the `FilterArgumentsFactory` class-level caches (`input_object_types` plus the `_type_filterset_registry` collision map), and drops every `FilterSet` subclass's phase-2.5 binding state — `_owner_definition`, `_expanded_filters`, `_is_expanding_filters`, and the candidate-metadata snapshot `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._publish_filters` publishes from the set's filter table once the expansion is cached, read off the family's lifecycle descriptor at `django_strawberry_framework/sets_mixins.py::SetLifecycleAttrs.binding_attrs`. Each attribute is dropped only where the subclass set it directly, so the subclass reads as unbound again (every binding read is the class's own slot). Both submodule lookups the clear needs are best-effort: an unimportable `filters.factories` or `filters.sets` skips only its own dependent reset.
- **Materialized class objects stay parked in the `django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs` module `__dict__`; no clear path strips them.** `materialize_input_class` overwrites the module global on the next finalize, and it is the ledger reset that drives the rebuild. Stripping a global with `delattr` would break any `strawberry.lazy(...)` LazyType held by a consumer module whose reload fixture did not also reload the holder; leaving the class parked lets that LazyType keep resolving until the next finalize replaces it.
- **Import-cycle-safe integration via subsystem registration.** [`django_strawberry_framework/registry.py`][registry] is a low-level module imported by `types/`, `optimizer/`, and the package's `__init__.py`, so it imports nothing from the filter subsystem. The owning module announces its own teardown at import time, and `TypeRegistry.clear()` replays whatever announced itself:

  ```python
  # django_strawberry_framework/filters/__init__.py
  register_subsystem_clear(_helper_referenced_filtersets.clear, owner="filters.helper_references")

  # django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py
  register_subsystem_clear(
      clear_filter_input_namespace,
      owner="filters.input_namespace",
      before_bind=True,
  )

  # django_strawberry_framework/registry.py
  class TypeRegistry:
      def clear(self) -> None:
          ...  # the registry's own state
          for clear in iter_subsystem_clears():
              clear()
  ```

  **Laziness is preserved**: only an imported owner can register, so a consumer who never imports `filters` has nothing to clear and no `except ImportError` branch exists. **A rename cannot rot silently**: registration resolves the function object at the owner's import. **`owner` is a stable logical identity, not an import path**, so `importlib.reload` replaces the old callback instead of accumulating duplicates. `before_bind=True` marks the input-namespace reset as generated state the finalizer re-runs before rebuilding generated types; the helper-reference ledger is a full-clear-only callback. `tests/filters/test_finalizer.py::test_registry_clear_works_without_filters_imported` pins that `registry.clear()` runs cleanly with no filter callback registered.
- **Partial-finalize recovery.** If `finalize_django_types()` raises mid-phase-2.5 (e.g., an unresolved `RelatedFilter`), the ledger and the module globals keep whatever was materialized before the raise; a subsequent `finalize_django_types()` re-runs the binding pass, and the idempotent `(name, input_class)` check lets already-materialized classes pass through.
- **Public `clear_filter_input_namespace()` helper.** Exposed from `django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs` so tests can clear the filter namespace WITHOUT clearing the full `TypeRegistry`; `registry.clear()` reaches it through its registered row.

Rationale companion — this Decision's justification and its five rejected alternatives: [Decision 9][rationale-d9].

### Decision 10 — Joint `0.0.8` cut

Filtering shipped in `0.0.8` alongside the ordering card ([`DONE-028-0.0.8`][kanban]). This card changes no version string: the release is single-sourced in `django_strawberry_framework/__init__.py` `__version__` (hatchling derives the packaging metadata from it), and the maintainer cuts it.

Rationale companion — this Decision's justification and its two rejected alternatives: [Decision 10][rationale-d10].

### Decision 11 — `filter_input_type(FilterSet)` consumer helper

The package ships a public helper at [`django_strawberry_framework.filters.filter_input_type`][filters], in two spellings that evaluate to one annotation: the call `filter_input_type(MyFilter)` and the type-checkable generic `FilterInput[MyFilter]`:

```python
# django_strawberry_framework/filters/__init__.py (shape; the module is authoritative)
def _filter_input_annotation(filterset_class: object, *, helper_spelling: str) -> object:
    return build_lazy_input_annotation(
        filterset_class,
        expected_base=FilterSet,
        helper_spelling=helper_spelling,
        expected_label="a FilterSet",
        ledger=_helper_referenced_filtersets,
        module_path=INPUTS_MODULE_PATH,
    )


def filter_input_type(filterset_class: type[FilterSet]) -> object:
    return _filter_input_annotation(filterset_class, helper_spelling="filter_input_type()")


class FilterInput(Generic[_FilterSetT]):  # _FilterSetT is bound to FilterSet
    def __class_getitem__(cls, filterset_class: object) -> object:
        return _filter_input_annotation(filterset_class, helper_spelling="FilterInput[...]")
```

Both spellings return `strawberry.LazyType("<Name>FilterInputType", "django_strawberry_framework.filters.inputs")`, the lazy reference Strawberry itself turns an `Annotated["<Name>", strawberry.lazy(...)]` annotation into. The body is single-sited with the order twin at `django_strawberry_framework/utils/inputs.py::build_lazy_input_annotation`: it validates eagerly (a non-`FilterSet` raises `TypeError` worded with the spelling the consumer wrote, `filter_input_type() requires a FilterSet subclass; got ...` or `FilterInput[...] requires ...`), records the class in `_helper_referenced_filtersets` for the finalize-time orphan check, and returns the `LazyType` that Strawberry's `LazyType.resolve_type` resolves against the inputs module's `module.__dict__` at schema build (pinned by `test_filter_input_type_returns_lazy_type_named_for_the_input_class` and `test_filter_input_type_returns_lazy_type_resolving_in_the_inputs_module`). The return carries no `typing.ForwardRef`, so nothing ever evaluates the generated input name in the consumer's globals; that is what lets the helper resolve under `from __future__ import annotations` (below). A call is not a valid type expression, so a type checker rejects `filter_input_type(MyFilter)` in an annotation and accepts `FilterInput[MyFilter]`, rejecting `FilterInput[NotAFilterSet]` statically through the `FilterSet` bound. `FilterInput` is never instantiated: the resolver receives the generated Strawberry input object, an opaque handle whose one use is `MyFilter.apply_sync(filter, queryset, info)` / `apply_async`.

Consumers use it as a normal Python type annotation in a resolver signature:

```python
@strawberry.field
def all_galaxies(
    self,
    info: strawberry.Info,
    filter: FilterInput[GalaxyFilter] | None = None,
) -> list[GalaxyType]:
    ...
```

The annotation evaluates **when Strawberry evaluates it during schema declaration / collection**; the exact moment depends on the consumer's import style:

- **With normal annotations** it evaluates at module-load time; the `LazyType` is stored on the resolver's `__annotations__`.
- **With `from __future__ import annotations`** (PEP 563), Python stores the annotation as a string and Strawberry evaluates it, helper call included, in the resolver's `__globals__` at type-collection time. Two implications: (a) `GalaxyFilter` MUST be importable from the resolver module's globals at type-processing time (a `TYPE_CHECKING`-only import fails the schema build with `name 'GalaxyFilter' is not defined`), while `GalaxyFilterInputType` never needs to be, because the returned `LazyType` names its module itself; (b) Strawberry may evaluate the annotation more than once, so **the helper is safe to call repeatedly with the same `FilterSet`** — the ledger is a `set`, and each call returns an equal `LazyType`.

Either way, the `"GalaxyFilterInputType"` lazy reference stays unresolved until `strawberry.Schema(...)` time, by which point `finalize_django_types()` has materialized the class per [Decision 6](#decision-6--finalizer-phase-25-binding-seam--materialize-before-schema-ordering).

**Orphan `FilterSet` validation.** A `FilterSet` referenced via the helper but never wired via `Meta.filterset_class = MyFilter` on any `DjangoType` would not be materialized, and `LazyType.resolve_type` would fail for `MyFilterInputType` at `strawberry.Schema(...)` time. The package fails loud at finalize:

- The helper records every `FilterSet` it is called with into the module-level `_helper_referenced_filtersets: set[type[FilterSet]]` in [`django_strawberry_framework.filters`][filters].
- Finalizer phase 2.5 subpass 3 compares the helper-referenced set against the wired filtersets and raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] for the orphans with the message quoted under [Decision 6](#decision-6--finalizer-phase-25-binding-seam--materialize-before-schema-ordering) (it names both helper spellings, since the ledger does not record which one the consumer wrote).
- `registry.clear()` replays the `filters.helper_references` teardown callback, so the orphan check is consistent across test-fixture reloads.
- `test_orphan_filter_input_type_reference_raises_at_finalize` pins this.

Rationale companion — this Decision's justification and its four rejected alternatives: [Decision 11][rationale-d11].

### Decision 12 — Live HTTP coverage strategy

Package coverage is earned through fakeshop live `/graphql/` HTTP flows wherever a real query reaches the line, per [`AGENTS.md`][agents] #"any line reachable via a real GraphQL query against fakeshop".

Live HTTP tests land in [`examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_library_api.py`][fakeshop-test-library] and cover: scalar-field filter clauses, choice-enum filter clauses, forward-FK filter clauses, reverse-FK filter clauses, M2M filter clauses, logical-and / logical-or / logical-not combinations, optimizer cooperation (`assertNumQueries(N)` against a filtered queryset with nested selection), the explicit `queryset=` **scope boundary** (the live tests pin that nested and flat predicates through the branch answer only from the rows it admits; it scopes the parent filter, not the nested resolver's output), and flat relation leaves answering like their nested twins under the target types' `get_queryset`, `isNull: true` and `exclude=True` answering over the visible rows.

Package-internal tests land in `tests/filters/`: an `__init__.py` shell, a `fixtures/` sub-package for the cross-module `RelatedFilter` fixtures, four modules mirroring the four `filters/` source modules (`test_base.py`, `test_sets.py`, `test_factories.py`, `test_inputs.py`), and `test_finalizer.py` for the phase-2.5 binding pass, which lives in [`types/finalizer.py`][finalizer]. They cover what a live query cannot easily reach: the Layer-4 cycle rule under every expansion and registration order, the re-entry guard, `LazyRelatedClassMixin.resolve_lazy_class` failure paths, `FILTER_DEFAULTS` Relay-vs-scalar branch selection, the `LOOKUP_PREFIXES` map for `construct_search`, error surfaces, and cache behavior.

Rationale companion — this Decision's justification and its two rejected alternatives: [Decision 12][rationale-d12].
