## Edge cases and constraints

- A consumer `FilterSet.__init__` may materialize `self.form` and edit its field before permission checks. Once the annotation-owning hook supplies the comparison type, `FilterSet._refresh_materialized_form_comparisons` installs only the inner cleaner on that actual form field; its widget, validators, outer `clean`, and one normal `Form.clean` pass remain owned by the consumer. A consumer subclass overriding the package element or range field's `to_python` or `clean` owns a complete parser, even when its inner slot is empty. Before preparing any missing package parser, the actual form must take its native field-cleaning route and that field's widget data (or disabled initial) must be nonempty. A field whose complete cleaner is unrelated to the package's element or range field, including one a child constructor installs per request, needs no early hook for its own cleaning; an unresolved actual annotation output still needs its owner at the bind ask, after whole-form cleaning when the field's parser is complete. A disabled field's nonempty `BoundField.initial` is knowable from that materialized form after the gate even without a wire key: async apply awaits a missing owner outside the worker before its one clean only when an automatic inner parser is absent; a complete field instead joins the one postclean bind phase if its final cleaned value is active. This holds at the root, in a logical arm and in a nested child. An already cached owner still supplies the missing cleaner on an own initial field; the field name is retained independently of whether another named leaf filled the owner map. An unbound or unchanged `empty_permitted` form follows its native no-field-clean route before initial dependency discovery, reusing the form owner's change decision and widget-refusal recovery. If the consumer's outer `full_clean` adds an active value after that unchanged bound return, the same form joins the postclean bind phase; unchanged forms adding nothing keep zero asks and hooks. `stamp__year` with `range`, `stamp` with `year__range`, and an annotation literally named `stamp__at` with `year__range` all resolve through the hook annotation's output field; the remaining path is never restricted to one segment.
- A sync flat leaf crossing several declared or scoped undeclared hops invokes only the annotation-owning target hook between steps 5 and 6. A stored generated integer field keeps the same drop and range behavior as its computed integer column, despite Django exposing a `GeneratedField` wrapper and a separate native `Col.output_field`. A scoped `F` integer alias range still widens or empties on two bounds through the package's actual `gte` / `lte` predicate; an `ExtractYear` scoped range emits the native year-bound lookups and refuses an impossible year. A native CSV range remains its own numeric predicate. Earlier hops remain at step 8; the map keyed by declaration or scope and database alias prevents a second call during `.qs`. A denied leaf invokes none of those flat hooks.

- **Same-module `RelatedFilter` references** (e.g., `GalaxyFilter` and `CelestialBodyFilter` in one `filters.py` with `RelatedFilter("CelestialBodyFilter")` and `RelatedFilter(GalaxyFilter)`). Layer 2 handles this: the absolute-path lookup fails and the `bound_class.__module__` retry succeeds.
- **Cross-module `RelatedFilter` references via absolute path** (e.g., `RelatedFilter("apps.products.filters.CategoryFilter")`). The absolute-path lookup succeeds; the retry is never reached.
- **Circular `RelatedFilter` cycles** (`A → B → A`, a longer ring, a self-reference, or a set pointing into a cycle). A flat path ends where it reaches a filter set already on it (the same class object, compared by identity, never by a metaclass `__eq__`), which contributes its own declared and `Meta.fields` filters there unexpanded, or at a declaration marked `flat=False`, which copies nothing, so `A` publishes `b__<B's filters>` and `b__a__<A's own filters>` (the head `b__a__b` included) and `B` the mirror, whichever type is registered first; a set on the path, or one whose `RelatedFilter`s lead back to a set on it, contributes what its `get_filters()` (a consumer override included) makes of its declared, `Meta.fields` and `RelatedFilter` filters along that path, while a set that leads back to none contributes its published table; expansion terminates, raising only when a set's flat filters pass `MAX_FLAT_FILTERS` (a [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] at the first path past it, naming the root set, the declaration being expanded and the path; whether a graph raises does not depend on which set builds first, which set the message names can), and the cache writes only when no string `_filterset` remains. A subclass is a set of its own: a cycle through its parent returns to the parent, so the subclass's paths run one set further than the parent's. The apply-time recursion over related and logical branches is capped at `FilterSet._MAX_LOGIC_DEPTH` with a typed [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror].
- **`Meta.fields = "__all__"`** (the top-level shorthand). Expands to the scalar and forward-relation columns plus the primary key under the conditional `FILTER_DEFAULTS` per [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor), and **excludes** M2M; relations become nested branches only through an explicit `RelatedFilter(...)`. `FilterSet.get_fields` carries the two deltas against `django_filters.utils.get_all_model_fields` (PK added, M2M removed) per [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline); tests pin both. `Meta.exclude` with no `Meta.fields` (an empty one included) is the same sweep and publishes the same keys as `"__all__"` with that exclude; a `Meta.exclude` naming the primary key (an auto `id`, a non-auto key, an MTI child's `<parent>_ptr`) keeps it out under both spellings, and one naming nothing the set could publish (a typo, `"pk"`, which is no field name) is refused (Error shapes). A dict-form per-field `"__all__"` (`{"name": "__all__"}`) expands to that field's concrete lookups, restricted to `exact` / `in` / `isnull` for a Relay owner's own PK, and without the text-pattern lookups on a non-text column whose filter is the package default (Decision 3); listed by name there, a pattern lookup generates a `String` leaf (Decision 4).
- **`Meta.fields = {"galaxy__name": ["exact"]}`** (the double-underscore lookup-path shorthand). **Renders as a flat field named `galaxyName` with a nested lookup input** (`filter: { galaxyName: { exact: "..." } }`) — NOT as `filter: { galaxy: { name: { exact: "..." } } }`. `_build_input_fields` groups entries by their top-level GraphQL field name, so the consumer sees a flat surface that does not collide with an explicit `RelatedFilter(GalaxyFilter)` on the same filterset. Beside a declared `galaxy = RelatedFilter(...)` the path walks that branch, so `galaxyName` answers as `galaxy: { name }` does, inside the target type's `get_queryset` and the branch's explicit `queryset=` ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 3); with no declared root it is a plain traversal of the parent model. Consumers wanting the nested surface declare `RelatedFilter(GalaxyFilter)`; `HIDE_FLAT_FILTERS` (per [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline)) then hides the flat copies that branch's expansion publishes and keeps `galaxyName`, which is the set's own filter whether or not a `RelatedFilter` shares its first segment.
- **`Meta.fields = {"created": ["date", "year", "year__gt"]}`** (transform lookups). Every lookup of a column, through a transform chain or not, is a leaf of that column's head: `created: { date, year, yearGt }`. The head is the generated filter's name minus the tail django-filter's own `get_filter_name` appends for its `lookup_expr` (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_group_filters_by_head`), so a `RelatedFilter` expansion keeps the alias it is published under (`branchName`), and `year__exact` is the `year` leaf, since django-filter names both `created__year`. A chain leaf is named segment by segment (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::lookup_leaf_names`): `year__in` is `yearIn`, `year__isnull` `yearIsNull`, `year__icontains` `yearIContains`, with python attrs `year_in`, `year_is_null`, `year_i_contains`; never the camel case of the flattened path. Two lookups of one head named by one attr raise `ConfigurationError`. A generated filter a `get_filters()` override re-keys under a name not ending in that tail is its own head. The input keeps its fixed depth (head, operator bag, value): no chain nests. The cookbook's recursive `filterset_to_trees` / `try_add_sequence` / `sequence_to_tree` tree-form, which would nest such chains, is not ported.
- **`Meta.filterset_class = AdminGalaxyFilter` on a secondary `Meta.primary = False` `DjangoType`**. The secondary type is registered and reverse-discoverable; the filter binding runs on its definition exactly as on the primary's. The input-type namespace is name-keyed, so two `DjangoType`s on one model with different `filterset_class`es generate two distinct input types.
- **`Meta.filterset_class` naming a `FilterSet` without `Meta.model`**. Refused at finalize subpass 1 ([Error shapes](#error-shapes)), whatever the set's `Meta` declares (nothing, `model = None`, `fields`, `exclude`) and whether it carries leaves or only `RelatedFilter` branches: every filter of a served set is typed, validated and applied against its `Meta.model`, and the relation hops a leaf's visibility walks start there, so a model-less set would publish an input no request could apply. A model-less `FilterSet` is a base: a subclass declaring `Meta.model` is what a `DjangoType` wires, and `Meta.model` with `Meta.fields = []` publishes only the declared filters (`Meta.model` with neither `fields` nor `exclude` is django-filter's own `AssertionError` at class creation). Applied programmatically while unbound, a model-less set refuses a leaf naming the missing model and applies its `RelatedFilter` branches. The order family differs: an `OrderSet` resolves its paths against the queryset's model, so a model-less related-only `OrderSet` wired as `Meta.orderset_class` is legal ([spec-028 Decision 8][spec-028]).
- **`Meta.filterset_class` on a `DjangoType` that also declares `Meta.interfaces = (relay.Node,)`**. The filter binding runs at phase 2.5 after the Relay-Node injection; the owner's own PK, named by its single-segment key, becomes a GlobalID filter typed to the owner.
- **A `RelatedFilter` target set keyed on a relative of the relation's target.** The branch correlates the target set's rows by the relation's own link columns ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 4a), so the target set's `Meta.model` must be the model the `field_name` reaches, a proxy on its table (either way, sibling proxies included), or a multi-table-inheritance descendant keyed by its parent link, which restricts the branch to the rows that descendant extends. An MTI ancestor qualifies only across a forward or many-to-many relation whose foreign key the database enforces (the outer row references an existing target row) and whose columns it carries; across a reverse relation its own row would match itself, and a `db_constraint=False` key or a `ForeignObject` may name a target row that does not exist. Anything else raises at expansion (see Error shapes).
- **A `RelatedFilter(queryset=...)` or its child rows read `distinct()`** ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 4a). A child filter django-filter runs `distinct()` for (`MultipleChoiceFilter`, `AllValuesMultipleFilter`, `ModelMultipleChoiceFilter`, any `distinct=True` filter), a target `get_queryset` returning `distinct()` rows, and a sliced, `distinct()` or combined (`union()`) explicit `queryset=` each admit the child rows among the rows the explicit queryset holds, nested, flat, under `not` / `and` / `or` and through raw form data: `filters/sets.py::_admitted_rows` reads the queryset as an `IN` subquery of its keys and never combines the two querysets. A `queryset=` keyed on another model, a proxy or multi-table-inheritance relative included, is refused with a `ConfigurationError` naming the filter and both models when its branch is active; one pinned by `.using` to an alias the request does not read is refused as Django refuses a subquery across databases, before any parent row is read.
- **A path re-entering the bound set's table from a proxy or multi-table-inheritance owner** ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 3). Re-entry is by table: a hop whose model shares the bound type's `_meta.concrete_model` answers for the bound type (`utils/querysets.py::relation_target_type`). A set bound to a `ProxyBranch` type, keyed on `Branch` or on `ProxyBranch`, reads that type's visibility on `shelves__alt_branches__city`, never the `Branch` primary's, in a flat leaf, through a `RelatedFilter` targeting the set, and in `orderBy`; a `Branch` root reaching `BranchNote.branch` (declared to `ProxyBranch`) answers with the root, as does a sibling proxy. A multi-table-inheritance parent and child are different tables: a set keyed on `Venue` and bound to a `LendingDesk` type reads `Venue` rows a path reaches through the `Venue` type, since the child type cannot scope parent rows that are not desks, and answers with the root only for a path reaching `LendingDesk` itself.
- **Filter on a field that is NOT in the `DjangoType`'s `Meta.fields`** (e.g., the type exposes `name` but the filterset declares `description`). The filter clause still applies; the consumer can filter on columns they cannot select. This matches the cookbook and is intentional.
- **A declared choice filter whose own choices share no value with its choice column** (`DateRangeFilter(field_name="condition")` on a `TextChoices` column, `ChoiceFilter(field_name="tier", choices=[(10, "ten")])`, another column's `Choices` class, a `ChoiceFilter` declared with no `choices`). The input would publish the column's enum while the filter's own form refused every member, so the build refuses it naming the column ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor), `ChoiceFilter` row); a `DateRangeFilter` has no served spelling: on a column without `choices` it is refused too, alone, as a `Meta.filter_overrides` selection and inside a CSV `in` or `range`, whose own `filter` cannot look up a list. Sharing one value builds: each member off the filter's own choices is `FILTER_INVALID` `invalid_choice`.
- **A filter a `get_filters()` override publishes only over the expanded surface** (one added once `super().get_filters()` holds an expanded key, a donor set's filter or `RelatedFilter` head borrowed under an expanded key, a declared filter or flat copy edited in place, a filter a set's own table holds only around a cycle). The class-creation `base_filters` never holds it, while the published table every request copies does, so the deep-copy audit of [Decision 6](#decision-6--finalizer-phase-25-binding-seam--materialize-before-schema-ordering)'s subpass 2.5 copies the published table: one holding a lock is refused at `finalize_django_types()` by the key it is published under, with the `RelatedFilter` path for a set the audit reaches through one (a filter an expansion copies first names the one declaration it copied through), never a `TypeError` inside the input build or on the first filtered request. A copy the schema never names (a copied `RelatedFilter` head, a flat copy `HIDE_FLAT_FILTERS` hides) that an override edits in place is refused too, though a pipeline-built instance defers it: django-filter's own constructor (`BaseFilterSet.__init__`) deep-copies the published table whole on every construction outside the pipeline (a raw `FilterSet(data)`, `DjangoFilterBackend`, Layer 4's non-deferring instances; a set no type serves publishes it at its first construction) and any read of `filters` copies it, so such a table fails a supported entry point with a raw `TypeError` and the configuration is refused at build.
- **A leaf of a connection-served set resolving to a lookup or transform class that is not Django's own** (`Meta.fields = {"signed_big": ["doubled"]}` over a project `Transform` registered on `BigIntegerField`, a project `Lookup` named by a declared `lookup_expr`, a `Func`-based transform in a declared `field_name`, `class MyHour(ExtractHour): pass`, a Django lookup registered on a project transform, a project lookup on a Django transform's output, an instance registration, a reverse relation key whose forward field's class carries a project `exact`). Refused at build by the subpass-2.5 served-leaf audit ([Decision 6](#decision-6--finalizer-phase-25-binding-seam--materialize-before-schema-ordering)), from a root connection, a nested `<relation>Connection` alone, a `RelatedFilter` child only the nested branch reaches and a `Meta.fields` relation path alike; a Django class registered under a new name (`ExtractHour` as `hour_of_day`, `IStartsWith` on one field instance as `begins`) builds and answers, as do a `method=` filter, a `filter` override and a declared filter over a queryset annotation, which the seal judges per request, and a set no connection serves, which a project resolver applies through `FilterInput[...]` and `apply_sync` outside the visibility boundary. `"__all__"` leaves every such class out on every set ([Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline)), so only a named leaf reaches the audit.
- **A declared filter whose own form and lookup answer no value of its column** (`TimeFilter(field_name="occurred_at")` on a `DateTimeField`, a `UUIDFilter` on an integer column, a `BigIntegerField` included, a `DurationFilter` on an integer or decimal column, a `BooleanFilter` on a date, a `DateFilter` on an `IntegerChoices` column, a consumer `class TimesInFilter(BaseInFilter, TimeFilter)`, a `DateRangeFilter` inside a CSV `in` on a text column or a CSV `range` on any column, a CSV `MultipleChoiceFilter`, a CSV `ChoiceFilter` whose keys spell no date of a `DateField`, a bare `Filter(lookup_expr="in")` on an integer column, a `DateFromToRangeFilter` on an integer column, `filter_overrides={DateTimeField: {"filter_class": TimeFilter}}`). The leaf publishes the column, so every value of its input would be refused, raise a masked error, or constrain nothing (match every row); the build refuses it naming the declaration, `Model.column`, the form field class and the lookup ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor), the catch-all row, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_refuse_form_serving_no_value`). The samples are one value per stock scalar and every member of a choice column's enum: no Django, django-filter, Strawberry or graphql-core API yields a value of a scalar or a column, so the table holds inputs only, and whether each answers is asked of the filter's own form and lookup. On a column without `choices`, a form whose values are its own eagerly declared choices (a `ChoiceField` and its typed and multiple kin, no model-choice field, whose `validate` and `valid_value` resolve to Django's or django-filter's code, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_validates_its_keys`) is asked each key as a client sends it, the empty and null keys included (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_asked_samples`), and one declaring none answers no value. Its callable, lazy (`lazy()` promise, `SimpleLazyObject`), choice-iterator or `QuerySet` `choices` are never evaluated at build (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::defers_choices`, which the choice-column check shares), so a table they read need not exist yet; such a form, and one whose `validate` or `valid_value` is a consumer's (accepting values off its keys), is asked the scalar's samples with `validate` off instead. A model-choice form field is not asked: it cleans by querying its queryset, and the probe runs no query. A `coerce` that is a class, a consumer's `TextChoices` included, is called with each sample: the `decimal.InvalidOperation` a `coerce=Decimal` raises for a key no decimal spells, which Django's `TypedChoiceField._coerce` leaves uncaught, answers no, and any other error a consumer class raises propagates from the build as it would from a request. Any other form on a text scalar is not asked (it parses text by its own syntax, which no finite sample covers), nor is one on a consumer `SCALAR_MAP` scalar with no sample. Validators and the form field's `validate` are off for the probe: each bounds which values of the kind pass, never whether the form reads it (Django reads the kind in `to_python`), so `NumberFilter(min_value=18)` and a form field whose `validate` accepts even numbers only build, a `validate` reading the database runs no query at build, and an `EmailField` / `SlugField` form keeps its per-request `FILTER_INVALID`. A `UUIDFilter` on an integer or decimal column is refused although `UUIDField` reads a 32-digit numeral as hex, since that value is never compared as the number sent. Generated leaves (django-filter's choice for the column), a `method=` filter, a consumer `filter` override, a form field any consumer class or `coerce` cleans (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_cleans_in_shipped_code`: every class of its MRO whose own namespace defines `to_python`, `clean` or `_coerce` is Django's or django-filter's, and a typed choice field's `coerce` is a class or a function they define), a column whose `to_python` or `get_prep_value` resolves outside Django (for a relation, a `ForeignObject` with its `ForeignKey` and `OneToOneField` kin or a `ManyToManyField`, any field its last path step targets, `path_infos[-1].target_fields`, which Django's `RelatedLookupMixin.get_prep_lookup` prepares the value through, read again down to the concrete key columns; `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_django_converts`) and a lookup whose `get_prep_lookup` does (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_django_resolves`: consumer code bounding which values pass, which one sample cannot speak for) are not asked, and a lookup no field registers never reaches the probe (its set refuses it when it publishes, the next edge case); a transform, a null test, a column holding values as given and the package's column-cleaned filters type their leaf on other arms. A relation's copy is asked as its child set declares it, on that set's model (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::_LeafDeclaration`).
- **Deferred `choices` under Django's own choice form field** (`TypedChoiceFilter(choices=SimpleLazyObject(...))`, a `TypedMultipleChoiceFilter`, a CSV `in` or `range` over a `TypedChoiceFilter`, a `ChoiceFilter` whose `field_class` is `forms.TypedChoiceField`, any of them with a `QuerySet` or a `CallableChoiceIterator` as `choices`). Django's `ChoiceField` family normalizes `choices` when the field is built, where django-filter's own choice fields leave them to whoever iterates them, so the build reads every form field on a copy handing a `SimpleLazyObject` or `QuerySet` over in a subclass of Django's `CallableChoiceIterator` that also answers `len()` by iterating and stays true unread (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_UnreadChoices`, through `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::copied_filter`): the schema builds with no call and no query, a fresh database runs `check` and `migrate`, and each request's form evaluates the choices as it would without the package. An iterator declared as `choices` is no deferred value: each copy holds its own copy of it, its keys are asked as a list's are, and a CSV `MultipleChoiceFilter` over it is refused as one over a list is. Consumer code reading its own deferred `choices` at build (a `field_class` whose `__init__` reads or counts `self.choices`, a `field` override reading `len(self.extra["choices"])`) evaluates them where it is built, `len()` included, the schema build included, as often as it would on the declared value (a `SimpleLazyObject` and a `QuerySet` cache what they evaluate), and builds and serves as it would without the package.
- **A declared predicate Django resolves on no queryset** (`NumberFilter(field_name="signed_big__gt")`, `occurred_at__year__gt`, `CharFilter(field_name="label", lookup_expr="unaccent")` without `django.contrib.postgres`, `parent__label__icontains`, `genres__name__typo`, `ModelChoiceFilter(field_name="parent", lookup_expr="icontains")`, `ModelMultipleChoiceFilter(field_name="children", lookup_expr="unknown")` or `lookup_expr="icontains"`, a many-to-many either way, a `GenericRelation` or a reverse one-to-one with a lookup only its target's key registers, a package `RangeFilter` over a forward key). Every request naming it would answer Django's masked `FieldError`, so its set refuses it when it publishes: at schema build for a served set (root, nested `<relation>Connection`, reached through a `RelatedFilter`, an unserved set bound to a type for `FilterInput[...]`), at first use for an unbound one (its first construction, a raw `FilterSet(data=...)`, `DjangoFilterBackend` or `FilterView` included, or the package's first read of it), and at every construction after until fixed ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor), `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::refuse_unresolvable_lookup`). A `method=` filter and a `filter` override over the same path, a queryset annotation, a `FilteredRelation` alias, a name past a relation the related model lacks (`parent__bogus`, `partner__twice` under a hop whose type annotates `twice`), an `IntegerRangeFilter` over a forward key and a `RangeFilter` over a relation-valued `pk` build; the target's key spelled past a to-many relation (`children__id` with `icontains`) builds and answers Django's rows whether or not a hop is walked, and a raw set declaring `icontains` on the relation itself is refused at its first construction, walked or not. A lookup a project registers after a set publishes is not seen by that set: Django registers lookups at startup.
- **A `ListFilter` / `ArrayFilter` whose lookup compares one value** (`ListFilter(field_name="email")`, django-filter's default `exact`; `gte`, `iexact`, `icontains`, `startswith`, `isnull`, `year`, `ArrayFilter(field_name="ints", lookup_expr="len")`, `ints__0`, a `Meta.filter_overrides` `ListFilter` for `exact` / `gte`). It publishes `[<scalar>!]` and would hand the whole list to the lookup: no row on text (every row under `gte`), a masked `TypeError` on an integer or relation key, `FILTER_INVALID` on a decimal or temporal column. Its set refuses it when it publishes, at the times the unresolvable-predicate refusal does ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor), `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::refuse_listless_lookup`). `in`, `range`, `year__in`, `date__in`, a relation's `in`, a `JSONField`'s `exact` / `contains` / `gte` / `has_keys` / `has_any_keys`, an `HStoreField`'s `has_keys`, a `RangeField`'s `exact`, a `CompositePrimaryKey`'s `exact` / `gt` (its `isnull` refused), a `method=` filter, a project lookup, a column whose field class is project or third-party code (one preparing a list in `to_python`, `get_prep_value` or `get_db_prep_value`) and a stock django-filter parent build. A `range` list of one or three members is `FILTER_INVALID` (`invalid_values`) before any query, raw form data its form error; `[]` matches no row.
- **A filter whose form cleans a kind an encoder-less `JSONField` cannot encode** (`NumberFilter(field_name="payload__n")`, a `DateFilter` / `DateTimeFilter` / `TimeFilter` / `UUIDFilter` / `DurationFilter` on the column or a key path, a consumer CSV of one, django-filter's `RangeFilter` / `DateFromToRangeFilter` / `DateTimeFromToRangeFilter` / `TimeRangeFilter`, a package `ListFilter` / `RangeFilter` whose `Meta.filter_overrides` entry cleans a `Decimal`). A lookup that prepares its value through the column (a subclass of Django's `FieldGetDbPrepValueMixin`: `exact`, `gt`, `in`, `contains`, `range`, a key's `exact` / `lt` / `in`) hands it to `JSONField.get_db_prep_value`, which encodes it with the column's `encoder`, `json.dumps` with none, so a `Decimal`, `date`, `datetime`, `time`, `timedelta` or `UUID` would raise `TypeError` on every request that sends a value. The kind is the one each form field cleans, read off its class (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_cleaned_kind`: the `FORM_FIELD_SCALARS` row, a `DurationField`'s `timedelta`, which no scalar publishes, and a `TypedChoiceField` / `TypedMultipleChoiceField`'s `coerce` when it is a class), not the scalar it publishes: a `DurationFilter` publishes `String` and is refused, as is a `TypedMultipleChoiceFilter(coerce=Decimal)`; a kind the class does not name (a `coerce` that is no class, a consumer form field, a subclass defining its own `to_python`, `clean` or `_coerce` below the class that names the kind, `django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_keeps_cleaning_of`, which passes over django-filter's `BaseCSVField` since it cleans each member through the classes after it) is not refused: a `DateField` subclass whose `clean` returns the ISO text of the date it validates compares that text. A kind `json.dumps` encodes (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py #"_JSON_NATIVE_KINDS: tuple"`: `str`, `int`, `float`, `list`, `tuple`, `dict`, `None`) is never refused, so a `TypedMultipleChoiceFilter(coerce=tuple)` compares the JSON array its `tuple` encodes to. The schema build refuses it with [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the filter as declared, the refused kinds and three fixes: a form cleaning text or a number (an `IntegerField` / `FloatField` `field_class`), a bare `Filter` / `TypedFilter` comparing the JSON value as sent, or an `encoder=` on the column (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::_refuse_kind_json_cannot_encode`). A text lookup (a key's `icontains`, `has_key`) never reaches the encoder and builds (`NumberFilter(field_name="payload__n", lookup_expr="icontains")` is `Decimal`), and a `method=` filter or a consumer `filter` override owns what its value means and is never refused. A declared encoder (`encoder=DjangoJSONEncoder`) is trusted: the filter builds, typed by its form, and compares the encoder's text of the value (`"5"` for `Decimal("5")`), which matches a stored JSON string, not a stored number. A leaf published as `JSON` (a bare `Filter` / `TypedFilter` over the column or a key path, a `method=` filter whose form is a `forms.JSONField`) takes an object as one value: the normalizer binds it whole under the filter's one form-data key (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/inputs.py::publishes_json_value`), and a dict sent to any other non-range leaf stays refused as a malformed value (`django_strawberry_framework/filters/sets.py::FilterSet._reject_dict_form_value`).
- **Filter that raises a Django ORM error at queryset-translation time** (e.g., a JSON-field lookup the backend does not support). The Django `FieldError` / `NotImplementedError` propagates as a `GraphQLError`; the framework does not pre-validate the backend's supported lookups.
- **`construct_search` lookup-prefix handling**. `construct_search` maps a `^` / `=` / `@` / `$`-prefixed filter name to `istartswith` / `iexact` / `search` (PostgreSQL full-text only) / `iregex`, the map ported verbatim from `django_graphene_filters`; no consumer surface reads it until [`Meta.search_fields`][glossary-metasearch_fields] ships.
- **`Meta.fields` with no lookups** (e.g., `{"name": []}`). `django-filter` generates no filter for the field, so the generated input has no `name` entry; no error is raised.
- **Filter input position empty** (`filter: {}`). The pipeline applies no clause and returns the queryset unfiltered. The optimizer's [Queryset diffing][glossary-queryset-diffing] cooperation is unaffected.
- **Async resolver returning a filtered queryset**. Strawberry's async path awaits the resolver; the queryset's filter clauses apply normally.
- **A `str`-subclass name in `Meta.fields` / `filter_fields` / `Meta.exclude`** (a `StrEnum` member, `class Name(str)`). It names the field its text spells, exactly as Django, django-filter and DRF read a field name: `FilterSet.get_fields` reads every key, list member, lookup value and `"__all__"` spelling through the base `str` slot ([`utils/inputs.py::plain_set_meta_fields`][utils-inputs]) before django-filter hashes, compares or stores it, so each generated filter's `field_name` / `lookup_expr` is a plain `str`, an excluded name is excluded, and no consumer `__eq__` / `__hash__` runs. A declaration with no `str` subclass is read unchanged, and a `SimpleLazyObject` declaration (top level or a lookup list) as the object it stands for.
- **A `set` / `frozenset` `Meta.fields` / `filter_fields`, or a set of lookups in a dict declaration** (`fields = {"name", "description"}`, `{"name": {"exact", "icontains"}}`). django-filter iterates the declaration as given, so its hash order would move with `PYTHONHASHSEED`. `FilterSet.get_fields` puts upstream's field map in canonical order ([`utils/inputs.py::canonicalize_set_meta_fields`][utils-inputs], the order an `OrderSet` expands in): the names upstream kept (`Meta.exclude` applied) in `canonical_sort_key` order, and a set of lookups as a sorted lookup list. `base_filters`, the generated filter input's fields and each field's lookup sub-input, and a parent's flat `RelatedFilter` paths are then identical under every seed. The declaration is never rewritten as a `list`, so a set still may not name a declared filter (django-filter grants that allowance to a `list` / `tuple` only), and a `list` / `tuple` / dict of lists keeps its declared order.
- **A one-shot or bare-string `Meta.fields` / `filter_fields` / `Meta.exclude`** (`fields = (n for n in names)`, `iter(...)`, `map(...)`, `exclude = "name"`). django-filter walks `Meta.fields` on every expansion and tests `Meta.exclude` once per field, so a spent iterator would expand to nothing on a later pass and a string would exclude by substring. `FilterSet.get_fields` refuses both shapes at class creation with a [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the set and the attribute ([`sets_mixins.py::require_re_readable_field_declaration`][sets-mixins]); a `filter_fields` declaration is reported as `Meta.fields`. A lookup-bag dict's per-field lookup value is held to the same rule ([`sets_mixins.py::require_lookup_names`][sets-mixins]): a one-shot or bare-string value other than `"__all__"` is refused naming the field. A sized collection passes (`list`, `tuple`, `set`, `frozenset`, a dict, `.keys()` / `.values()`). A `SimpleLazyObject` declaration is judged as the object it stands for. A bare-string `Meta.fields` other than `"__all__"` is left to django-filter's `TypeError`. A lazy translation string (`gettext_lazy("name")`) is a string in every one of these slots although its real class is not `str` ([`utils/_narrowing.py::is_text_atom`][utils-narrowing]): `Meta.exclude` refuses it with the same message, naming its proxy class, and `Meta.fields` hands it to django-filter as the plain string it stands for.
- **`Meta.fields` referencing a model property (not a field)**. Rejected by `django-filter` at `FilterSet` class creation (`TypeError("'Meta.fields' must not contain non-model field names: ...")`). Filtering on a property requires a custom `Filter` declaration with an explicit `method=`.
- **Filter against a queryset that's already been `.values(...)`-projected**. Filters apply; the projection persists. The framework does not pre-validate.
- **Two `<TypeName>FilterInputType`s of the same string name**. Rejected by the per-module namespace's name-uniqueness check (per [Decision 9](#decision-9--input-class-namespace-vs-typeregistry-and-lifecycle)): the same `(name, input_class)` pair is idempotent; the same name claimed by a different input class raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming both and the filterset to rename.
- **`RangeFilter` sub-input class names across two filtersets that share a `field_name`** (e.g., two filtersets each declaring a `RangeFilter` on `lifetime_fines_cents`). The nested range class is named per owning filterset (`f"{filterset_cls.__name__}{Pascal(value_path)}RangeInputType"`, the value path of the Decision 4 typing rule) and built fresh each schema generation, since its name and axes depend only on the owning filterset, Django field path and inner scalar. These nested classes are embedded in the annotation, not materialized through [Decision 9](#decision-9--input-class-namespace-vs-typeregistry-and-lifecycle)'s ledger, so the owner qualifier is what keeps two filtersets' ranges apart; a pair it does not separate falls to the type-name claim (next row).
- **A `RangeFilter` whose `lookup_expr` ends on a transform** (`RangeFilter(field_name="occurred_at", lookup_expr="date")`, a bare `year` / `month` / `time`, a consumer-registered transform in a set no connection serves). Django's query reads the final name as a transform with an implicit `exact` when no lookup of that name is registered where the walk ends, so the range compares the transform's output: its bounds are published and cleaned as the output (`Date`, `Int`), its type is stemmed on the output's value path (`<Set>OccurredAtDateRangeInputType`, apart from a `range` over the column in the same set), and a lone bound applies through the transform (`occurred_at__date__lte`), so an end date keeps a row stamped later that day (`filters/base.py::_lookup_lhs_path`, the split `_membership_path` reads too). Two bounds compare through the same transforms as a `range` (`occurred_at__date__range`), so an end date keeps a row stamped later that day too. django-filter's `RangeFilter` drops a `lookup_expr` transform and compares the bare column; the package keeps it, since each bound is cleaned as the transform's output and the rows must agree with the declaration.
- **A `RangeFilter` whose declared lookup is no `range`** (`RangeFilter(field_name="price")`, django-filter's own spelling with the default `exact`; a declared `gte`, `lte`, `lt`, `in`, `iexact`, `icontains`, `isnull`). The declared lookup names the published key and nothing more: two bounds compare as `range` (over an integer column, or as the compared column's `gte` + `lte` over a relation itself in raw form data), a lone bound as `gte` / `lte`, as django-filter's `RangeFilter.filter` replaces the lookup of every declaration (`filters/base.py::RangeFilter.filter`). The integer bound holding reads the lookups the range applies, never the declared one (`filters/base.py::_integer_compared_column`), so a raw bound past the column under a declared `iexact` or `contains` is held as under `range`, never handed to an unbounded `int()`. No `RangeFilter` declaration is refused for its lookup: `filters/base.py::refuse_unresolvable_lookup` asks only whether Django resolves it, and `filters/base.py::refuse_listless_lookup` judges only a `ListFilter` / `ArrayFilter`.
- **Two generated input classes under one GraphQL type name.** `pascal_case` drops letter case and collapses interior underscore runs, and a set name joined to a member stem can spell another set's name (`BookFilter` + `set_code` = `BookFilterSet` + `code`), so two members, two sets, a root and a nested input, a `FilterSet` and an `OrderSet` root, or a model input and a form or serializer input can derive one name. Every generated class the package builds shares one module, and Strawberry merges two classes of one module and qualname as one type, so the second shape's fields would vanish from the schema. The arguments factory claims each root and every operator bag and range it embeds, and each write-family input claims its name at materialization ([`utils/inputs.py::claim_generated_type_name`][utils-inputs]): classes of identical fields share one identity and merge into one type; a second shape gets its own identity, so any schema serving both fails Strawberry's `DuplicatedTypeName`, which [`DjangoSchema`][glossary-djangoschema] restates as [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming both declarations, members and the cause; two shapes reached from one set's root raise that `ConfigurationError` at finalize, because every schema serving the set would serve both. Shapes compare field by field whatever the declaration order, and claims hold until `registry.clear()`, so a `finalize_django_types()` rerun after a failed pass meets the classes the first pass kept; which sets reached each shape is forgotten before each bind, so the same-set refusal never compares a rebuilt set with the classes its failed pass left behind. Two schemas of one process may each serve one of the pair. Pinned in [`tests/filters/test_factories.py`][test-filters] `::test_two_shapes_of_one_generated_name_in_one_set_raise_at_finalize` and [`tests/test_schema.py`][test-schema] `::test_a_generated_name_clash_in_one_strawberry_schema_is_refused` and `::test_django_schema_names_the_declarations_behind_a_generated_name_clash`; every split shape serves two mounts side by side in `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_filter_publication_api.py::test_a_generated_name_clash_split_across_two_schemas_builds_and_executes`, and two bags of one name whose lookups are declared in opposite orders share one type in `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_filter_publication_api.py::test_two_bags_of_one_name_and_one_lookup_set_in_any_order_share_one_type`.
- **Python keywords `and` / `or` / `not` in dataclass field positions**. Strawberry input types are dataclass-backed and cannot carry fields named `and`, `or`, or `not`. The package uses `and_`, `or_`, `not_` and pins the GraphQL names via `strawberry.field(name="and")` / `name="or"` / `name="not"`.
- **A head both declared and generated** (`name = CharFilter(lookup_expr="iexact")` beside `Meta.fields = {"name": ["icontains"]}`). One `name` operator bag carries both lookups; each binds its own form key (`iExact` → `name`, `iContains` → `name__icontains`), so supplying both ANDs two predicates. A declared head over another column (`name = CharFilter(field_name="city")`) keeps its lookup on the declared filter the same way.
- **Two filters on one `(head, lookup)` slot** (a declared `name = CharFilter(field_name="description", lookup_expr="icontains")` beside the generated `name__icontains`). One would be unreachable from the generated input, so `filter_lookup_table` raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming the filter set, both form keys, the lookup and the head, at input build or at the first hand-built mapping. Two `__` heads that flatten to one input attr (`a__b_c`, `a_b__c`) raise the same way; a head spelled exactly like another head's flattened attr keeps that attr.
- **Flat expansions of a child's declared filters.** `BookFilter`'s `shelf = RelatedFilter(ShelfFilter)` exposes `ShelfFilter.home_branch` (`ModelChoiceFilter(field_name="branch")`) flat as `shelfHomeBranch`, bound to the expanded copy `shelf__home_branch`, so the copy's own queryset validates the value (a pk outside it answers "Select a valid choice") exactly as the nested `shelf: { homeBranch: ... }` form does. A child filter reading its model while it filters walks as a filter of the hop's target set ([Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline) Layer 4): an `AllValuesMultipleFilter(field_name="code")` on a `Shelf` set reached through `shelf`, `shelves`, a many-to-many or two hops answers its nested twin's rows, its value validated against the choices the parent's copy lists (the values the parent's rows reach through the relation). An expanded declared child named like a lookup (`name__exact`) is its own head beside the generated `name`.
- **A declared model-choice filter with a callable `queryset`** (`ModelMultipleChoiceFilter(field_name="branch__name", to_field_name="name", queryset=lambda request: ...)`, django-filter's `to_field_name` idiom with a queryset read per request). The schema build calls no queryset, so the input is typed from the model the `field_name` path ends on over the declaring set's model, the model a model `queryset` over the same rows would name ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor) model-choice row): `ShelfFilter.home_branches_named_for_request` (`branch__name`) and `home_branch_for_request` (`branch`) read `Branch`, `BranchFilter.names_for_request` (`name`, crossing no relation) reads `Branch` itself, a two-hop path (`Book`'s `shelf__branch__name`, `shelf__branch`) reads the last relation's target, and a path into a multi-table-inheritance child (`KioskFault`'s `kiosk__name`, and `kiosk__pk`, whose key is the child's parent link) reads the relation's target, whose own and inherited columns are all `to_field_name` candidates; `BranchFilter.pks_for_request` (a bare `pk`) reads the set's own model. `to_field_name="pk"` is the primary key, as with a model `queryset`. A `field_name` that is no path of fields on the set's model, a set with no `Meta.model`, or a `to_field_name` naming no column of the model refuses the build with a `ConfigurationError` naming the set, the filter and the remedy.
- **Flat expansions of owner-bound child filters.** `LoanFilter`'s `patron = RelatedFilter(PatronFilter)` exposes `PatronFilter.email_must_have_at_sign` (`CharFilter(field_name="email", method=...)`, its validator appended in `PatronFilter.__init__`) flat as `patronEmailMustHaveAtSign`. A `method=` resolves on the owning filter set and a child `__init__` runs only for the child's instances, so the copy is a `filters/sets.py::ProjectedChildFilter` ([Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline) Layer 4): it validates with the field a `PatronFilter` instance builds (a value without `@` answers `missing_at_sign` under `patron__email_must_have_at_sign`), runs the method inside that instance over the `Patron` rows `PatronType.get_queryset` lets the request see, and keeps the loans whose `patron` is among them. The same holds for every flat leaf of a child that overrides `__init__`, for a child filter holding any other `filter` of its own (`BookFilter.title_starts`, a `TitleStartsFilter` whose `__init__` stores its own `filter`, flat as `booksTitleStarts` on genres and shelves and `bookTitleStarts` on loans), and for a projected copy expanded again at the next hop. An `exclude=True` child filter projects as the complement of its positive twin, negated at the outermost parent (a parent with no visible child row matches it); a child filter holding a `filter` of its own (a `method=`, or a callable stored on the instance) stays positive whatever its `exclude` flag.
- **Flat leaves walking declared branches** ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 3). Anonymous `allLibraryBooks(filter: { shelfTopic: { exact: "secret" } })` returns the same nothing `shelf: { topic: { exact: "secret" } }` returns, because `ShelfType.get_queryset` hides the `secret` shelf from both; `shelvesTopic` on `allLibraryBranches` answers only from the shelves `BranchFilter.shelves`'s explicit `queryset=` admits, for staff too. A walked leaf answers over the visible rows as Django answers the flat path, running its filter once per supplied value, so two lookups part from the existential nested twin: `bookShelfTopic: { isNull: true }` on loans also matches a loan whose book, or whose book's shelf, is hidden, absent or unset, while `book: { shelf: { topic: { isNull: true } } }` needs a visible shelf with no topic; and the declared `exclude=True` `GenreFilter.without_book_titled` (`withoutBookTitled`) matches what `not: { books: { title: ... } }` matches, as do its expansions `genresWithoutBookTitled` on books (a book with no visible genre matches) and `bookGenresWithoutBookTitled` on loans, never "has a visible related row the exclusion keeps". A leaf on the key of a declared hop's rows reads only the visible ones: an item whose every entry `EntryType.get_queryset` hides answers `entriesId: { isNull: true }` like an item with no entries, an entry whose property `EntryFilter.property`'s explicit `queryset=` excludes answers `propertyId: { isNull: true }`, and a key naming a hidden row matches nothing. A nested branch and a flat leaf walking one declared hop, at the top level, in any logical arm or a nesting level down (`shelf: { branch: { ... } }` beside `shelfBranchName`), share one run of the hop's target `get_queryset` per request (`examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_relation_filter_defaults_api.py::test_one_declared_hop_runs_its_target_scope_once_however_many_branches_and_leaves_walk_it`). A relation no `RelatedFilter` declares reads its target type the same way: anonymous `allLibraryBranches(filter: { altShelves: { exact: [<pk of a secret shelf>] } })` returns nothing, as for a pk no row carries, and `allCategories(filter: { itemsName: ... })` never matches through a private item; staff, who see those rows, match them. A consumer `ModelMultipleChoiceFilter` subclass overriding `filter` on `shelves` reads each chosen shelf's `pk` walked or not, and a branch whose only named shelf is hidden does not match, while an empty choice it reads as a default shelf (a key, model instances, a queryset or one instance, through a crossing `lookup_expr` too) answers as unwalked through the visible shelves only, an empty choice a many-to-many override reads as `genres__isnull=True` keeps a book whose only genres are hidden and no book holding a visible genre, a book chosen on that key raises Django's own refusal walked as unwalked, and an override naming `shelves` in its own Python instead of its `field_name` reads every shelf, its own scoping; a many-to-many whose through key onto its source Django gives no reverse name of its own (an auto-created self-referential one read forward, symmetrical or not), both of whose through keys share one hidden name, names no through key the view could condition, so its override keeps the chain re-bound to the target key over the visible targets (`filters/sets.py::_names_every_through`; `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_relation_key_overrides_api.py`, `tests/filters/test_sets.py::test_a_through_key_django_cannot_name_keeps_the_key_column_fallback`). On `Book.shelf__alt_branches`, whose first key is the non-null `Book.shelf`, an empty or absent choice read as no related row keeps a book whose shelf is hidden, as unwalked over the visible rows (the view joins the shelf round its target); a first `ForeignObject` (`ShelfLabel.shelf`) or a key onto a multi-table-inheritance child (`KioskFault.kiosk`) keeps Django's inner join (`examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_relation_key_overrides_api.py::test_a_to_many_view_through_a_key_no_round_trip_reaches_keeps_its_inner_join`).
- **A dotted key reaching a relation another set declares with `queryset=`** ([Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation) step 3). The explicit `queryset=` bounds only the paths whose walk crosses its declaration. `branch__shelves__topic` on a `Shelf` set declaring `branch = RelatedFilter(<a Branch set whose shelves declaration holds queryset=>)` walks that declaration and matches only through the shelves the queryset admits; the same key on a `Shelf` set declaring no `branch` crosses no declaration, matches through a shelf outside that queryset, and answers for its hops' target types' `get_queryset` alone (`Branch`'s on `branch`, `Shelf`'s on `shelves`), so a shelf the `Shelf` type hides still never matches. A row every route must not reach belongs in `get_queryset`.
- **A declared filter whose `lookup_expr` crosses a relation** (`ListFilter(field_name="shelf", lookup_expr="tier__in")`, `RelationPkMultipleFilter(field_name="shelf", lookup_expr="branch__in")`, `CharFilter(field_name="shelf", lookup_expr="branch__name")`, a to-many `ListFilter(field_name="shelves", lookup_expr="tier__in")`). Every package read meaning the path Django walks asks `django_strawberry_framework/filters/base.py::_filterset_walked_lookup` (`shelf__tier` and `in`): the element is typed and cleaned as the crossed column, each crossed hop's target type scopes the rows compared and its target set's gate fires for the column compared, and a to-many crossing runs `in` on the target column over the hop's visible rows, each as `field_name="shelf__tier", lookup_expr="in"` answers. The filter is never rewritten: `declared_filters`, `base_filters`, an instance's `filters`, its `label` and a `filter_queryset` override read the declared pair, the published lookup keeps the declared token (`tierIn`), and a refusal names the declared pair. A GlobalID filter whose `lookup_expr` crosses a relation (`GlobalIDFilter(field_name="shelf", lookup_expr="branch")`) validates the crossed relation's type and coerces its node id through that relation's key, and the Phase 2.5 encode-only audit reads that type too. A `method=` filter (or a `filter` stored on the instance) and a first name only the queryset holds (an annotation, a `FilteredRelation` alias, refused by Django on a fresh query) read their pair as declared; a class-level `filter` override walks as the shipped class does, by the two override rules of the flat-leaf edge case above for the path walked: into a target column it runs over the crossed hop's visible rows on a per-request copy carrying the walked `field_name` / `lookup_expr` (`field_name="tier", lookup_expr="in"`), as it does under `field_name="shelf__tier"`; ending on a relation key (`field_name="shelf", lookup_expr="branch"`, a to-many `field_name="shelves", lookup_expr="branch"`) it runs once as declared over the parent's rows with its own declared pair (`filters/sets.py::_KeyMatch`, compared on the key the walked path ends on), and for a to-many key's value naming no row on a copy whose `field_name` is the relation's alias and whose `lookup_expr` is the lookup the walked path applies where it ends (`exact` for `field_name="shelves", lookup_expr="books"`, `filters/sets.py::FilterSet._relation_view`); either way the filter object keeps its declared pair.
- **A field or lookup the lookup table does not hold** in a hand-built mapping (`{"nope": ...}`, `{"name": {"gt": ...}}` on a head without `gt`, or a bare value on a head with several lookups and no `exact`) raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] instead of being written as a form key `django-filter` ignores. Under a range head a misspelled lookup (`{"span": {"rnage": {...}}}`) reaches the range filter as the head's bare value, and the range value's `start` / `end`-only check raises it. A bare value is shorthand for the head's `exact` lookup, or for its only lookup. A wire input cannot reach these: the generated input types it.
- **Lookup names + Python keyword `in`.** Per [Decision 3](#decision-3--six-layer-lazy-resolution-pipeline)'s `LOOKUP_NAME_MAP` the package generates `i_contains` as the Python attr and the schema's converter names it (`iContains` by default, `i_contains` under `auto_camel_case=False`). Same shape for every initial-`i` case (`i_exact`, `i_starts_with`, `i_ends_with`, `i_regex`) and for `is_null`; `in` alone is pinned (Python attr `in_`, GraphQL name `in` whether or not `auto_camel_case` is on; a converter overriding `get_graphql_name` respells it, and the normalizer reads the respelled key). Two generated filter fields the converter names alike (`field_2` beside `field2` by default) are refused when the `DjangoSchema` is built (`schema.py::_refuse_field_name_collisions`). The normalizer writes an `in_` value under the `in` filter's own form key when building the form-data dict.
- **Multi-`DjangoType`-per-model filterset binding.** When a model is exposed by several `DjangoType`s (per [`Meta.primary`][glossary-metaprimary]) and one `FilterSet` is wired onto two of them, the strict multi-owner check of [Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor) runs at phase-2.5 subpass 1: owners over different tables (a multi-table-inheritance parent and child), a divergence on own-PK Relay identity, or a custom `get_queryset` on either owner raises [`ConfigurationError`][glossary-configurationerror] naming both owners. Consumers wanting separate surfaces declare two `FilterSet` subclasses (`PublicBookFilter` / `AdminBookFilter`).
- **Partial-finalize recovery for the input-class namespace.** If `finalize_django_types()` raises mid-phase-2.5, already-materialized classes stay in the module's `__dict__` and the ledger keeps them; a subsequent `finalize_django_types()` resumes, and the idempotent `(name, input_class)` check lets them pass through.
- **Fakeshop schema-reload pattern with already-materialized filter inputs.** `registry.clear()` resets both the model-to-`DjangoType` registry AND the filter-input lifecycle state in one call; the reload then re-runs `finalize_django_types()`, which re-materializes the input classes and overwrites the parked module globals in place. No stale ledger entry, field spec, factory cache entry, or binding attribute survives into the next run.

The generated identity filter compiles against `<relation>__pk`, because the stored column is not the primary key a raw-pk value or a primary-key GlobalID carries; a `RelatedFilter` expansion of the leaf, and a many-to-many leaf walking a visibility hop, keep the qualification ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor)). An absent one first runs over the hop target's unscoped rows (`FilterSet._absent_leaf_applies`), which lack it; a filter raising `FieldError` there compiled a lookup, so it constrains, and it is compiled again over the rows the type shows. A relation key whose rows another type answers for, or whose class overrides `filter` and so compares the id across the relation, answers `GLOBALID_UNVALIDATABLE` rather than Django's `FieldError`.

Over a target type's `get_queryset` it asks whether a visible related row exists, so a hidden row answers like a missing one, as on a `ForeignKey`.

A null value under a `null_label` is the relation's null test over the rows each relation shows and each declaration's `queryset=` admits, so it also matches a parent whose related rows are all hidden or outside that `queryset=`. A head crossing a `RelatedFilter` whose target model no `DjangoType` is registered for (`content_type` over `ContentType` on a set no type serves), a `method=` head and an absent head run as declared; a request naming no head runs no `get_queryset` for one. A head overriding `filter` follows the overridden relation-key rule (a to-many head, or a forward one two or more relations away, keeps only the parents reaching the visible row its value names), and its null value under a `null_label` runs as declared over its relation joined only to the rows each relation shows and each declaration's `queryset=` admits, forward or to-many. A sliced `queryset=` refuses every key as Django does, and a walk through its declaration (a head's null value, a copied head's key through a sliced hop, the walked key `shelves__code` through it) and a nested branch under it (`shelves: {code}` over GraphQL or `apply_sync`) admit the visible rows the slice holds, where Django would refuse to combine the slice. As for a walked key, a set built with no `request=` hands a named head's hooks `None` (a hook reading `info.context` strictly raises), and an async-only target `get_queryset` a named head reaches raises `SyncMisuseError`. The unbound form a `FilterView` or DRF's browsable API renders still lists the declaration's choices.
- **Helper validation**. `filter_input_type` / `FilterInput[...]` validate eagerly (`TypeError` for a non-`FilterSet`) even though the returned annotation is lazy, so misuse fails at the resolver-declaration site.

- **Projected annotation owner state.** A preinspected child can be built on base rows before the sync hook: its constructor restriction still narrows the real matching rows, with both excluded and allowed controls; a sync constructor validator on the outer list field remains effective after promotion. An async child built with cached annotation rows retains its constructor-installed inner validator after queryset cloning. A consumer filter field edited before form construction, or an already materialized form field, keeps its own public `RangeField.bound_field` validator. A sync field supplied by that consumer needs no early hook for its own cleaning; if its actual comparison output is an unresolved annotation, the owning target hook runs after the entire actual form clean to bind-ask its final active value. For an ordinary resolved column, the hook stays at application. Another active annotation leaf on the same child may need the hook before validation; the resulting child-context promotion fills only missing canonical inner cleaners and leaves the owned range/list field and its validators intact. A projected `method=` list/range leaf uses its sent values and keeps ordinary step-8 hook timing, so an unrelated invalid field wins first.
- **Postclean annotation ownership.** A complete native, whole-consumer or owned-inner field reaches its final value only after its actual full form clean, so its uncached bind-only owner is acquired once after all forms in the same root/logical validation input have completed native cleaning without ordinary errors. A missing automatic package list/range inner parser is the narrower after-gate preclean dependency. Form.clean and post-super additions, repairs and removals are read from final `cleaned_data`; raw invalid `.qs` keeps local or cached safe siblings and cannot reacquire a suppressed owner. Nested step-3 visibility and ordinary resolved flat-hop timing keep their existing phases.

### `tests/filters/` (package tests)

System-under-test is `django_strawberry_framework` itself. Four modules mirror the four `filters/` source modules, alongside the `__init__.py` shell, the `fixtures/` sub-package and `test_finalizer.py`:

- [`tests/filters/__init__.py`][test-filters] — empty shell so pytest collects under `tests.filters.<module>`.
- [`tests/filters/test_base.py`][test-filters] — covers the primitives, `RelatedFilter` and `LazyRelatedClassMixin`: `RangeField` / `validate_range` accept valid pairs; `RelatedFilter` accepts class / absolute path / unqualified name forms and rejects a non-`FilterSet` target; `LazyRelatedClassMixin.resolve_lazy_class` tries absolute then bound-module-prefixed and propagates the `ImportError` when neither resolves (`test_lazy_related_class_mixin_raises_when_unresolved_string_has_no_bound_class`); `GlobalIDFilter` accepts both raw `str` and `strawberry.relay.GlobalID` objects and rejects a wrong `type_name` with `GraphQLError("GlobalID type mismatch...")`; `GlobalIDMultipleChoiceFilter` validates every list element independently with the offending index named. The raw drivers read the bind-asking form: `test_django_filter_backend_answers_a_value_the_database_cannot_bind_with_400` and `test_a_strict_filter_view_answers_a_value_the_database_cannot_bind_with_no_rows` (a year past 9999 and a duration past 64 bits of microseconds, generated `in`, declared list and comparison), `test_a_consumer_form_override_is_asked_by_the_validator_itself` and `test_a_consumer_form_override_keeps_its_own_raw_form` (a consumer `form` override), `test_an_unbound_filter_form_cleans_nothing` and `test_an_empty_permitted_unchanged_filter_form_skips_the_asks` (an unbound form and an unchanged empty-permitted one clean as Django cleans them, unasked), `test_a_consumer_form_clean_runs_wherever_django_runs_it` (a consumer `Meta.form`'s `full_clean` and `clean` run as Django runs them, skipped only by a refused text) and `test_the_typed_path_reads_the_set_forms_widget_once_per_ask` (validating the set's own form asks nothing again).
- [`tests/filters/test_sets.py`][test-filters] — covers `FilterSetMetaclass` + `FilterSet`: the metaclass collects `RelatedFilter` declarations into `cls.related_filters` and binds each; the ### `tests/types/test_base.py`

`test_meta_filterset_class_is_allowed_not_deferred` asserts `"filterset_class" not in DEFERRED_META_KEYS` AND `"filterset_class" in ALLOWED_META_KEYS`, pinning [Decision 7](#decision-7--metafilterset_class-promotion-gate).

### `tests/types/test_definition_order.py`

`test_filterset_class_resolves_across_module_boundary` — two `DjangoType`s in two `tests.types.fixtures.*` modules, each with a `Meta.filterset_class` pointing at a sibling `FilterSet`; `finalize_django_types()` resolves both bindings without `ImportError`.

### `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_library_api.py`

System-under-test is the live `/graphql/` HTTP endpoint; coverage MUST be earned here where a real query reaches the line. The filter contracts:

- `test_library_branches_filter_by_name_icontains` — `{ allLibraryBranches(filter: { name: { iContains: "main" } }) { id name } }` returns only the matching branches.
- `test_library_books_filter_by_choice_enum` — `circulationStatus: { exact: ... }` returns only books in that status.
- `test_library_books_filter_by_non_relay_fk_scalar_id` — `{ allLibraryBooks(filter: { shelf: { id: { exact: 1 } } }) { id title } }`; `BookFilter.shelf` accepts a **raw `int`** key because `ShelfType` is non-Relay.
- `test_library_books_filter_by_relay_m2m_global_id` — `genres: { id: { exact: "<GenreType GlobalID string>" } }`; `BookFilter.genres` accepts a **Relay GlobalID string** because `GenreType` is Relay-Node-shaped. The test builds the GlobalID by encoding (`relay.GlobalID(type_name=..., node_id=...)`), not with `relay.GlobalID.from_id(...)`, which parses an already-encoded string.
- `test_library_branches_filter_by_reverse_fk_lookup` — `{ allLibraryBranches(filter: { shelves: { code: { iContains: "A" } } }) { id } }` returns only branches with matching shelves.
- `test_library_books_filter_combines_and_or_not` — `and` / `not` composition (the `and_` / `not_` Python attrs serialize as `and` / `not`).
- `test_library_books_filter_preserves_optimizer_cooperation` — a filtered query selecting `shelf { id code }` and `genres { id name }` under `assertNumQueries(N)`; the optimizer's `select_related("shelf")` / `prefetch_related("genres")` survive the filter clause.
- `test_library_branches_filter_respects_related_queryset_boundary_on_parent` — `BranchFilter.shelves` is `RelatedFilter(ShelfFilter, queryset=Shelf.objects.filter(topic="permanent collection"))`; with `branch_A` owning one in-constraint and one out-of-constraint shelf and `branch_B` owning only out-of-constraint shelves, `{ allLibraryBranches(filter: { shelves: { code: { iContains: "A" } } }) { id shelves { id code topic } } }` excludes `branch_B`, includes `branch_A`, and may still surface both of `branch_A`'s shelves through `BranchType.shelves` (the constraint scopes the parent filter, not the nested resolver's output — [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation)).
- `test_book_genres_uses_absolute_import_path_related_filter` — `BookFilter.genres = RelatedFilter("apps.library.filters_genre.GenreFilter")` resolves through `import_string`; `genres: { name: { exact: ... } }` returns the matching books.
- `test_nested_related_filter_honors_target_get_queryset` — `ShelfType.get_queryset` hides `topic == "secret"` shelves for non-staff users; with `branch_visible` owning `Shelf(code="A", topic="general")` and `branch_hidden` owning `Shelf(code="A", topic="secret")`, an anonymous `shelves: { code: { exact: "A" } }` excludes `branch_hidden` (step 3 of [Decision 8](#decision-8--relation-permission-cascade--get_queryset-cooperation)), and a staff request includes it.
- `test_apply_raises_graphqlerror_on_invalid_filter_input` — the explicit `form.is_valid()` step. An invalid enum literal cannot reach it (GraphQL input coercion rejects it before the resolver runs), so `PatronFilter` declares `email_must_have_at_sign = CharFilter(field_name="email", method="filter_email_must_have_at_sign")`, whose validator raises `forms.ValidationError("missing @", code="missing_at_sign")`. `{ allLibraryPatrons(filter: { emailMustHaveAtSign: { exact: "bogus" } }) { id } }` returns an error with `message == "Invalid filter input"`, `extensions.code == "FILTER_INVALID"`, and `extensions.errors["email_must_have_at_sign"][0]` carrying the `"missing @"` message and the `"missing_at_sign"` code.
- The `MultipleChoiceFilter` family row ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor)): a holder schema over `Shelf` declares `MultipleChoiceFilter` (`conjoined=True` too), `TypedMultipleChoiceFilter` and `AllValuesMultipleFilter` over the text column `code`, the text choice column `condition` and the integer choice column `tier` (an `AllValuesMultipleFilter(method=...)` on each of the three too), and a `Meta.filter_overrides` `MultipleChoiceFilter` `exact` on `topic`. `test_multiple_choice_filter_publishes_a_list_of_its_column_values_over_http` pins each `exact` as `[String!]` or `[<column enum>!]`; `test_multiple_choice_filter_keeps_the_rows_matching_its_list_over_http` filters by a list (`BLANK` and `MEMBER_1` as their values, `conjoined` matching every value, under `not`, `[]` and `null` constraining nothing); `test_multiple_choice_filter_refuses_a_value_off_its_choices_over_http` answers a value off the filter's choices with `FILTER_INVALID` `invalid_choice`; `test_an_all_values_multiple_method_filter_hands_its_method_the_cleaned_list_over_http` holds each `method=` filter's method to one call with its column and the list its form cleaned (`MEMBER_3` the text `"3"`), and `test_an_all_values_multiple_method_filter_refuses_an_unheld_value_over_http` answers a value no row holds with `invalid_choice` before the method runs; `test_multiple_choice_filter_refuses_a_name_off_the_column_enum_as_a_graphql_error` leaves a name the enum lacks to GraphQL coercion. Package rows: `tests/filters/test_inputs.py::test_convert_filter_to_input_annotation_types_a_columnless_multiple_choice_method_filter` (`list[str]`, never from `coerce`), `tests/filters/test_inputs.py::test_normalize_input_value_rejects_scalar_on_multiple_choice_filter` (a non-list refused, an `in` wrapper keeping the CSV arm), the `method-without-field` rows of `tests/filters/test_inputs.py::test_an_expanded_child_leaf_is_refused_under_its_declaration` and its siblings (an `AllValuesMultipleFilter(method=...)` backed by no model field refused) and the `multiple-choice` row of `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_filter_apply_arguments_api.py::test_an_expanded_malformed_argument_names_the_consumers_original_filter_class`.
- A column-backed `method=` filter whose form field is built only per request ([Decision 4](#decision-4--upstream-primitives-parity-floor), the `MultipleChoiceFilter` family and catch-all rows): the `column-all-values-multiple` (`[String!]`) and `column-form-field-built-per-request` (a `Filter` subclass whose `field` reads the bound `model`, `String`) cases of `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_library_api.py::test_library_method_filter_spellings_the_refusal_advises_filter_over_http` publish the column's type and the method keeps the books it matches; the `form-built-per-request` rows of `tests/filters/test_inputs.py::test_a_method_filter_named_isnull_is_typed_by_a_form_cleaning_its_own_kind` keep `Boolean` for such a filter named `isnull`.

- `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_filter_widget_binding_api.py::test_a_nested_split_range_bound_the_zone_cannot_present_is_filter_invalid` — parametrized over a dict and a prefixed `QueryDict` form mapping, the start and end bound, and a bound whose wall clock leaves years 1..9999 under `UTC` (before year 1, past year 9999) and under `America/New_York` (`0001-01-01T00:30:00+00:00`): `FILTER_INVALID` keyed to `span` with `"Enter a value the current time zone can represent."` / `"invalid"`; the same `0001-01-01T00:30:00+00:00` under `UTC` filters (every row as a start, none as an end).
- `test_apply_passes_graphql_enum_coercion_before_form_validation` — `circulationStatus: { exact: "NOT_A_REAL_ENUM_VALUE" }` produces the GraphQL coercion-layer error (not `FILTER_INVALID`): the resolver never runs.
- `test_library_flat_expanded_method_filter_runs_in_its_own_filterset_over_http` — flat `patronEmailMustHaveAtSign: { exact: "p1@x" }` on `allLibraryLoans` returns the loans of that patron, an unknown email returns none, and a value without `@` answers `FILTER_INVALID` with `missing_at_sign` under `patron__email_must_have_at_sign`: the expanded `method=` child runs inside a `PatronFilter` instance. `test_library_flat_leaves_over_a_to_many_relation_stay_independent_over_http` — flat `loansNote` + `loansId` over `BookFilter.loans` are satisfied by two different loans of one book while the nested `loans: { note, id }` branch needs one loan matching both. The package twins in `tests/filters/test_sets.py`: `test_expand_related_filter_projects_owner_bound_children_only` (one node per child shape), `test_customizes_instances_is_the_init_seam`, `tests/filters/test_sets.py::test_projected_field_identity_belongs_to_its_child_but_templates_are_fresh`, `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_projected_owned_filters_api.py::test_projected_method_distinguishes_constraining_none_from_identity`, `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_projected_owned_filters_api.py::test_projected_method_distinguishes_constraining_none_from_identity`, `tests/filters/test_sets.py::test_a_twice_projected_leaf_keeps_its_child_projection_metadata`, `test_projected_leaf_gets_no_candidate_row`, `test_projected_leaf_child_runs_on_the_parents_database_with_its_request`, `test_projected_leaf_copies_keep_their_projected_class_and_projection` (`pickle` and `deepcopy` keep the runtime-built projected class), and `test_flat_expanded_declared_child_fires_the_gates_its_nested_twin_fires`.
- `test_a_filter_a_get_filters_override_adds_filters_on_every_spelling_over_http` — `PatronFilter.get_filters()` adds `email_contains`; `PatronFilter` overrides `__init__` and sits in the `LoanFilter` <-> `PatronFilter` cycle, yet `allLibraryPatrons(filter: { emailContains })`, `allLibraryLoans(filter: { patron: { emailContains } })` and the flat `patronEmailContains` (run inside a `PatronFilter` instance) each return exactly the matching rows. `test_a_method_filter_a_target_get_filters_override_adds_filters_through_a_hop` (and `_async`) — the `method=` filter `IssueFilter.get_filters()` adds, `title_starts`, filters periodicals through `PeriodicalFilter.issues`, flat (`issuesTitleStarts`) and nested.
- `test_library_series_hop_handle_is_gated_as_its_nested_spelling` — `PrintingFilter.in_series` hops over `series` under an attribute of its own, so the flat copy `inSeriesHandle` of `SeriesFilter.handle` spells no relation; a denying `SeriesFilter.check_handle_permission` or `PrintingFilter.check_in_series_permission` refuses it as it refuses `inSeries: { handle: ... }`, before any row is read. - `test_root_get_queryset_runs_before_filter_apply` — `BranchType.get_queryset` hides `city == "restricted"` branches for anonymous users (`Branch` has `name`, `city`, `tags` per [`examples/fakeshop/apps/library/models.py::Branch`][fakeshop-library-models]); with `branch_public(city="Boston")` and `branch_private(city="restricted")` both matching `name: { iContains: "andromeda" }`, an anonymous request sees only `branch_public` and a staff request sees both.
- `test_relay_global_id_filter_rejects_wrong_type_name` — a GlobalID typed `LoanType` over a genre's PK is rejected with `GraphQLError("GlobalID type mismatch: filter expects GenreType but received LoanType")`; the correctly-typed `GenreType` GlobalID returns the matching books; a mixed `in: ["<wrong>", "<right>"]` list is rejected whole with the offending index named.
- `test_library_globalid_filter_into_a_custom_node_id_compares_the_house_code` — parametrized over every GlobalID filter shape reaching `PublisherType` (whose id carries `house_code`): the own `id` key (`exact`, `in`, `not:`), an own-id leaf through a hop (flat and nested, from publishers and from printings), the forward keys `publisher_2` (direct, through a hop, nested, `not:`), the `to_field` key `licensor` (stored by the publisher's name), the to-many key `coPublishers` and the two-hop `editionPrintingsCoPublishers` (each `exact`, `in`, `not:`), and a publisher's nested `printings2Connection(filter:)`; each shape returns the emitted id's row, returns Tor (whose house code is Gollancz's primary-key digits) rather than Gollancz, and matches nothing for an id carrying a primary key or an unknown code, at the statement count `test_library_house_roots_issue_the_same_statements_under_a_pk_slot_filter` pins under a primary-key filter. `test_library_globalid_filter_into_a_custom_node_id_refuses_an_uncoercible_code` pins the NUL-bearing code's `GLOBALID_INVALID` text on every shape; `test_library_series_filter_compares_its_annotated_handle_on_its_own_rows`, `test_library_series_hop_compares_the_handle_on_the_rows_its_type_reads` (flat and nested `inSeries` over the id and the declared `handle`, and the `series` key, each spelling answering alike), `test_library_series_key_compares_the_handle_as_its_hop_twin_does` (`not:` and the `printings2Connection` window), `test_library_series_key_refuses_another_types_id_as_its_hop_twin_does` and `test_library_series_key_runs_the_statement_its_hop_twin_runs` pin the annotated `SeriesType.handle` id on its own rows, through the hop and through the `series` key alike; `test_library_pk_slot_globalid_relation_key_compares_the_stored_key_column` pins a primary-key target's compiled key-column predicate.
- The relation element rule (Decision 4 `ListFilter` / `ArrayFilter` and `RangeFilter` rows): `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_raw_filter_forms_api.py::test_a_declared_list_over_an_integer_keyed_relation_cleans_each_raw_element_as_its_key` (a foreign key and its reverse, a many-to-many, a one-to-one and its reverse, `ListFilter` and `ArrayFilter`, under the package's and django-filter's `FilterSet` over a `QueryDict`: a key and an integral decimal match, a junk element is the form's "Enter a number.", an element past the integer range or fractional drops and a list left empty matches nothing without a query, a key no row holds is compared), `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_raw_filter_forms_api.py::test_a_declared_list_over_a_text_keyed_relation_strips_each_raw_element_as_its_key` (a text primary key and a `to_field` strip a padded key as their columns do) and `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_raw_filter_forms_api.py::test_a_declared_filter_over_a_relation_cleans_only_where_its_lookup_compares_the_relation` (a lone `gte` bound cleaned as the key, a `code__in` crossing compared as the crossed column's text), `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_raw_filter_forms_api.py::test_a_method_filter_over_a_relation_receives_each_raw_element_as_sent` (a `method=` filter on the target's `code`) and `examples/fakeshop/test_query/test_raw_filter_forms_api.py::test_a_public_integer_list_primitive_drops_members_the_column_cannot_convert` (a plain integer column's `ArrayFilter` `in` drop);
